# Glix — Complete Project Context
# Last Updated: 2026-08-03 (Phase 76 — root-caused and fixed a Phase 75 regression that made the app freeze and get killed by the OS: `(tabs)/profile.tsx`'s new focus-refetch guard fed itself, because `analyticsDirtyAt` was a `useCallback` dependency *and* `fetchProfile()` synchronously bumps `analyticsDirtyAt` as its first statement — focus → fetch → bump → new callback identity → effect re-runs → "new activity" is true again → fetch → bump → forever, 4 HTTP requests per turn, bounded only by render speed. That storm also starved every other screen's request in `lib/api.ts`'s per-request `SecureStore` interceptor, which is why Discover sat on a spinner that never even timed out. Fixed in `profile.tsx` and hardened identically in `analytics.tsx` (same shape, one `fetchProfile()` call away from the same fate). Supersedes this phase's earlier "no code regression found" conclusion — see below. Also kept from that first pass: a `useColdStartHint()` "waking up the server" message on Discover/Community's loading states, and the app's first-ever `ErrorBoundary` wrapping the root `Stack`. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `jest` 12/12. Full detail below. See Phase 75 below.)
# Last Updated (prior): 2026-08-03 (Phase 75 — a 9-phase user-approved plan executed in one continuous pass, plus a mid-session auto-logout bug report: real per-episode air times sourced from TVmaze `airstamp` (was show-level-slot-only, missing for most streaming originals) with the countdown removed from the widget in favor of a kept release time; a first-class Shows/Anime split (Shows Hub heading toggle + new "My Anime" profile screen); reviews gated on having watched; a default-layout preference system (global default + per-screen override); Community's Activity feed switched from watch history to real reviews (+ a self-follow-button 400 fix + Discussions 8-requests-to-1 collapse); a rewatch system (show-level rounds with per-episode ticks, movie "watch again" counter) on a parallel presence-based table set; a Celery/Redis infra cost pass plus 5 cartesian-join query fixes and 2 N+1 fixes; and a closing sweep. Two independent auto-logout root causes fixed in the token-refresh/auth-boot path, outside the plan's scope. Backend suite 148→192 (+44), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `jest` 12/12. Full detail below.)

## Phase 76 — Post-Update Freeze/Crash: Self-Triggering Focus-Refetch Loop

**(0) THE ROOT CAUSE — a self-triggering refetch loop in `(tabs)/profile.tsx`.** Found on the second pass, after the user escalated with "profile tab and most of the things are not working... app is fully stuck and then fails, exits out." This supersedes item (1) below, which concluded no code regression existed — that conclusion was wrong, and is left in place rather than quietly deleted because the reasoning that produced it is worth keeping honest: the first pass audited the files the *bug report* pointed at (`discover.tsx`, `discoverStore.ts`, `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx`, `lib/api.ts`) and correctly cleared all of them, but the actual regression was in a file the report never named.

Phase 75.8 added a staleness guard to this screen's `useFocusEffect` so tab-switching wouldn't re-run 4 requests every time. As shipped, the guard fed itself:

```
useFocusEffect(useCallback(() => {
  ...
  const hasNewActivity = analyticsDirtyAt !== lastSeenProfileDirtyAtRef.current;
  if (!isStale && !hasNewActivity) return;
  lastSeenProfileDirtyAtRef.current = analyticsDirtyAt;   // pre-bump value
  fetchProfile(); fetchWatchlist(); fetchMovieWatchlist(); fetchLists();
}, [analyticsDirtyAt, ...]));                             // ← reactive dep
```

`fetchProfile()` (`watchStore.ts:764`) sets `analyticsDirtyAt: Date.now()` **synchronously, as its very first statement** — that is deliberate, it is the funnel point every watch mutation passes through. But `analyticsDirtyAt` was also a dependency of this `useCallback`. So: focus → fetch → bump → `analyticsDirtyAt` changes → new callback identity → `useFocusEffect` re-runs the effect → `hasNewActivity` is true again (because `lastSeen` was recorded from the *pre-bump* value) → fetch → bump → … with no terminating condition. `lastProfileFetchedAtRef` could not stop it: `hasNewActivity` is OR'd past the staleness check by design. Each turn fires 4 requests (`/profile/`, `/watchlist/`, `/movies/watchlist/`, `/lists/`) and the loop iterates as fast as this render-heavy screen can re-render — every turn faster than the ~1ms granularity of `Date.now()`, so the values never collide and the loop never self-terminates.

**Why this also broke Discover, which shares no code with it.** Two amplifiers. (a) The backend's DRF throttle is 120 requests/min — tripped within a second, so everything else 429s. (b) More decisively: `lib/api.ts`'s request interceptor does `await SecureStore.getItemAsync(ACCESS_TOKEN_KEY)` on **every** request. With thousands of queued requests, that native bridge queue backs up, and a new request sits in the interceptor *before* it is ever dispatched — which is before axios's 15s `timeout` clock starts. That is the exact mechanism for a spinner that hangs forever and never produces a timeout or an error card, matching the user's screenshot precisely (search bar + segment control + a spinner, nothing else, indefinitely). `fetchFeed`'s own error handling was never reached because its request never left. Meanwhile the JS thread is saturated re-rendering the Profile screen, so the app stops responding and the OS kills it — the reported "app fully stuck and then exits."

**Fix.** Two independent changes, either of which alone breaks the cycle, applied to `(tabs)/profile.tsx`:
1. `analyticsDirtyAt` is no longer a reactive `useCallback` dependency — it is read via `useWatchStore.getState()` inside the effect. It never needed to be reactive: this is a *focus* effect, and the only question it asks is "did anything change while I was away," which is answered by one read at focus time. `lastSeenProfileDirtyAtRef` is now recorded **after** `fetchProfile()`'s own synchronous bump, so this screen's own refresh can never be misread as someone else's watch mutation.
2. A hard `PROFILE_MIN_REFETCH_MS = 2_000` floor checked first, capping re-entry from any future source regardless of how the dirty flag is reasoned about.

`app/analytics.tsx` carries the byte-identical guard shape. It was **not** looping in practice — none of its six fetchers bump `analyticsDirtyAt` (verified: `fetchProfile()` is called only from `profile.tsx`, `watchStore.ts` itself, `show/[id].tsx`, `show/[id]/season/[season].tsx`, `episode/[id].tsx`, and `loading.tsx`) — but it is one added `fetchProfile()` call away from the same fatal behavior, so it was hardened identically. Grepped every `useFocusEffect` in the repo: these two files are the only ones, so the class is fully swept. Files: `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/analytics.tsx`.

**Also fixed:** `profile/anime` (new in Phase 75.2) was never registered as a `<Stack.Screen>` in `app/_layout.tsx`, so it fell back to the Stack's default `animation: 'fade'` while every sibling profile destination slides. Cosmetic only — expo-router registers routes from the filesystem regardless — but it made My Anime feel like a different kind of navigation than My Shows/My Movies. Now registered alongside its siblings.

**(1) First pass — investigation that cleared the files the report named (conclusion superseded by (0)).** User reported Discover (screen recording confirmed blank for the full ~13s clip — no spinner motion, no error card, while Shows/Movies Hub rendered real cached data correctly in the same recording), plus Profile and "most things," stopped working right after this session's Phase 75 EAS update + Render backend redeploy. `git diff` against the Phase 75 commit confirmed `discover.tsx`/`discoverStore.ts`/`DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` were untouched this phase — last changed in Phase 74's own selector-conversion pass, re-read in full and re-verified field-for-field correct (every scoped selector reads a single primitive, no reference-instability bugs, nothing omitted from the original bare destructure). `tsc --noEmit` clean. `eas channel:list`/`eas update:view` confirmed the Phase 75 update published correctly to the `preview` channel, `runtimeVersion` 1.0.0 matching (no native changes this phase, so no channel/runtime mismatch is possible). Live-tested the actual deployed production backend end to end: first a fresh throwaway account against every Phase 75 endpoint (discover feed/genres/for-you, watchlist, movies watchlist, lists, profile, analytics dashboard/statistics/monthly-summary, comments feed, activity feed, rewatch show detail, public profile, users search/followers) — all 200, correct empty shapes; then a fully populated account to rule out a crash only real data could trigger — added a show, bulk-marked all 62 episodes watched (`POST /watch-state/bulk-toggle/`), started a rewatch round and toggled an episode within it, added a movie and marked it watched, added a movie rewatch, posted a show review and a movie review, then re-checked every endpoint above — all returned correct data (`active_rewatch`, `rewatch_count`, `is_self` all populate correctly) with zero 500s anywhere. Read `profile.tsx` and the new `profile/anime.tsx` in full (not just their diffs) — both logically sound; `isAnimeByGenresAndLanguage()`'s `e.show.genres` access confirmed safe (a required, always-populated field on live API responses, not an optional one that could be `undefined`); `profile/anime.tsx`'s reuse of `ShowGridCard`/`ShowListRow`/`MovieGridCard`/`MovieListRow` (named exports from the `profile/shows.tsx`/`profile/movies.tsx` route files) are self-contained components with no dependency on their originating screen.
2. **First pass's working theory — a real gap, but not the root cause (see (0)).** Render's free-tier dyno restarts — and goes cold, 20-50s to wake per Render's own documented behavior — on every backend redeploy, which this session triggered to ship Phase 75's backend changes. `app/_layout.tsx`'s boot path for an *already-authenticated returning user* (a token already sitting in SecureStore) proceeds straight into the tabs the instant the local token read resolves; it never runs the cold-start-aware `waitForBackend()` wait-and-message loop that `app/loading.tsx` already has, and has always had, for the login/register path (`Updates`/OAuth boot routes through `/loading` explicitly; a cold relaunch with an existing token does not). Shows/Movies Hub paint real content instantly regardless of backend state, since they read AsyncStorage-persisted `watchlist`/`movieWatchlist` data with zero network dependency for first paint; Discover (`discoverStore.ts`) and Community's Discussions/Activity tabs are non-persisted, in-memory Zustand state with nothing to show until a live round trip resolves — and `lib/api.ts`'s transient-failure retry logic (2 retries × up to 15s each, plus backoff) can legitimately keep a screen's spinner up for close to 47 seconds before the friendly "Can't reach Glix" error card ever appears, comfortably longer than the 13-second clip, explaining why it read as permanently frozen with zero signal rather than slow-but-recovering.
3. **Fix — two hardening changes.** New `useColdStartHint(active: boolean): boolean` hook in `lib/warmup.ts`, reusing the existing `waitForBackend()` the login path already relies on — a one-shot, non-blocking probe that flips true a few seconds into a still-loading fetch if the backend turns out to be waking, without ever gating or retrying the caller's own request (so a warm/fast backend, the common case, never shows anything different). Wired into `discover.tsx`'s feed-loading branch (gated on `isLoadingFeed && !feedData[activeSegment]`) and both of `community.tsx`'s non-persisted tabs (`DiscussionsTab`'s `isLoading`, `ActivityTab`'s `isLoadingFeed && activityFeed.length === 0`) — a slow-to-wake backend now shows "Waking up the server — this can take up to a minute." under the spinner instead of an unexplained bare loading state. Second, grepped the whole codebase and confirmed **zero** error boundaries existed anywhere — `componentDidCatch`/`getDerivedStateFromError`/`ErrorBoundary` were entirely absent outside unrelated skill-data CSV files — meaning any uncaught render-time exception, from any cause, ever, unmounted the tree silently with no signal at all, exactly the failure mode this report describes regardless of what actually triggers it next time. New `components/ErrorBoundary.tsx`: a class component (React has no hook equivalent for `componentDidCatch`) delegating its fallback UI to a separate theme-aware functional component (`useAppTheme()` isn't callable from a class), wired around the root `<Stack>` in `app/_layout.tsx` — a themed "Something went wrong / Try again" screen now replaces a silent blank one for any future render crash anywhere in the app.
4. **Deliberately not changed.** Did not reroute the returning-user boot path through `/loading`'s full wait-and-splash gate — that would add a wait to every cold app open (not just the post-redeploy window this specific report hit), a bigger behavior change than this investigation's evidence justified; the two targeted fixes above address the actual "looks broken, no signal" symptom without that tradeoff. Flagged as a `ROADMAP.md` Remaining Work item instead. Files: `client-mobile/lib/warmup.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/discover.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/community.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`.

**Not verifiable this session:** no physical device. The loop in (0) is proven by code inspection and is deterministic — `fetchProfile()`'s synchronous `set` and the `useCallback` dependency array are both unambiguous, and there is no path by which the effect can settle — but that it is the *whole* of what the user experienced cannot be confirmed without reproducing on-device. Every data-layer path Discover/Profile/Community depend on was independently verified working end-to-end against the real production backend during item (1)'s investigation, with both a fresh and a fully populated test account, so no server-side contribution remains. The cold-start hardening in (3) stands on its own merits regardless.

## Phase 75 — Air-Time Truth, Anime Split, Rewatch, Community Reviews & Performance

**(1) Air-time truth — the Upcoming pipeline lied about time.** `CachedEpisode` stored a bare `air_date` with no clock; the in-app Upcoming tab counted down to local midnight, so every countdown ended in `00m` and was wrong by up to a day, and only shows with a fixed TVmaze broadcast slot (`CachedShow.airs_time`/`airs_timezone`, Phase 73) ever got a real time at all — most streaming originals have no such slot. TVmaze's per-show `/lookup/shows` (Phase 73) returns nothing for a slot-less show; its `/shows/{id}/episodes` endpoint carries a per-episode `airstamp` (full ISO instant) that exists even for those shows — the actual missing source. New `fetch_episode_airstamps()` (`core/airtime.py`) parses it via `django.utils.dateparse.parse_datetime`; new `CachedEpisode.air_datetime`, `CachedShow.tvmaze_id`/`next_episode_air_datetime` (migration `0017_air_time_truth`). `sync_show_air_time()` bulk-updates `air_datetime` for a show's episodes bounded to `air_date >= today - 7 days` (not a full-catalogue rewrite) on the existing self-throttled monthly `airtime_checked_at` gate, and separately populates `next_episode_air_datetime` by matching `next_episode_season_number`/`next_episode_number`. Fixed a real coverage bug in `sync_active_shows()`: it only queued `Status.RETURNING` shows, so an ENDED-but-still-airing show with a dated next episode never got an air-time check — widened to include any show with a future `next_episode_air_date` or a recent `CachedEpisode.air_date`. New shared `lib/dateFormat.ts::resolveAirInstant(airDate, airDateTime?, airsTime?, airsTimezone?)` resolves in order: exact per-episode instant → show broadcast slot → local-midnight fallback — every countdown/air-time/grouping call site now routes through it, fixing a real cross-midnight bug along the way: an episode airing 9pm US-Eastern is already the next calendar day in IST, so date-bucketed UI (day badges, calendar grouping, the widget) previously showed it a day early for users east of the source timezone; `lib/upcoming.ts`, `components/CalendarGrid.tsx`, and both countdown call sites in `app/(tabs)/index.tsx` now derive their display date from the resolved instant instead of the raw `air_date` string. Widget: both `widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx` and `widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx` drop the countdown text entirely per the user's explicit ask, keeping the day badge and the resolved local air-time line; fixed an O(n²) header-label recomputation inside the Android widget's render loop (precomputed once instead of per-row) and deleted a stale comment in `dateFormat.ts` that no longer matched `widgetPayload.ts`'s actual (nothing-precomputed) behavior. Files: `backend/core/models.py`, `backend/core/migrations/0017_air_time_truth.py` (new), `backend/core/airtime.py`, `backend/core/tasks.py`, `backend/core/serializers.py`, `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/upcoming.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/widgetPayload.ts`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/CalendarGrid.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/__tests__/dateFormat.test.ts`.

**(2) Shows/Anime split — anime was a runtime heuristic buried in a filter chip, not a first-class surface.** New `components/HubHeadingSwitch.tsx` (two large Shows/Anime words, matching the header's existing title size, active/inactive via `c.textPrimary`/`c.textTertiary`) replaces the Shows Hub's static `<Text>Shows</Text>` title; filters WATCH LIST rows through the existing `lib/anime.ts::isAnimeByGenresAndLanguage()` heuristic (Animation genre + `original_language === 'ja'`) before bucketing, so the pill counts stay honest — UPCOMING is deliberately **not** filtered (explicit user decision, commented in-code so it doesn't read as an oversight later). New `app/profile/anime.tsx` ("My Anime") — a `SegmentedControl` Shows|Movies over the existing `watchlist`/`movieWatchlist` data, reusing `ShowRow`/`MovieRow` and the exported row/card components from `profile/shows.tsx`/`profile/movies.tsx`. Anime fully **removed** (not just hidden) from `profile/shows.tsx`/`profile/movies.tsx` — the `animeOnly` tag toggle and its filter-pipeline branch deleted from both, since the point of the split is a real separation, not a duplicate view; Profile's `totalShows`/`totalMovies` counts now exclude anime, with a new `totalAnime` covering both. Layout toggle repositioned below the filter-pill row on both `(tabs)/index.tsx` and `(tabs)/movies.tsx` (matching a supplied reference screenshot), leaving only the `ListChecks` list/grid button in the header's top-right slot; `profile/shows.tsx`/`profile/movies.tsx` keep their header-position toggle unchanged (no list button there, so no uniformity problem to solve). New Profile "Anime" section + "My Anime" row with a count badge, between Movies and Lists. Explicitly out of scope, stated rather than silently skipped: the Movies Hub tab (`(tabs)/movies.tsx`) still shows everything including anime movies — only the Shows Hub and My Movies were asked to split. Files: `client-mobile/components/HubHeadingSwitch.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/profile/anime.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/movies.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/shows.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/movies.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`.

**(3) Reviews gated on watched — a show/movie could be reviewed before ever being watched.** `ShowReviewView.post`/`MovieReviewView.post` now 403 unless `WatchState.objects.filter(user=, episode__show_id=).exists()` (≥1 episode — requiring 100% would be hostile to mid-season rating) or the movie itself is watched; DELETE stays ungated, since removing a review must always be possible. `RatingReviewCard.tsx` gained a `canReview: boolean` prop — false renders dimmed non-interactive stars, hides the note input, and shows a single "Mark as watched to rate" line, as one branch inside the existing card rather than a new component. Files: `backend/core/review_views.py`, `client-mobile/components/RatingReviewCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`.

**(4) Lists: context-aware default + Profile section.** `app/lists/index.tsx` now reads `?media=tv|movie` from `useLocalSearchParams()` to seed its media filter instead of hard-coding "All"; the Shows Hub and Movies Hub's list-button entry points now link in pre-filtered to their own media type. Added a "Lists" section heading with a count badge on Profile — the My Lists row previously dangled under the Movies heading with no heading of its own. Files: `client-mobile/app/lists/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/movies.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`.

**(5) Default layout preference — a single persisted global with only a blind toggle.** Replaced `preferredLayout`/`toggleLayout()` with `defaultLayout` (persisted, Settings-owned) + `layoutOverrides: Partial<Record<LayoutScope, LayoutMode>>` (persisted, per-screen: shows/movies/myShows/myMovies/myAnime) + a `useLayoutFor(scope)` selector; `setDefaultLayout()` also clears every override, matching the design intent that changing the default shouldn't leave stale per-screen overrides fighting it. Persist store `version` bumped with a `migrate` step seeding `defaultLayout` from any existing `preferredLayout`, so no one's current choice silently resets. All five consumers migrated in the same pass: `(tabs)/index.tsx` (including the Upcoming 3-way List/Grid/Calendar toggle), `(tabs)/movies.tsx`, `profile/shows.tsx`, `profile/movies.tsx`, the new `profile/anime.tsx`. New Settings "Default layout" row (List/Grid `SegmentedControl` + hint) inside the existing Appearance card. Files: `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/components/LayoutToggle.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/movies.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/shows.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/movies.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/anime.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/settings.tsx`.

**(6) Community: reviews feed, self-follow fix, performance.** `FriendsActivityView` rewritten from a `WatchState`/`MovieWatchState` union to real `ShowReview`/`MovieReview` rows from followees (same `MAX_FOLLOWEES`/`RAW_ROW_CAP`/window bounds, private accounts still excluded) — Activity now shows what someone thought, not just that they watched something; retired the old `episodes`/`movie` card types. Fixed a confirmed live bug while rebuilding it: `friends_feed_cache_key(user_id)` had no `page` component, so every page of the feed served back page 1's cached payload for the full 120s TTL — added `page` to the key, busted on review save/delete via `signals.py`. Fixed the reported bug: your own row in a Followers/Following list rendered a tappable "Follow" button that 400s (the DB's self-follow `CheckConstraint` guarantees `is_following` is always false for yourself) — `FollowEdgeSerializer` had no `is_self`, and `components/UserRow.tsx` already had an `isSelf` prop built for exactly this that neither `app/user/[username]/connections.tsx` nor `app/community.tsx` was passing; added `is_self` to the serializer (viewer id via context) and wired both call sites. Performance: Community's Discussions tab fired 8 parallel per-show `GET /comments/?show_id=` requests on every mount and pull-to-refresh; replaced with one new paginated `GET /api/comments/feed/` (`CommentFeedView`, built on the existing `CommentQuerysetMixin.annotated_queryset`) — 8 requests → 1. All three Community tabs plus `connections.tsx` converted `FlatList` → `FlashList` (repo standard; FlashList 2.0.2 needs no `estimatedItemSize`). `_build_public_profile_blob`/`FollowToggleView`'s separate `.count()` calls collapsed into one `annotate(Count(...))`. Files: `backend/core/social_views.py`, `backend/core/social_serializers.py`, `backend/core/cache_keys.py`, `backend/core/signals.py`, `backend/core/comment_views.py` (new `CommentFeedView`), `backend/core/comment_urls.py`, `client-mobile/store/socialStore.ts`, `client-mobile/components/ActivityRow.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/community.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/user/[username]/connections.tsx`.

**(7) Rewatch — nothing tracked a second viewing.** New parallel presence-based tables (migration `0018_rewatch`), deliberately not a change to `WatchState`'s own `UNIQUE(user, episode)` — load-bearing across the "already watched" check, badge counts, and every `Count()` in analytics, so relaxing it would silently corrupt all of those: `ShowRewatch` (a "round" — `user`, `show`, `round_number`, `started_at`, `completed_at`, `UniqueConstraint(user, show, round_number)`), `RewatchEpisodeState` (presence-based per-episode tick scoped to one round, `UniqueConstraint(rewatch, episode)`), `MovieRewatch` (append-only, no unique constraint — a rewatch is a real repeatable event, undone by deleting the most recent row). `UserProfile.total_rewatch_time_watched` tracks the breakdown while a rewatch still also adds to `total_time_watched` (both move, by design). New `core/rewatch_views.py`/`rewatch_urls.py`: `ShowRewatchDetailView` (GET active round + DELETE to cancel), `ShowRewatchStartView` (400 unless every aired episode is already in `WatchState` and no round is already active; `round_number = max(existing) + 1`, starting at 2), `RewatchEpisodeToggleView` (presence toggle within the active round, `±runtime_minutes` on both time-watched counters mirroring `WatchStateToggleView`'s F()-expression clamp-to-0 pattern, stamps `completed_at` when the round covers every aired episode), `MovieRewatchCreateView` (POST +1 row / DELETE removes the most recent). Found and fixed a real design bug before it shipped: filtering the "active round" lookup by `completed_at__isnull=True` made a just-completed round immediately un-toggleable (untick the last episode → 400 on the very next tap) — the toggle view and detail GET now resolve the *latest* round regardless of completion state, while `ShowRewatchStartView`'s "one already active" check correctly keeps the `completed_at__isnull=True` filter. `WatchlistSerializer.active_rewatch`/`MovieWatchlistSerializer.rewatch_count` added (two-repo change, TS mirrors updated same pass). `app/show/[id].tsx` gained a "Rewatch"/"Rewatching · Round N" card when `progress_percentage === 100`, with episode checkmarks writing rewatch state instead of `WatchState` while a round is active; `app/movie/[id].tsx` gained "Watch again" (subtitle: "Watched N×"). Every mark-watched entry point honors rewatch mode: `components/EpisodeRow.tsx`, `app/show/[id]/season/[season].tsx`, `app/episode/[id].tsx` — traced (not assumed) that the Shows Hub row and Upcoming row checkmarks specifically need no extra branching, since a fully-watched show (rewatch's own precondition) can never produce an unwatched-episode row on either surface. `lib/useCatchupCascade.ts` no longer fires while a rewatch round is active. Rewatch minutes added to `AnalyticsDashboardView` as a distinct "Rewatched" tile; `RewatchEpisodeState.watched_at`/`MovieRewatch.watched_at` unioned into the heatmap and `AnalyticsStatisticsView`'s daily/weekly/monthly buckets. Files: `backend/core/models.py`, `backend/core/migrations/0018_rewatch.py` (new), `backend/core/rewatch_views.py` (new), `backend/core/rewatch_urls.py` (new), `backend/core/urls.py`, `backend/core/serializers.py`, `backend/core/analytics_views.py`, `backend/core/analytics_serializers.py`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id]/season/[season].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/episode/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/components/EpisodeRow.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/SeasonCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/lib/useCatchupCascade.ts`.

**(8) Performance — the free tier was burning most of its Redis budget on infra chatter, not real traffic.** Infra (inert until the next Render redeploy, not yet triggered): `render-start.sh` disables Celery gossip/mingle/heartbeat and drops both worker/beat to `-l WARNING`; `CELERY_BROKER_TRANSPORT_OPTIONS = {"visibility_timeout": 900, "polling_interval": 5.0}` widens the ~1s default BRPOP polling (the single largest confirmed Upstash command source); `CELERY_TASK_IGNORE_RESULT=True` (confirmed safe — nothing in `core/` uses `AsyncResult`/`.get(timeout=...)`), `CELERY_WORKER_ENABLE_REMOTE_CONTROL=False`, `CELERY_WORKER_SEND_TASK_EVENTS=False`; `resume_stalled_imports` crontab widened `*/5`→`*/15`. DRF throttling moved off the Redis-backed default cache: new `CACHES["throttling"]` `LocMemCache` alias + `core/throttling.py` (`LocalAnonRateThrottle`/`LocalUserRateThrottle`) — 2 Redis round trips per throttle class per request, eliminated; safe specifically because `render-start.sh` runs a single gunicorn worker, so per-process counters are still a real limit (`--max-requests 500` recycles the process periodically, resetting counters — an accepted tradeoff for abuse protection, not routine correctness). Backend queries: found and fixed 5 occurrences (the plan named 2) of the same cartesian-join anti-pattern — annotating one queryset with two `Count(..., distinct=True)` aggregates over two different relations off the same base row explodes into a join-then-dedupe row count — across `analytics_views.py` (Dashboard/Completion/YearReview) and `views.py` (`WatchlistView`, `ContinueWatchingView`), each replaced with two flat `.values(...).annotate(Count(...))` queries merged in Python (the codebase's own established convention, e.g. `episodes_by_show`, chosen over the plan's literally-worded `Prefetch` suggestion). Self-caught via new tests before reaching real usage: 5 of these grouped queries raised `FieldError` — `CachedEpisode`'s primary key is `tmdb_id`, not `id`, so `Count("id")` doesn't exist on that queryset; fixed to `Count("pk")`. `lists_views.py`'s `CustomListsView.get()` collapsed from 3 full `CustomListItem` table scans to one `select_related` query grouped in Python. `BulkWatchStateToggleView`'s per-loop `CachedShow.objects.get()` replaced with `in_bulk(show_ids)`. Caching added to `AnalyticsDashboardView`/`AnalyticsStatisticsView` (300s)/`AnalyticsMonthlySummaryView` (900s), busted by the existing watch-mutation signal hooks plus the new rewatch models. Frontend: `analytics.tsx`/`profile.tsx`'s focus-effect fetch batches gained a `analyticsDirtyAt` staleness guard (bumped in `fetchProfile`) instead of refetching unconditionally on every focus. `(tabs)/index.tsx`'s Upcoming clock now ticks at 1s only when the soonest item is under an hour out, else 60s — simplified from an initial design that used a redundant bridge state + extra `useEffect` to work around a perceived circular dependency, replaced with a plain unmemoized per-render computation once the extra render was recognized as self-defeating for a performance fix. Files: `backend/render-start.sh`, `backend/config/settings/base.py`, `backend/core/throttling.py` (new), `backend/core/analytics_views.py`, `backend/core/views.py`, `backend/core/lists_views.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_watchlist_counts.py` (new), `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/analytics.tsx`.

**(9) Sweep.** Extracted the byte-duplicated `FilterPill` (`(tabs)/index.tsx` and `(tabs)/movies.tsx`, differing only by 2px of padding) into `components/FilterPill.tsx`. Removed now-dead code: widget `formatCountdown` imports, `animeOnly` state on the two profile screens, retired activity-card types, a stale `dateFormat.ts` comment. Design-system compliance re-checked on every new/edited surface (colors from `useAppTheme().theme.colors` only, `PressableScale` for taps, `GlassSurface` for cards). Re-verified the `tsc` baseline fresh rather than carrying it forward, per this repo's own recorded history of a "pre-existing" TS error once turning out to be a live crash — confirmed zero, nothing carried. Files: `client-mobile/components/FilterPill.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/movies.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`.

**(10) Auto-logout bug report (user-reported mid-session, not part of the 9-phase plan) — two independent, compounding root causes.** `lib/api.ts`'s `performRefresh()` treated any failure during a token refresh — including a genuinely transient network blip — identically to "session expired," wiping otherwise-valid tokens; rewritten around a discriminated `RefreshOutcome` (`success`/`expired`/`transient_failure`) so tokens are only cleared on an actual 401/403 from the refresh endpoint. Separately, `app/_layout.tsx`'s boot-time auth check set `isAuthenticated(false)` on a SecureStore-read timeout (a 2s race), forcing an incorrect redirect to `/login` despite valid stored tokens — changed to optimistically assume authenticated on that specific timeout path; a genuinely logged-out user still gets correctly bounced one round-trip later via the normal 401→refresh→expired flow, so the fix carries no downside for that case. Files: `client-mobile/lib/api.ts`, `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`.

**Batch-wide verification:** `manage.py check` clean, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean after applying both `0017` and `0018`, `pytest` 192/192 inside the `watchtracker_backend` container (up from 148 — 44 new: `test_rewatch.py` (17, new file), `test_rewatch_analytics.py` (4, new file), `test_comment_feed.py` (3, new file), `test_watchlist_counts.py` (7, new file), rest split across expansions to `test_reviews.py`, `test_social_views.py`, `test_airtime.py`), `tsc --noEmit` (`node --stack-size=8000`) zero errors, `jest` 12/12 (up from 7 — 5 new `resolveAirInstant`/`resolveDisplayDateIso` cases in `__tests__/dateFormat.test.ts`). **Deliberately not done this session:** the plan's own suggested live Django-shell check against real TVmaze (a network show + a slot-less streaming show) — covered instead by `test_airtime.py`'s TVmaze-response-branch tests, not a live external call; the Render redeploy needed for (8)'s infra savings to actually take effect. **Not verifiable this session: no physical device or emulator** — the countdown-removed widget layout, the Shows/Anime heading toggle and repositioned layout controls, every rewatch UI surface, and the resolved-local-air-time line all need a real device pass; verified instead via the full backend test suite, `tsc`, `jest`, and direct code-path tracing.

# Last Updated (prior): 2026-08-01 (Phase 74 — a single large user-issued 14-item bundle, executed in one continuous pass with performance deliberately last per the user's own sequencing: unreleased-content gating extended to movies (TV side was already correct), half-star ratings, a full social layer (follow graph/search/public profiles/activity feed), Watch Activity full-history heatmap, a Movies analytics segment, avatar picker expansion, a backend/client performance pass, and a final security/audit pass — plus a mid-session ad hoc follow-up ("buttons feel laggy... everything should be snappy") that added a scoped React re-render/perf pass on top. Full detail below.

**(1) Unreleased-content gating extended to movies — TV episodes were already correctly blocked from being marked watched before airing; movies had no equivalent check at all.** `MovieWatchStateToggleView` had zero release-date validation on its create branch, and `MovieRow`/`MoviePosterCard` had no `disabled` concept whatsoever (unlike `ShowRow`/`ShowPosterCard`, which already dim and disable the checkmark for a future episode). New shared helpers — `backend/core/dates.py::has_released()` (null date = not released, for user-initiated toggle gates) and `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts::hasAired()` — replace what was previously 9+ hand-inlined `x.air_date && x.air_date <= today` checks scattered across the client. Gated `MovieWatchStateToggleView`'s create branch (mirrors `WatchStateToggleView` exactly, including "null date counts as not-yet-released") and `tasks.py`'s `_import_one_movie` (matching how `_import_one_show` was already gated). Wired `isReleased`/`disabled` into all four movie toggle entry points: the list row, its animation-flush path, the grid checkmark, and `movie/[id].tsx`'s `handlePrimaryAction` (which serves both the header icon and the big action button). Fixed two related silent-correctness bugs uncovered while building this: `BulkWatchStateToggleView` was already filtering unaired episodes out of what it actually created, but its response echoed back *every* requested id regardless — so a client's optimistic state could silently diverge from the server with no error surfaced at all; fixed by returning `applied_episode_ids`/`skipped_unaired_ids` and having `watchStore.bulkToggleWatchState` reconcile against them. `CatchupCheckView` counted previous-unwatched episodes with no air-date filter, so the Catch-Up modal could promise a count that a subsequent `bulk-toggle` call would then partially refuse — added the same `air_date__lte=today` filter the toggle views already use. Exposed and fixed one more latent bug along the way: `TMDBService.get_show_details()`/`get_movie_details()` passed raw TMDB JSON date strings into `update_or_create(defaults={...})`, which Django does not auto-coerce on plain attribute assignment (only a DB round-trip parses it) — so a fresh cache-miss object's date fields were still strings in memory, crashing the new `has_released()` date comparison. Root-caused and fixed with a new `_parse_tmdb_date()` helper applied at all 3 call sites, not worked around in the gating logic. Files: `backend/core/dates.py` (new), `backend/core/views.py`, `backend/core/tasks.py`, `backend/core/services.py`, `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`, `client-mobile/components/MovieRow.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/MoviePosterCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/movies.tsx`.

**(2) Half-star ratings — the existing rating system was worse than "unsupported."** `review_views.py` cast the incoming rating with Python's `int()`, which does not raise on a float — `int(3.5)` silently returns `3` and the endpoint returned 200 OK, meaning a half-star rating was already being silently corrupted before this phase, not merely rejected. `ShowReview.rating`/`MovieReview.rating` changed from an implicit small integer to `DecimalField(max_digits=2, decimal_places=1, validators=[MinValueValidator(Decimal("0.5")), MaxValueValidator(Decimal("5.0"))])` plus a `CheckConstraint` pinning the value to the 10 legal half-steps (migration `0015` — no `RunPython` data migration needed, since Postgres implicitly upcasts an existing `smallint 3` to `numeric 3.0` on the column-type `ALTER`). Both review serializers needed an explicit per-field `coerce_to_string=False` — DRF's global default serializes `Decimal` as a JSON *string*, which would have silently changed the API's response type for every existing integer rating too. New module-level `_parse_rating(raw)` in `review_views.py`, using `Decimal(str(raw))` rather than `Decimal(raw)` directly — DRF hands the view a Python float, and `Decimal(3.5)` (float constructor) is actually `3.5000000000000002220...`, not the clean `3.5` the string-then-Decimal path produces — validated against a set of the ten legal values. New `client-mobile/components/StarRatingDisplay.tsx` (read-only, half-fill via a full outline star plus a second filled star clipped inside an `overflow:'hidden'` half-width wrapper — explicitly `{position:'absolute', top:0, left:0, height, width}`, not `StyleSheet.absoluteFill`, which sets `right:0` and double-constrains Yoga against an explicit width) and a substantially rewritten `RatingReviewCard.tsx` for the interactive version, using one `PressableScale` per star (not two nested Pressables, which would break the spring animation) with the half/full zone derived from `e.nativeEvent.locationX < SIZE/2`. New `app/profile/reviews.tsx` — the `GET /reviews/shows/`/`/reviews/movies/` list endpoints already existed, fully paginated and working, and were simply never consumed by any screen before this phase. Files: `backend/core/models.py`, `backend/core/migrations/0015_alter_moviereview_rating_alter_showreview_rating_and_more.py` (new), `backend/core/review_serializers.py`, `backend/core/review_views.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_reviews.py`, `client-mobile/components/StarRatingDisplay.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/components/RatingReviewCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/reviews.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`. Verified: a dedicated pytest run with `--create-db` after the migration caught the exact staleness trap this repo's own `pytest.ini` (`--nomigrations --reuse-db`) has bitten before — the cached test DB schema silently predated the new column type until rebuilt once, and ratings were briefly (falsely) observed rounding under the stale schema.

**(3) Full social layer — Community went from a client-side comment aggregator to a real follow graph, in one phase, per the user's explicit "make it way better and most capable" ask (`/ultrathink`).** Backend, new modules following the existing `lists_*`/`review_*`/`analytics_*` precedent: `social_serializers.py`, `social_views.py`, `social_urls.py`. New `Follow` model — presence-based (a row's existence *is* the relationship, mirroring `CommentLike`/`WatchState`, not a boolean field), UUID pk, `follower`/`following` FKs with explicit `related_name`s (`outgoing_follows`/`incoming_follows` — two FKs to the same model require distinct names or Django raises `fields.E304`), a `UniqueConstraint(follower, following)` and a `CheckConstraint(~Q(follower=F("following")))` (Django 6 `condition=`, matching `Comment.comment_exactly_one_target`'s existing pattern), indexes on both `(follower, -created_at)` and `(following, -created_at)`. New `UserProfile.is_private` — a single boolean ("ghost mode": excluded from search, activity feed, and everyone else's view of the profile) — deliberately **not** a follow-request/approve flow, which would force a `status` field onto `Follow` and break the presence-based convention this codebase standardizes on; the privacy need is covered at a fraction of the complexity. Six new endpoints, all under `/api/v1/`, all paginated via the existing `StandardResultsPagination`: `UserSearchView` (`select_related("profile")` mandatory to avoid N+1 on avatars, an `Exists()` `is_following` annotation, a `Case/When` prefix-match-first rank, private accounts excluded via `Q(profile__isnull=True) | Q(profile__is_private=False)`); `FollowToggleView` (copies `CommentLikeToggleView`'s exact body — filter-then-delete-or-create with an `IntegrityError`-inside-`atomic()` guard for the concurrent-double-tap race — self-follow returns 400, a nonexistent OR private target returns 404, deliberately indistinguishable); `FollowerListView`/`FollowingListView` (query the `Follow` table directly, resolve the viewer's own `is_following` once per page via a single `IN` query passed through serializer context, not per row); `UserProfileDetailView` (~9-query target-owned blob cached 300s, viewer-relative `is_self`/`is_following`/`follows_you` computed fresh every request from one combined edge query so someone else's cached profile can never leak into your own follow state); `FriendsActivityView` (read-time fan-in, deliberately **not** a fan-out inbox table — the single Celery worker `--concurrency=1` already shares capacity with TV Time imports and can't absorb a write on every episode/movie watched — bounded by a 14-day window that is load-bearing (without it `ORDER BY watched_at DESC` would sort over a followee's entire history), `MAX_FOLLOWEES=200`, `RAW_ROW_CAP=400`/source, a 120s cache, and multi-episode binges collapsed into one card — grouped by `(user, show, local calendar day)` — before pagination). Privacy contract enforced structurally, not just by convention: `PublicUserSerializer`/`FollowEdgeSerializer` in `social_serializers.py` are plain `serializers.Serializer`, never `ModelSerializer` with `fields="__all__"`, which would be one careless edit away from leaking email, reviews, or private lists. `UserProfileSerializer` (existing) extended with `follower_count`/`following_count` SerializerMethodFields so the Profile bar needs no second request. Client: new non-persisted `store/socialStore.ts` (mirrors `listsStore.ts`'s precedent — nothing here is worth surviving an app restart offline), `components/UserRow.tsx`/`FollowButton.tsx`/`ActivityRow.tsx`, `community.tsx` restructured into a `SegmentedControl` hub (Discussions | Activity | People) with the original comment-aggregator body kept *verbatim* as Discussions (the one part of the old screen that actually worked, `MAX_SHOWS_TO_QUERY=8` fan-out and all), each segment fetching only when active. New `app/user/[username].tsx` (public profile — stats grid, top shows, recent movies, public lists, followers/following bar) and `app/user/[username]/connections.tsx` (`?tab=followers|following`), both registered in `_layout.tsx`. `CommentCard.tsx`/`ReplyCard.tsx`'s author name/avatar made tappable through to the new public profile (nested `PressableScale` inside the card's own outer `PressableScale`, confirmed safe via the existing `CommentActions` precedent of nested Pressables in this exact file). Profile's social bar restored from the Phase 12-era collapsed 2 cells (Shows/Movies, Followers/Following having been removed back then as permanent hardcoded zeros with no social graph to back them) to the originally-intended 4 cells — resync was previously the *entire* bar's press target, which is now moved off onto the watch-time card alone (which already had the `RefreshCw` hint and is the card whose numbers actually drift), since a tap on "Followers" silently triggering a stats resync was a genuinely surprising side effect once the cell became a real navigation target. A Settings "Private account" toggle was added for `is_private` — a client affordance for a field the backend already accepted but nothing in the UI could set. Files (backend): `backend/core/models.py`, `backend/core/migrations/0016_userprofile_is_private_follow.py` (new), `backend/core/social_serializers.py` (new), `backend/core/social_views.py` (new), `backend/core/social_urls.py` (new), `backend/core/urls.py`, `backend/core/serializers.py`, `backend/core/cache_keys.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_social_views.py` (new, 15 tests). Files (client): `client-mobile/store/socialStore.ts` (new), `client-mobile/components/UserRow.tsx`/`FollowButton.tsx`/`ActivityRow.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/community.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/user/[username].tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/user/[username]/connections.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/components/CommentCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/ReplyCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/settings.tsx`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`. Verified: 15 new backend tests covering self-follow rejection, private-account 404 indistinguishability on search/follow/profile/followers, the double-tap race guard, binge-collapsing, and the 14-day window exclusion.

**(4) Watch Activity full history — the heatmap was hard-capped at ~1 year; the user wanted "full history since they started using the app," "more accurate."** Extended the existing `AnalyticsHeatmapView` with a `?range=all` mode rather than adding a second endpoint — the pre-existing `?days=` path (dense, uncached, still used by the 365-day view and the 30-day streak strip) is completely untouched. `?range=all` derives the start date from `WatchState.objects.aggregate(Min("watched_at"))` (clamped to 15 years back, defensively — not an expected case), returns a sparse, year-grouped envelope (only days with actual activity, plus per-year rollups: `episodes_watched`/`minutes_watched`/`days_active`/`max_episodes_in_a_day`) rather than a dense day-by-day fill — ten dense years would be 3,650 objects; sparse is the user's real count of active days. Cached 900s under its own key; the 365-day path deliberately stays uncached (staleness there is visible — mark an episode, tab back, today's cell should light up immediately). Found and fixed a real normalization bug that the sparse/multi-year mode made unworkable: intensity was computed as a quartile of the *window's own max* single-day episode count — over a multi-year window, one 17-episode binge day anywhere in the whole history flattened every ordinary 1-4 episode day down to the lowest intensity, since `eps >= max_eps * 0.25` etc. all failed against a max of 17. Replaced with fixed thresholds (7+/4+/2+/1 → intensity 4/3/2/1), with a direct 1:1 mapping fallback when a user's own `scale_max` never reaches 4 (a light watcher's few episodes still span the color range instead of flattening to intensity 1 the opposite way). This is a shared helper (`_intensity_for_count`), so the fix's blast radius is stated and accepted explicitly: the existing 365-day heatmap's colors change, and so does `AnalyticsStreakView`'s 30-day mini-strip — deliberate, since the old scheme was wrong on both surfaces for the identical reason, not just for whichever one motivated the fix. Client: `WatchHeatmap.tsx` — which already technically supported an arbitrary-length `data` array (it derives week count from `data.length`) — would have rendered ~3,640 `<Rect>`s in one non-virtualized `<Svg>` for a decade of history; instead it renders **one year at a time** behind a year-chip selector (53×7 ≈ 371 rects, identical cost to the existing 365-day view), with a per-year summary strip (all four numbers straight from the payload) and a guarded `scrollToEnd` on `onContentSizeChange` (the guard ref resets per year-change specifically so it doesn't fight the user's own scrolling on every subsequent layout pass). New `heatmapAll`/`fetchHeatmapAll()` in `watchStore.ts`, deliberately **not** added to `analytics.tsx`'s existing `useFocusEffect` batch (already 6 parallel requests on a 4-thread Render box) — fired lazily only when the user taps "Full history." Files: `backend/core/analytics_views.py`, `backend/core/analytics_serializers.py`, `backend/core/cache_keys.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_analytics_views.py`, `client-mobile/components/WatchHeatmap.tsx`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`. Verified: new tests for the intensity helper's two branches (fixed-threshold vs. light-watcher fallback) and the sparse year-grouping/rollup math against multi-year fixture data.

**(5) Movie analytics section, added as a segment alongside the existing TV-only dashboard — explicit user decision that the existing TV numbers must not change.** New `AnalyticsMoviesView` (~7 queries, cached 300s, invalidated via the *existing* `MovieWatchlist`/`MovieWatchState` signal-receiver pair in `signals.py` rather than a new invalidation path): watched/tracked/completion, total+average runtime, this-year count, longest movie, top genres, by-decade breakdown, recent movies. The genre computation deliberately avoids the `_genre_stats` per-row-iteration pattern used for shows (which exists *because* `CachedShow.genres` is an `ArrayField` with no combo to group by) — `MovieCache.genres_string` is a comma-separated combo string with only a few dozen distinct combinations across any real library, so it groups by the raw combo string in one query, then splits+tallies in Python over those few dozen rows, not over every watched movie. Explicitly did **not** build a top-actor/director section for movies — no per-user movie credits data is persisted anywhere in the schema, and fabricating one would be a third dishonest-data surface next to `AnalyticsProvidersView`'s already-honest empty stub. Mounted as a `SegmentedControl` (Shows | Movies) on `analytics.tsx`, with `fetchMovieAnalytics()` fired lazily on first switch to the Movies segment rather than added to the screen's already-6-request focus-effect batch. While building this, found and fixed a confirmed-live, unrelated bug: `AnalyticsCompletionView`'s `movie_completion_pct` was hardcoded `0.0` with a comment claiming "movies not tracked separately in this schema" — false; `MovieWatchlist`/`MovieWatchState` are real tables queried elsewhere in the same file. The field was *also* missing from `CompletionSerializer` entirely, meaning DRF would have silently dropped it even if the view alone had been fixed — both fixed together, and the client's own hardcoded `moviePct={0}` (which ignored whatever the API actually returned) removed. Two smaller correctness/cleanup items done in the same file while already there: deleted a genuinely dead function (`_watch_time_summary`, zero call sites, confirmed via grep); collapsed `AnalyticsMonthlySummaryView`'s 12-months-in-a-Python-loop (each iteration independently re-querying count/sum/genre/top-show — roughly 4 queries × 12) into one query for the whole year plus a single Python pass, same `select_related`+`Counter` shape `_genre_stats` already established; renamed the misleadingly-named `AnalyticsActorsView` (it returns the user's own MVP-*character* votes, not real actor/cast data, which doesn't exist per-user anywhere in this schema) to `AnalyticsMVPCharactersView`/`/api/analytics/mvp-characters/` — safe since a grep confirmed zero client consumers ever called the old path. Files: `backend/core/analytics_views.py`, `backend/core/analytics_serializers.py`, `backend/core/analytics_urls.py`, `backend/core/cache_keys.py`, `backend/core/signals.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_analytics_views.py`, `client-mobile/app/analytics.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/GenreChart.tsx` (widened its `data` prop to `Pick<GenreStat,'genre'|'percentage'>[]` plus an optional `title?` prop, so the movies segment could reuse it directly instead of a parallel near-identical chart component), `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`.

**(6) Avatar picker expanded from 2 tabs to 3.** "Cast" renamed "Characters" to match what it actually returns (TMDB has no dedicated character-portrait asset — every photo is unavoidably the credited actor's real headshot). Cartoon's DiceBear style set broadened from 6 styles to 9 (`avataaars`/`croodles`/`fun-emoji`/`lorelei`/`open-peeps`/`personas`/`pixel-art`/`shapes`/`bottts-neutral`); new "Icons" tab (`identicon`/`rings`/`shapes`/`initials`) for abstract/geometric options. The `data` memo — previously a binary ternary between the two old pools — converted to a `switch` over the tab so a future 4th pool is a one-line addition. Backend companion fix, done proactively rather than reactively: `get_popular_characters()` (feeds the Characters tab) loops up to 16 sequential uncached TMDB credits calls, which could block one of 4 gunicorn threads for the entire chain on this endpoint's 24h cache miss — broadening the character pool without also parallelizing that loop would have made an already-fragile cold-path worse, not just wider. Parallelized via `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=8)` + `pool.map()` (preserves input order in its results regardless of completion order, keeping the picker's output deterministic), matching the identical pattern `DiscoverFeedView` already established. Files: `client-mobile/components/AvatarPickerModal.tsx`, `backend/core/services.py`.

**(7) Editable username, end to end — Settings had a Username row that looked interactive but did nothing; Profile's EDIT button opened the avatar picker despite its label implying more.** `UserProfileSerializer.username` made writable (`source="user.username"`, DRF's nested source builds `validated_data["user"]` on write, which the serializer's `update()` pops and saves onto the related `User` row separately, since the serializer itself targets `UserProfile`) with a `validate_username` doing case-insensitive uniqueness (`username__iexact`, excluding self) plus Django's own username charset/length validator. New shared `EditProfileSheet.tsx` — Username field + Change Photo — consumed from *both* Profile's EDIT button (previously mislabeled) and Settings' previously-inert Username row, rather than building two separate small UIs for the same action. Found and fixed a real security/robustness gap during the Group L security review (not user-reported): `validate_username`'s pre-check is case-*insensitive*, but `User.username`'s actual DB-level unique constraint is case-*sensitive* (Django's default `AbstractUser` field) — a narrow but real TOCTOU race meant two concurrent renames landing on the exact same username could both pass the pre-check before either committed, and the second `.save()`'s `IntegrityError` would have propagated as an unhandled 500 rather than the same "already taken" 400 the pre-check normally returns. Fixed with a `try/except IntegrityError` inside `UserProfileSerializer.update()`, converting to the identical `ValidationError`. Files: `backend/core/serializers.py`, `backend/core/profile_views.py`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/components/EditProfileSheet.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/settings.tsx`.

**(8) Lists brought to the Shows hub (previously Movies-only) + a "My Lists" row on Profile.** Backend needed zero changes — `CustomListItem.media_type` was already `tv|movie`, `CustomListItemToggleView` already validated against both, `_build_cover_map` already resolved `CachedShow` covers alongside `MovieCache` — this was purely a client wiring gap. Added the `ListChecks` header button to the Shows hub (copying `movies.tsx`'s existing button exactly), an `AddToListSheet`+`ListPlus` action on the show detail screen (the slot freed by removing Archive, item 10 below), and a per-media-type `SegmentedControl` (All/Shows/Movies) on `/lists`, backed by a new `tv_count`/`movie_count` breakdown on `CustomListSerializer` and `listsStore.ts`'s `toggleListItem`. Files: `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/lists/index.tsx`, `backend/core/lists_views.py`, `backend/core/serializers.py`, `client-mobile/store/listsStore.ts`.

**(9) Quick fixes bundle.** Removed the duplicate Settings entry — the header gear icon and a "More" section row both routed to `/settings`; kept the Community row in "More" since the header's own Users icon leads to the much richer Community hub now (item 3), not a duplicate. Removed the Archive button from the show detail screen entirely (button, imports, state, handler) — explicitly **client-only**: the backend `ArchiveToggleView`/`ARCHIVED` status and every other consumer (`watchStore.ts`'s undo-remove re-POST, `profile/shows.tsx`'s "Stopped" chip, `tasks.py`'s push/digest exclusion) were deliberately left alone, since removing the model field itself would have been a silent breaking change across five consumers for a UI-only ask. Rounded the Google Sign-In button's corners to `14` to match the primary Sign In/Sign Up buttons' established radius — `GoogleSigninButton` is a native view, so the `borderRadius` needed a wrapping `View` with `overflow:'hidden'` (a style prop alone doesn't reliably clip a native view on Android). Fixed the reported "missing movie duration" (user screenshot: Supergirl's title/rating/runtime text illegible over the backdrop) — runtime was already rendering; the actual bug was `heroRow`'s negative `marginTop` (intentionally pulling the poster over the backdrop) combined with `heroMeta`'s `justifyContent:'flex-end'`, which meant any hero content taller than the poster (a 3-line title, the meta row, genre chips) overflowed *upward* into the backdrop's dark gradient, illegible against light-theme ink. Fixed on `movie/[id].tsx`, `show/[id].tsx` (identical pattern), and `episode/[id].tsx` (checked proactively for the same pattern rather than assuming only the two reported screens had it) by switching to `flex-start` + a `paddingTop` sized to clear the backdrop, keeping only the poster's own negative offset. Files: `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/episode/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/components/SocialSignInButtons.tsx`.

**(10) Performance pass — deliberately done last, per the user's own explicit sequencing ("maybe do this as last after all update and fixes").** The real worst-case TMDB proxy latency was ~43.5s: `Retry(total=3)` is 4 attempts, and a prior phase's own in-code comment claiming a "3.5s bound" counted only the backoff *sleep* between attempts, not that each attempt also carries its own `timeout` (10s at the time) — 4×10s + 3.5s ≈ 43.5s, comfortably past the client's 15s axios timeout even though the backend was still legitimately working, which is the actual mechanism behind the intermittent "Can't reach Glix" reports on a merely-slow (not dead) TMDB. Tightened to `total=2` (3 attempts) and a `timeout` default of `4` — new worst case ≈13.5s, inside the client's budget with real margin. Parallelized `DiscoverGenresView`'s 16 sequential TMDB calls (one `discover_tv`/`discover_movies` call per genre, on a 24h cache miss) via the same `ThreadPoolExecutor` pattern `DiscoverFeedView` already established. Infra: added `CONN_MAX_AGE=60` (was unset anywhere in the project — every single request opened a fresh TLS connection to the managed Postgres host) and Redis `OPTIONS` socket timeouts (`socket_connect_timeout`/`socket_timeout=3` — confirmed by reading Django's *native* `RedisCache` backend source, not assumed, that `OPTIONS` passes straight through to redis-py's `ConnectionPool.from_url()`; deliberately did **not** add `IGNORE_EXCEPTIONS`, since that is a django-redis-only feature this backend doesn't implement — passing it would raise a `TypeError` from redis-py's own connection constructor rather than degrading gracefully). Show/movie detail screens no longer blindly call `fetchWatchlist()`/`fetchMovieWatchlist()` on every mount when the store already has data loaded — both screens only need the full list to look up their own entry, and the Shows/Movies Hub tabs already keep it fresh on their own focus effects; a mount-time refetch on every single detail-screen open previously hit `/watchlist/?page_size=all`, the heaviest endpoint in the app, every single time. Client request hygiene: `discoverStore.ts`'s `runSearch` (every debounced keystroke) and `fetchFilteredResults` (every Filter & Sort toggle) had no in-flight dedup at all — fixed with module-scope `AbortController` refs, aborting the previous request before starting the next. This surfaced a real companion bug in `lib/api.ts`: its own transient-failure retry interceptor would otherwise treat a deliberately-aborted request identically to a dropped connection (`!error.response`) and retry the already-superseded request, silently undoing the cancellation — fixed with an `axios.isCancel(error)` guard. Both `discover.tsx` (~25 fields) and `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` (mounted alongside the Discover screen at all times, including while closed) converted from a bare unselected `useDiscoverStore()` destructure to per-field scoped selectors — the exact pattern already documented and applied to `index.tsx`/`movies.tsx`/`profile/*`/`community.tsx` in earlier phases, just missed for Discover. Two smaller backend cleanups: `ShowDetailView`/`MovieDetailView` each instantiated `TMDBService()` twice per request — now one shared instance reused for both calls; `DiscoverFilterView`'s trending+filter branch (TMDB's `/trending` has no server-side genre/language filter, so this filters one already-fetched page in Python) left `total_pages` at TMDB's raw unfiltered count, letting the client's "load more" pagination promise dozens of pages that could legitimately come back with 0-3 items after filtering — capped defensively to 20 pages when a filter is active (not a full fix; TMDB genuinely doesn't support this server-side). Files: `backend/core/services.py`, `backend/core/views.py`, `backend/core/search_views.py`, `backend/config/settings/base.py`, `client-mobile/store/discoverStore.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/api.ts`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`.

**(11) Mid-session ad hoc follow-up: user reported buttons "feel laggy," then broadened it to "not only buttons but everything should be snappy and fast."** Investigated rather than guessed. Found `ShowRow`/`MovieRow`/`ShowPosterCard`/`MoviePosterCard` — the row/card components rendered inside the Shows Hub's and Movies Hub's `FlashList`s — were never wrapped in `React.memo`, unlike `EpisodeRow`/`CommentCard`/`ReplyCard`, which already were (an established but inconsistently-applied pattern in this codebase). Without memoization, every visible row/card in a list re-renders on *any* mutation to the store's `watchlist`/`movieWatchlist`, not just the one row whose own data actually changed — which is exactly what a checkmark tap "dragging the whole list along with it" looks like from the outside. Traced the root cause one layer deeper into why memoization alone wouldn't have been sufficient: `toggleWatchState`'s optimistic `set()` unconditionally rebuilt all 3 watchlist buckets (`to_watch`/`up_to_date`/`archived`) into fresh array/object references on every single episode toggle, even though only one bucket's one entry ever actually changes — the other two buckets' unrelated shows got brand-new object references for no reason, which is enough on its own to force every subscriber (and, pre-memo, every row) to re-render. Far more significant: `bulkToggleWatchState` (the endpoint behind both "Mark Season Watched" and the Catch-Up cascade) iterated every bucket with `.map()` and rebuilt *every entry's* nested `show`/`episodes` objects unconditionally — including entries with *zero* episodes in the toggled set — so a single-show 10-episode cascade was allocating fresh objects for the user's *entire* tracked library on every call. Fixed both: `toggleWatchState` now only touches the one bucket that actually changed (`...state.watchlist` spread for the other two, preserving their existing references exactly); `bulkToggleWatchState` now short-circuits and returns the exact same entry reference for any show with none of the toggled episodes, which is what actually lets `FlashList` skip re-invoking `renderItem` for those rows at all (memoization alone can't help if the incoming data reference changes regardless of content). Memoized all four components (`ShowRowComponent`/`MovieRowComponent`/`ShowPosterCardComponent`/`MoviePosterCardComponent`, wrapped via `export default memo(...)`, matching `EpisodeRow`'s established naming convention). While auditing for the broader "everything" ask, also found and fixed one more bare-unselected-store-hook instance beyond item 10's Discover fixes: `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` had the identical bare `useDiscoverStore()` gap. Files: `client-mobile/components/ShowRow.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/MovieRow.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/ShowPosterCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/MoviePosterCard.tsx`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/components/DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx`.

**(12) Final security/audit pass (Group L).** Confirmed `PublicUserSerializer`/`FollowEdgeSerializer` (item 3) are plain `Serializer`s with a hand-built field list, structurally incapable of an accidental `fields="__all__"` leak. Confirmed self-follow returns 400 and a private account is genuinely unenumerable — the same 404 fires for "user doesn't exist" and "user exists but is private," on search exclusion, `FollowToggleView`, `UserProfileDetailView`, and both connections list views alike, verified by dedicated tests, not just code inspection. Confirmed the username-uniqueness race (item 7) and closed it. Confirmed the release-date gates (item 1) are enforced server-side, not merely client-side UX. Found and **reported, not fixed**: `backend/.env.prod`'s `CELERY_BROKER_URL`/`CELERY_RESULT_BACKEND`/`REDIS_CACHE_URL` values are literally malformed — each one's value is the literal text `REDIS_URL="rediss://..."` (the wrapper prefix/suffix baked into the value itself, not stripped), and all three are byte-for-byte identical despite an adjacent comment insisting `REDIS_CACHE_URL` must use "a different db index" than Celery's. This file is untracked from git (confirmed not present in `HEAD`; `.gitignore`'s own comment explains real secrets were previously committed and untracked 2026-07-21, with the note that committed-history secrets remain live until rotated by hand) and is explicitly a local deploy template per its own header comment — whether Render's actual live dashboard config carries the identical bug, or was already hand-corrected independently of this file, could not be verified from this environment, so per the plan's own explicit instruction this was surfaced to the user rather than silently "fixed" against an unverified assumption about production. Files: none changed (audit-only item); `.env.prod` finding documented in `AUDIT.md`'s Phase 74 entry and `ROADMAP.md`'s Remaining/Future Work table instead.

**Batch-wide verification:** `manage.py check` clean, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean after applying both `0015` and `0016`, `pytest` 148/148 inside the `watchtracker_backend` container (up from 118 — 30 new tests: 15 in `test_social_views.py` (new file), 10 in `test_analytics_views.py` (heatmap intensity/range=all + movies/completion/monthly-summary), rest split across `test_reviews.py`'s half-star expansion and existing-suite updates for the movie-release-date gating fixtures), `tsc --noEmit` (`node --stack-size=8000`) zero errors, checked repeatedly after every group of changes rather than once at the end. **Not verifiable this session: no physical device or emulator** — the half-star SVG clip technique, both detail screens' light-theme hero-overlap fix, the year-chip heatmap's scroll/tap feel, the 3-tab avatar picker's layout, and every new social screen's on-device animation/gesture feel all need a real device pass; verified instead via the full backend test suite, `tsc`, and direct code-path tracing, per this repo's standing documented limitation.

# Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-31 (Phase 73 — user-reported bundle: Upcoming widgets' countdown restored (release-date-only label from Phase 72 reverted per follow-up feedback) plus a genuinely new real-time-of-day line sourced from TVmaze (TMDB has none), "For You" recommendations split by media type so the Series/Movies tabs stop sharing one mixed feed, three real push-notification bugs found and fixed (a foreground-notification suppression regression, a fresh-login token-registration gap, and a "new episode" alert condition that could essentially never fire), plus a broader bug sweep that found and fixed a fourth real bug (Cascade Catch-Up's `bulk_create` silently skipping badges/streak/cache-invalidation). Full detail below.

**(1) Upcoming widgets: countdown restored, plus a genuine local release *time* — not just a date — sourced from TVmaze.** Phase 72 replaced the widgets' countdown subtitle with a date-only label per a reference screenshot; user follow-up (this phase, with two more screenshots — the in-app Upcoming tab's own countdown+day-badge layout, and TV Time's widget showing an actual clock time like "6:30 AM") asked for the countdown to come back **and** a real release time added alongside it, matching the old design plus the new addition — not a replacement. Investigated why no prior phase had ever shown a clock time: TMDB's episode payload carries `air_date` only, a bare calendar date with zero time-of-day — this is why the very first countdown implementation always computed against local midnight and every duration ended in "00m", a ceiling baked into the data source, not a formatting bug. Asked the user how to source a real time; chose TVmaze (free, no API key) over date-only or no-time options. New `backend/core/airtime.py`: `fetch_air_time()` looks a show up on TVmaze by external id (`/lookup/shows?thetvdb=`/`?imdb=`) and returns `(wall-clock time, IANA timezone)` from `schedule.time` + `network.country.timezone`/`webChannel.country.timezone` — stored as a pair, not a resolved instant, since the correct UTC offset for a given episode depends on that episode's own date across a DST boundary. New `CachedShow.airs_time`/`airs_timezone`/`airtime_checked_at` (migration `0014`), refreshed from `refresh_show_cache` (the existing 6-hourly per-show Celery sweep) via `sync_show_air_time()`, self-throttled to roughly one lookup per show per month via the `airtime_checked_at` gate — deliberately not on any user request path, since this backend was already found overloaded once this session (Phase 72). Client: new `TMDBService.get_external_ids()` (cached 30 days, an external-id mapping is permanent) supplies the tvdb/imdb id TVmaze needs; `CachedShowSerializer` gained `airs_time`/`airs_timezone`; `Show`/`UpcomingItem`/`WidgetUpcomingItem` all carry the pair through. New `lib/dateFormat.ts::airDateTimeInstant()`/`formatLocalAirTime()` resolve the wall-clock+zone pair into an actual instant and then the device's own local clock time — DST-tested directly (`__tests__/dateFormat.test.ts`: the same 9:00 PM US-Eastern slot resolves to a different UTC instant in January vs July). Critical architectural point, addressing the user's explicit "sync correctly even off the app" instruction: **nothing time-relative is precomputed into the widget payload anymore** — `WidgetPayload.syncedAt`'s own docstring now explains why (a home-screen widget redraws on its own OS-driven schedule, routinely with the app not running, so a duration baked in at sync time is wrong by however long ago that sync happened). Both widget files (`widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`) compute the countdown and the local air time fresh at render, from raw `air_date`/`airs_time`/`airs_timezone` carried in the payload — restoring the original countdown text exactly (`formatCountdown`, unchanged) and adding the resolved air time as a second small line under the day badge, present only when the show has a known slot. Files: `backend/core/airtime.py` (new), `backend/core/models.py`, `backend/core/migrations/0014_...` (new), `backend/core/serializers.py`, `backend/core/services.py`, `backend/core/tasks.py`, `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/upcoming.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/widgetPayload.ts`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`. Verified: `pytest` (new `test_airtime.py` — 14 tests covering every TVmaze response branch — plus 4 new `refresh_show_cache` tests in `test_tasks.py`), `tsc --noEmit` zero errors, `jest` 7/7 (4 new DST/null-slot tests). **Not verifiable this session: no device** — same standing limitation as every prior widget phase.

**(2) "For You" recommendations mixed tv and movie candidates into one shared pool — the Series tab and Movies tab showed the identical list.** User reported: switching Discover Hub's segment toggle between Series and Movies didn't change the "For You" row at all. Root cause, confirmed by reading `recommendations_views.py`: `ForYouRecommendationsView` already computed `show_seeds`/`movie_seeds` separately (for good reason — a watched-shows signal shouldn't seed a movie recommendation) but then merged both seeds' candidate results into one `candidates` dict keyed `(media_type, tmdb_id)`, ranked together, and capped at one shared `RESULT_LIMIT=20` — so the single merged, cached (`recommendations_cache_key(user_id)`, no media-type component) response was identical regardless of which segment the client requested it for, and a user with a heavier show-watching history could see their movie recommendations crowded out entirely by show results, or vice versa. Fixed by splitting the endpoint on a required `?type=tv|movie` query param (mirroring `DiscoverFeedView`'s own existing `type` param): `_build_show_recommendations()`/`_build_movie_recommendations()` are now fully independent — separate seeds, separate candidate pools, separate `RESULT_LIMIT=20` each (doubled from the pre-split 20-shared-between-both), separate cache key (`recommendations_cache_key(user_id, media_type)`). Client: `discoverStore.ts`'s `forYou`/`hasFetchedForYou` became `Record<ActiveSegment, ...>` instead of a flat array/boolean, `fetchForYou(segment)` now takes the active segment and is called both on initial mount (`'tv'`) and from `setActiveSegment()` on every segment switch (same cache-once-per-segment guard `fetchFeed` already uses); `discover.tsx`'s render reads `forYou[activeSegment]`. Files: `backend/core/recommendations_views.py`, `backend/core/cache_keys.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_recommendations_views.py`, `client-mobile/store/discoverStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/discover.tsx`. Verified: `pytest` (2 new tests confirming a show-only watcher gets zero movie recommendations and vice versa, rest of the existing suite updated for the now-required `type` param), `tsc --noEmit` zero errors.

**(3) Push notifications weren't arriving — three independent, confirmed root causes, not one.** User reported push notifications simply not coming. Investigated each layer rather than guessing at one fix: (a) **`refresh_show_cache`'s "new episode" alert condition could essentially never fire.** It computed `existing_ids` (episodes already cached) *before* re-fetching the show's seasons, then alerted on any episode with `air_date == today` not in that pre-fetch set — but TMDB publishes episode rows weeks ahead of air date and `sync_active_shows` re-caches every `RETURNING` show every 6 hours, so by the time an episode's air date actually arrived it had *always* already been cached on some earlier sweep, permanently excluding it from its own alert. Fixed by gating on a new `CachedEpisode.notified_at` timestamp instead (migration `0014`, same migration as item 1's airtime fields) — set exactly once, before dispatch (so a concurrent/retried run of the same task can't double-alert), independent of how long the episode row had existed. (b) **A fresh login never registered a push token at all.** `_layout.tsx`'s push-token registration only runs from its cold-start "was there already a token in SecureStore" effect — but `login.tsx`/`register.tsx`/`forgot-password.tsx` all route through `app/loading.tsx` on every successful auth, and that screen never registered a token. A user's very first login on a fresh install silently never got a push token until they happened to force-quit and cold-relaunch the app while still signed in. Fixed by adding the same registration call (best-effort, fire-and-forget, matching `_layout.tsx`'s own pattern) to `loading.tsx`'s boot sequence. (c) **Foreground notifications were being silently suppressed.** `lib/notifications.ts`'s `setNotificationHandler` only set the now-deprecated `shouldShowAlert: true` — the installed `expo-notifications` version's actual `NotificationBehavior` type requires `shouldShowBanner`/`shouldShowList` (non-optional) for foreground presentation; without them, a notification arriving while the app was open was received but never displayed, reading exactly like "push notifications don't come" to anyone testing with the app foregrounded.
