Fixed by adding both fields alongside the deprecated one. Files: `backend/core/tasks.py`, `backend/core/models.py`, `client-mobile/app/loading.tsx`, `client-mobile/lib/notifications.ts`. Verified: `pytest` (2 new `refresh_show_cache` notification tests — fires once on the episode's air date, never re-fires on a later same-day sweep), `tsc --noEmit` zero errors.

**(4) Broad bug sweep (user-requested: "test for any more bugs here and there, find and fix them") — one more real, confirmed bug found.** Spawned a dedicated review pass across `views.py`/`signals.py`/`tasks.py`/`services.py`/`models.py` and the frontend stores, independent of items 1-3 above. Found: `BulkWatchStateToggleView` (the Cascade Catch-Up / "Mark Season Watched" endpoint)'s `watched=True` branch uses `WatchState.objects.bulk_create()` for its write — and Django never fires `post_save` for `bulk_create`, a fact `run_tvtime_import` already explicitly works around (calling badge/streak recalculation and busting the watchlist cache itself right after its own `bulk_create`) but which this endpoint did none of. Concretely: marking a whole season watched at once through Cascade Catch-Up earned no badges (`newly_earned_badges` in the response was always `[]` even for a genuine milestone), never counted toward that day's watch streak, and kept serving a stale `watchlist_all` response cache for up to `CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`. Fixed by adding the identical `recalculate_user_badges`/`recalculate_watch_streak` calls plus an `on_commit` cache bust that `run_tvtime_import` already does for its own `bulk_create`, correctly placed inside the same DB transaction so the response's post-refresh `profile.refresh_from_db()` picks up the newly-earned badges. Files: `backend/core/views.py`. Verified: `pytest` (4 new tests — cache invalidation, badge award + surfaced in response, watch-streak creation, all previously silently broken for this one endpoint).

**Batch-wide verification:** `manage.py check` clean, `makemigrations --check` clean after applying `0014`, `pytest` 118/118 inside the `watchtracker_backend` container (up from 93 — 25 new tests: 14 `test_airtime.py` (new file), 4 new in `test_tasks.py`, 4 new in `test_recommendations_views.py`, 2 in `test_bulk_watch_state_toggle.py` (new file), 1 new in `test_watchlist_cache.py`), `tsc --noEmit` (`node --stack-size=8000`) zero errors, `jest` 7/7 (up from 3 — 4 new DST/air-time tests). **Not verifiable this session: no physical device** — the widget's on-screen countdown+time layout and real push delivery both need a real EAS/dev-client build to confirm end-to-end; every fix was verified against live Postgres/Redis (Docker containers) and direct DST-crossing instant math instead.

# Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-31 (Phase 72 — user-reported bundle: TV Time import reliability/efficiency overhaul (cached external-id resolution, bulk episode upsert, ImportJob payload fingerprint, a beat task that self-heals a stalled import, progress-based client polling instead of a fixed wall-clock ceiling), Shows Hub response-payload slimming + gzip to fix free-tier "Instance failed"/502s during a big import, Celery/gunicorn free-tier hardening (acks_late, prefetch=1, max_tasks_per_child), and the Upcoming widgets' subtitle switched from a relative countdown to an absolute release-date label. Full detail below.

**(1) TV Time import: slow, failure-prone, and re-imports redid all the work from scratch.** User reported a friend's 466-show import repeatedly stalling/failing (Render screenshots: a job stuck at `RUNNING 69/466` for a day; `Import Failed — Request failed with status code 502`; "network error" on retry), and even a `SUCCESS` job's shows never showing up in Profile's "My Shows"/Shows Hub. Traced the actual cost of one import chunk rather than assuming the chunking itself (Phase B) was the problem: `TMDBService.get_season_episodes()` wrote each episode via `update_or_create()` — a SELECT + UPDATE/INSERT *per episode*, 40+ queries for a 20-episode season, multiplied across every season of every show in the export. `TMDBService.find_by_external_id()` — the tvdb_id/imdb_id → TMDB id lookup `_resolve_show_tmdb_id`/`_resolve_movie_tmdb_id` call for **every single item**, on **every single submission including a retry of an already-imported library** — was never passed `use_cache=True` at all, so a retried 400+ show import re-hit live TMDB 400+ times before `run_tvtime_import`'s own diff-against-already-imported logic ever got a chance to skip anything. Fixed both at the root: `get_season_episodes()` now does one `bulk_create(..., update_conflicts=True, unique_fields=["tmdb_id"])` per season instead of N `update_or_create()` calls; `find_by_external_id()` now caches for 30 days (a show's external ids are permanent) via the existing Redis-backed `_request(use_cache=True)` path every other TMDB lookup already uses. Separately, `TVTimeImportView.post()`'s resume-match check was calling `_sorted_import_payload()` (a full sort) on **both** the incoming submission and the stored `FAILED` job's multi-MB `payload` JSONField, deep-comparing the two, inside the request/response cycle — on every single retry. New `ImportJob.payload_fingerprint` (migration `0013`, sha256 of the sorted payload computed once at job creation) turns that into a cheap string-equality DB filter; `run_tvtime_import`'s own terminal `job.save()` was also a bare (non-`update_fields`) save, meaning the `FAILED` path re-serialized and rewrote that same untouched multi-MB `payload` back to Postgres on every single failure — exactly the path already under DB/worker contention. Fixed with explicit `update_fields`, only including `payload` when a run actually cleared it (`SUCCESS`). Root cause of the "stuck at 69/466 forever" symptom: `run_tvtime_import` re-enqueues itself per chunk (`apply_async(countdown=1)`) — a single point of failure if the container dies at just the wrong instant or the broker message is lost, and nothing was left to notice. New `core.tasks.resume_stalled_imports` (Celery beat, every 5 min) re-enqueues any `PENDING`/`RUNNING` `ImportJob` whose `updated_at` (touched after every single item, not just per chunk) has gone quiet for 6+ minutes — safe to re-enqueue a job that turns out to still be alive, since every write in the import path is idempotent by construction. Also raised `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_MEMORY_SIZE` from Django's 2.5MB default to 25MB (a several-hundred-show export's JSON body can exceed the default outright, failing before the view even runs) and made `CELERY_TASK_ACKS_LATE=True` / `CELERY_WORKER_PREFETCH_MULTIPLIER=1` / `CELERY_WORKER_MAX_TASKS_PER_CHILD=40` (render-start.sh runs gunicorn + this worker + beat in one free-tier container, so an unacked task lost to a mid-import restart, or unbounded prefetch queuing several users' work behind one big import, were both real failure modes there). Client-side, `pollImportJob()` (`lib/migration.ts`) gave up on a healthy, still-succeeding import after a fixed ~20 minutes of wall-clock time — exactly wrong for the biggest libraries, which take the longest and are exactly where the old inefficiency hurt most. Rewritten around progress, not elapsed time: it now only gives up after `processed` stops advancing for 10 straight minutes (comfortably past `resume_stalled_imports`' own 6-minute self-heal window), with a generous 2-hour absolute backstop against a truly pathological case. Files: `backend/core/services.py`, `backend/core/tasks.py`, `backend/core/views.py`, `backend/core/models.py`, `backend/core/migrations/0013_importjob_payload_fingerprint.py` (new), `backend/config/settings/base.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_tvtime_import.py`, `client-mobile/lib/migration.ts`. Verified: `manage.py check` clean, `pytest` 93/93 (inside the `watchtracker_backend` container), `tsc --noEmit` zero errors, `jest` 3/3. **Not independently confirmed this session: no access to the actual reported account's live import** — the specific "My Shows still showed 1" snapshot was very plausibly just this same job caught mid-run (screenshot shows `RUNNING 69/466`, a job now understood to be exactly the self-healing target of item above), not a separate caching bug — `signals.py`'s `Watchlist` post_save→cache-bust and the 25s response-cache TTL were both re-read and found already correct, so no separate fix was made there.

**(2) Shows Hub (`GET /watchlist/?page_size=all`) was the single heaviest endpoint in the app — likely contributor to the reported "Instance failed"/502 events during a big import.** This endpoint is what My Shows, the widget sync, and Profile's counts all read from, and it fully re-serializes on every fetch. For a 400+ show / 12,000+ episode library it was instantiating ~25,000 Django `CachedEpisode` model objects (via a nested `Prefetch("show__episodes", ...prefetch_related(watch_state_prefetch))`) plus a matching ~25,000 DRF serializer instances, to return episode fields (`overview`, `still_path`) nothing on this response path actually reads — `EpisodeRow.tsx`/`app/episode/[id].tsx` only ever render those from the dedicated season/episode endpoints, confirmed via grep before removing them. Replaced with two flat `.values_list()` queries (all episodes for the response's shows, all this-user's watched episode ids) building plain dicts client code already treats identically, via a new `LEAN_EPISODE_FIELDS` tuple and `CachedShowSerializer.get_episodes()` context override (`episodes_by_show`) — only `WatchlistView` opts in; `ShowDetailView`/`SeasonEpisodesView`/`ContinueWatchingView` etc. are untouched and still serve the full nested shape. Also added `GZipMiddleware` (every response here is JSON; BREACH doesn't apply — JWT-in-header auth, no reflected secret-next-to-attacker-input pattern anywhere in this API). Client: `Episode.overview`/`Episode.still_path` (`store/watchStore.ts`) are now optional, matching the shape actually returned by this one endpoint — both existing consumers already used `||`/ternary fallbacks, so this is a type-only change with no runtime behavior difference. Files: `backend/core/serializers.py`, `backend/core/views.py`, `backend/config/settings/base.py`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`. Verified: `pytest` 93/93 including `test_watchlist_cache.py`, `tsc --noEmit` zero errors.

**(3) Upcoming widgets: countdown subtitle replaced with an absolute release-date label, per user-supplied reference screenshot of a competing app's widget.** The per-row subtitle (`widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`) showed a relative duration string ("3d 22h 55m (Tuesday)") next to the episode code — redundant with the big right-aligned day-count badge (`formatDayBadge`, unchanged) already on the same row, and harder to scan than the plain date the reference image showed. New `formatReleaseLabel()` (`lib/dateFormat.ts`) returns `TODAY`/`TOMORROW`/`"MON, AUG 3"` — date-only, since TMDB's `air_date` carries no time-of-day for a TV episode, so there's no real release *time* to show beyond the day itself. `WidgetUpcomingItem.countdown`/`UpcomingWidgetShow.countdown` renamed to `releaseLabel` (optional, so a stale cached snapshot from an older build degrades to the widget's own date fallback rather than rendering `undefined`) across `lib/widgetPayload.ts` and both platforms' widget components; the in-app Upcoming tab's own countdown (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, `formatCountdown`) is untouched — the user's ask and reference image were specifically about the widget. Files: `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/widgetPayload.ts`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts` (comment only). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero errors, `jest` 3/3. **Not verifiable this session: no device** — same standing limitation as every prior widget-touching phase; the visual result needs a real EAS/dev-client build.

**Batch-wide verification:** `manage.py check` clean, `makemigrations --check` clean after applying `0013`, `pytest` 93/93 (inside the `watchtracker_backend` container, not the host venv — see Phase 69/70's note on the stray native Postgres on port 5432), `tsc --noEmit` (`node --stack-size=8000`) zero errors, `jest` 3/3. **Not verifiable this session: no physical device** — the widget subtitle change and the felt speed/reliability of a real large-library import both need a real build and a real large TV Time export to confirm end-to-end; the backend-side fixes (query counts, cache behavior, migration) were verified directly against a live Postgres/Redis via the Docker containers instead.

# Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-28 (Phase 69 — user-reported bundle: Upcoming's Overdue section rebuilt as recency-bounded LAST WEEK/LAST MONTH, home-screen widgets rebuilt (headless render fix + multi-item day grouping + flat black redesign + resize-aware row budget), Render cold-start UX (new `/health/` endpoint + client warmup/retry + real loading-screen error state), detail-screen action-button busy states, My Movies "Last Watched" real fix, Discover filter sheet Trending-highlight + scroll padding, Watchlist filter sheet opacity, new Trailer + Share features, and a TMDB session-reuse perf fix. Full detail below; see AUDIT.md's Phase 69 entry for the complete verification log.

**(1) Upcoming tab's Overdue section was unbounded, not recency-gated.** User screenshots showed today's app surfacing an OVERDUE(2) section for episodes from **6506 and 3173 days ago** — the exact backlog-dump failure Phase 54 (2026-07-23) had already fixed once via a 14-day `OVERDUE_RECENCY_MS` gate tied to `watched_recently || aired_recently`. Reading `lib/upcoming.ts` fresh (not trusting Phase 54's "Fixed" claim) found the actual gap: that gate only asked "is this show *currently* active" — a show binge-watched THIS week can still have a single ancient unwatched episode buried in its history (e.g. a season 1 episode skipped years ago) that satisfies `watchedRecently` even though the episode itself is nowhere near recent, which is exactly what leaked the 6506/3173-day items through. Root-cause fix: dropped the "is the show active" framing entirely — a missed episode only qualifies now if **the episode's own air date** is within a rolling window (`PAST_WINDOW_DAYS = 30`, `lib/dateFormat.ts`), full stop. Filtering by window *before* picking the earliest match (not after) also fixes a latent second bug the old code would have hit: a long-neglected show's genuinely-recent miss would previously have been shadowed by an ancient one if the ancient one sorted earlier. UI: the reference screenshot the user supplied (image 3) showed a competing tracker's "LAST WEEK/LAST MONTH" bucket names instead of a flat "OVERDUE" pill — matched that: `formatUpcomingHeaderLabel()` now buckets a past air date into `LAST WEEK` (≤7d), `LAST MONTH` (≤30d), or `EARLIER` (dead code path today since the 30-day gate means nothing past 30 days is ever emitted, kept for if the window is ever widened later). `groupUpcomingItemsByDate()`'s header shape changed from an OVERDUE-only optional `count` to every header always carrying `count: number` plus a `collapsible: boolean` flag; `UpcomingSectionHeader` and the Shows Hub's per-label `expandedPastLabels` state (replacing the old single `overdueCollapsed` boolean) now support multiple independent collapsible past sections instead of hard-coding one label. Files: `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/upcoming.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors.

**(2) Home-screen widgets — three independent bugs, one redesign.** (a) **Never-synced widgets stayed dead forever, app open or not.** The Android headless task handler (`widgets/android/WidgetProvider.tsx`) called `requestWidgetUpdate({widgetName: ...})` — which broadcasts to *every* widget instance of that name, not the specific instance the OS's pending `WIDGET_UPDATE`/`WIDGET_RESIZED`/`WIDGET_ADDED` transaction was actually waiting on. Reading the native module's own `WidgetTaskHandlerProps` type (`node_modules/react-native-android-widget/lib/typescript/api/register-widget-task-handler.d.ts`) showed the correct API is the `props.renderWidget` callback passed into the handler itself, which fulfils that exact pending transaction — switched to it, and confirmed against `RNWidgetBackgroundTaskWorker.java` (a `HeadlessJsTaskWorker` with a 30s timeout) that skipping `renderWidget` for `WIDGET_CLICK`/`WIDGET_DELETED` (nothing to redraw — the click's own `OPEN_URI`/`OPEN_APP` clickAction already handles the interaction) can't hang or leak a pending task, since task completion is tied to the JS function returning, not to `renderWidget` being called. Also added a real data source of last resort: `readPersistedStore()` falls back to the Zustand `watchtracker-store` AsyncStorage blob (same persist key `watchStore.ts` already uses — confirmed via grep, not assumed) whenever the SharedPreferences snapshot is empty/never-written, so a widget added before the app has ever run `syncWidgetData()` shows the real watchlist immediately instead of a permanent "Open Glix to sync" card. New shared `client-mobile/lib/widgetPayload.ts::buildWidgetPayload()` is the one definition both the live in-app sync path and this headless fallback path now call, replacing ~60 duplicated lines that used to live only in `watchStore.ts`. (b) **Upcoming widget only ever showed one item, even with several shows releasing the same day.** Root cause: `syncWidgetData()`'s `upcoming` array itself already held every windowed item, but both platforms' widget components rendered only `shows[0]` (Android had no visible bug here, actually — re-checked; the real single-item complaint was iOS's `HeroRow` + at-most-one `CompactRow`, a hard ceiling of 2 regardless of family). Fixed: `widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx` now renders up to a family-sized row budget (`systemLarge`: 7, `systemMedium`: 3, `systemSmall`: 2 — `app.json`'s iOS Upcoming widget gained `systemLarge` support, the only family with real extra vertical room, a genuine capability gap before this) grouped by day via a new `DayLabel` sub-component (mirrors the in-app tab's day-grouping), not a hero+compact pair. Android's `UpcomingWidget.tsx` gained the same day-grouping (`DayHeader`) and a **measured-height row budget** (`Math.ceil((height - 34) / 58) + 3`, from `WidgetTaskHandlerProps.widgetInfo.height`) instead of a flat cap, so a resized-taller widget genuinely renders more rows. (c) **Design**: both platforms dropped the Phase 55 per-row "card" treatment (background fill + border + margins per row) for flat rows directly on the widget's own black ground — that boxed-in look plus the per-row `marginLeft/marginRight` inset was the literal cause of the "gaps down the right edge" the user's screenshot showed; the big right-aligned day-count badge (`formatDayBadge()`) is unchanged. (d) **Resize robustness**: `app.json`'s Android widget config gained a wider size envelope (`minWidth` 250dp→180dp, `maxResizeWidth` 400dp→500dp, `maxResizeHeight` 350dp→800dp, `targetCellHeight` 2→3) — the prior bounds are very plausibly why user-reported resizes "didn't work": the OS was clamping the requested size back down to the configured max before the widget ever got a chance to render taller. `_layout.tsx`'s existing background-flush `AppState` effect gained a matching on-mount flush, so even a cold start with the backend unreachable pushes the last known watchlist (rehydrated from persist by then) to the widget rather than leaving stale data untouched until the first successful sync. Files: `client-mobile/lib/widgetPayload.ts` (new), `client-mobile/widgets/android/WidgetProvider.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/WatchlistWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`, `client-mobile/app.json`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors; `app.json` JSON-parse-valid; native widget contract cross-checked directly against the installed library's Java source, not assumed. **Not verifiable this session: no device/emulator** — actual on-device resize behavior, multi-row rendering, and the headless fallback path all need a real native build to confirm visually; these changes require a fresh EAS/dev-client build to reach a device at all (the widget native config changed).

**(3) Render free-tier cold-start: "spinner forever, then a permanent 'Can't reach Glix' banner."** Traced the actual boot path (`app/loading.tsx`) rather than assuming the bug was in the error-message text itself (already fixed for a different report back in Phase 21). Root cause, confirmed by reading the live code: `fetchProfile()`/`fetchWatchlist()` (`watchStore.ts`) both swallow their own request failures into `store.error` and **always resolve, never reject** — so `loading.tsx`'s `await Promise.all([...]); setReady(true);` fired unconditionally regardless of whether either call actually succeeded, and a Render free-tier dyno's 20-50s cold boot routinely exceeds `lib/api.ts`'s per-request budget (15s timeout × up to 2 retries), so on a cold start both calls would legitimately fail, `ready` still flipped true, and the user landed straight in the app already showing the permanent error banner with no retry affordance anywhere. Fixed with a dedicated boot-time wake sequence rather than stretching the general-purpose retry budget to also cover a multi-second dyno boot: new backend `GET /api/v1/health/` (`HealthCheckView` — `AllowAny`, no authentication/throttle classes, since its whole job is being hit repeatedly and cheaply by a client that isn't logged in yet and might not even have a live session) and a new `client-mobile/lib/warmup.ts` (`pingHealth()`, `waitForBackend()` — polls the health endpoint every 2.5s up to a 55s budget, cheap enough not to fight the free tier's own throttle). `app/_layout.tsx` fires one unawaited `pingHealth()` the instant the JS bundle evaluates (module scope, before `SplashScreen.preventAutoHideAsync()` even), overlapping with font load and the local SecureStore auth check to buy back real wake time before `loading.tsx` starts its own poll. `loading.tsx` rewritten around a real `'connecting' | 'waking' | 'error'` state machine: shows a friendly "Waking up the server — this can take up to a minute" line (new `AnimatedSplash` `statusText` prop, only rendered when non-empty, so the common warm-backend path is completely unaffected) once the wait crosses ~3s, and — the actual fix for the reported bug — checks `useWatchStore.getState().error` after the fetch `Promise.all` resolves and only sets `ready` if it's still falsy, surfacing a real `PressableScale` "Retry" button (matching the app's tactile-feedback convention, not a raw `Pressable`) instead of silently proceeding into a broken-looking app. Also fixed the underlying root cause a layer deeper for repeat offenders: `TMDBService.__init__` was building a brand-new `requests.Session` + `HTTPAdapter` on every single `TMDBService()` instantiation (24+ call sites across `views.py`/`search_views.py`/`tasks.py`) — meaning every TMDB call paid a full TCP+TLS handshake instead of reusing a warm connection, real added latency on every request, worst on pages firing several TMDB calls in parallel. Moved to a lazily-built, process-level shared `requests.Session` (`_get_shared_tmdb_session()`, `pool_maxsize=20` given the existing `ThreadPoolExecutor`-parallelized fetches in `DiscoverFeedView`/detail screens) — live-verified via Django shell that two separate `TMDBService()` instances now share the identical session object, and that a live TMDB call still round-trips correctly (including watching urllib3's own retry logic self-heal a genuine stale-pooled-connection `SSLEOFError` mid-session, recovering in ~1s total for two sequential calls — expected, standard behavior for connection pooling, not a regression). Files: `backend/core/views.py` (new `HealthCheckView`), `backend/core/urls.py`, `backend/core/services.py` (shared session), `client-mobile/lib/warmup.ts` (new), `client-mobile/app/loading.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/AnimatedSplash.tsx`. Verified: `manage.py check` clean, `pytest` 65/65 (run inside the `watchtracker_backend` container — the host venv's local Postgres actually pointed at a stray native Windows `postgres.exe` service squatting on port 5432 alongside Docker's own mapped port, a pre-existing machine-level environment quirk unrelated to this session's changes, worked around by running verification inside the container where `POSTGRES_HOST=db` resolves correctly), live `GET /health/` returned `200 {"status":"ok"}` from inside the container, `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors.

**(4) Show/movie detail action buttons (favorite, archive, remove, add/mark-watched) — investigated, found correctly wired; the real gap was zero visual feedback during a slow request.** User's screenshots plus item 3's own cold-start finding made the actual mechanism clear before touching any code: `show/[id].tsx`'s `handleToggleFavorite`/`handleToggleArchive` (POST `/watchlist/favorite/`, `/watchlist/archive/`) and `movie/[id].tsx`'s `handleRemoveFromWatchlist`/`handlePrimaryAction` (backed by `watchStore.ts`'s `removeMovieFromWatchlist`/`addMovieToWatchlist`/`toggleMovieWatchState`) were all traced end-to-end against their backend views (`FavoriteToggleView`/`ArchiveToggleView` in `views.py`) and found genuinely correct — same conclusion Phase 66 already reached for the movie-side buttons specifically, independently re-confirmed here for the show-side ones too. The actual bug: every one of these buttons uses RN `Pressable`'s `disabled` prop to block re-entrant taps while a request is in flight, but `disabled` alone gives **zero visual change** (no opacity shift, no spinner — `PressableScale`'s `style` prop here is a static array, not a function reacting to press/disabled state), so a tap that was genuinely working — just waiting out a slow/cold backend per item 3 — looked pixel-identical to a dead button for however long the request took. Fixed by giving every one of these buttons a real busy state: the icon swaps to a small `ActivityIndicator` and the button dims (new `iconBtnBusy`/`iconButtonBusy` style, `opacity: 0.55`) for the exact duration its own `isRemoving`/`isTogglingWatch`/`isAddingToWatchlist`/`isTogglingArchive`/`isTogglingFavorite` flag is true — no store/API logic touched, since none of it was actually broken. Also added the missing `disabled` prop to `movie/[id].tsx`'s header bookmark icon button (the icon-row version, not the labeled pill below it) for consistency with every other action button in the app, even though its internal early-return guards already made re-entrant taps a no-op either way. Files: `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors. **Lesson reinforced:** a "button doesn't work" report can be entirely accurate about the user's experience while the underlying wiring is completely correct — the gap here was purely the absence of an in-flight affordance, compounded by the same cold-start latency item 3 fixed a layer deeper.

**(5) My Movies "Last Watched" tab showed every tracked movie, not just watched ones.** `app/profile/movies.tsx`'s `filtered` memo ternary only branched on `'WATCH_NEXT'` / `'WATCHED'`, falling through to `allItems` (the full watch_next+watched union) for every other value including `'LAST_WATCHED'` — the sort-by-`updated_at` step that followed only *reordered* that full list, it never narrowed it, so the tab read as "show me literally everything, sorted by recency" instead of "movies I've actually finished, most recent first." Fixed by routing `'LAST_WATCHED'` (alongside the existing `'WATCHED'` case) to `movieWatchlist.watched` before the recency sort applies. Deliberately left the Shows-side `app/profile/shows.tsx` equivalent untouched — its "Last Watched" already sorts the *full* entry list with never-watched shows sinking to the bottom rather than being excluded, a different and already-intentional design (a partially-watched show legitimately belongs in a watchlist sort, unlike a movie which is a single-session watched/unwatched fact) that the user's report never named as broken. Files: `client-mobile/app/profile/movies.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors.

**(6) Profile/Analytics stats sync — audited fresh, found already correct (Phases 60/65 hold), closed one previously-open doc question.** Re-read `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx` and `app/analytics.tsx` live rather than trusting the "Fixed" claims at face value, given this exact area had two prior "fixed but wasn't" corrections in this same batch's own history (Phase 65 for profile.tsx, Phase 60 for analytics.tsx). Both hold: `profile.tsx` still derives Months/Days/Hours/Mins straight from `total_time_watched` (not the stale `watched_days`/`watched_hours`/`watched_minutes` fields) and still runs `useFocusEffect(fetchProfile)`; `analytics.tsx` still runs its full `useFocusEffect` fetch bundle (dashboard/genres/heatmap/streak/completion/monthly recap). `totalShows`/`totalMovies` on the Profile Hub read directly from the reactive `watchlist`/`movieWatchlist` Zustand state (mutated in place by every toggle/add/remove action), so they stay live without needing their own refetch trigger — confirmed, not assumed. Closed a real open item from `PROJECT_STATUS.md`/this file's own Phase 60 note, which had flagged `achievements.tsx`/`year-review.tsx` as sharing "the identical mount-only fetch staleness" as a still-open gap: checked `app/_layout.tsx`'s `Stack.Screen` registrations and confirmed both are root-Stack routes (`app/achievements.tsx`, `app/year-review.tsx`), not nested under `(tabs)/` — unlike Profile/Analytics (bottom tabs React Navigation keeps mounted/frozen across tab switches), a Stack-pushed screen genuinely unmounts on back-navigation and remounts fresh on the next push, so its mount-only `useEffect` already refetches every single time the user opens it. The flagged concern doesn't apply; no code change needed, and the stale doc note is corrected here rather than left open indefinitely. Files: none changed (verification pass only). **Lesson:** not every "same bug class" flag from a prior phase survives contact with the actual navigator structure — check where a screen is mounted before assuming a staleness pattern applies.

**(7) Discover filter sheet — Trending pill "can't be toggled," reported again despite Phase 58/64's prior fixes.** Read `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` fresh rather than re-trusting either prior phase's diagnosis, per this batch's own established practice of re-verifying claimed-fixed items against live code. Found the scroll mechanics (`ScrollView` `style={{flex:1}}`, safe-area-aware `paddingBottom`, always-visible scrollbar) genuinely intact from Phase 58/64 — hardened anyway, since the user's screenshot showed content still reaching the very bottom edge with no headroom: bumped the tail padding floor from `48 + insets.bottom` to `96 + insets.bottom` (same change mirrored on `WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx` for consistency) as a defensive margin, on the theory that a stale installed build (not yet incorporating Phase 58/64's fixes at all) is at least as likely an explanation as a remaining code bug, given this is the third report of the same symptom against code that reads correctly both times it was checked. The Trending complaint had a real, findable cause though: Phase 64 deliberately suppressed Trending's own `active` highlight (`active={opt.key !== 'trending' && sortOrder === opt.key}`) on the theory that Trending is the sheet's neutral default and must never render as "selected, with no way to turn off." That reasoning produced the opposite of what a user expects from a row of otherwise-identical pills — tapping Trending genuinely worked (`setSortOrder` correctly resolved to `'trending'` either way), but it could never look selected, which is indistinguishable from "broken" to someone expecting normal single-select pill behavior. Reverted the suppression: `active={sortOrder === opt.key}` for every pill including Trending, so it highlights exactly when it's the current sort (true by default, which is simply accurate) — the tap handler itself needed no change. Files: `client-mobile/components/DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors.

**(8) My Shows/My Movies filter sheet — opacity bump for legibility.** `WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`'s sheet used a flat `backgroundColor: c.glassFill` (the locked `rgba(30,30,30,0.65)` dark-theme token) with no blur layer underneath at all, unlike `LiquidTabBar.tsx`'s two-layer `BlurView` + tint recipe used everywhere else a surface needs to stay legible over busy, image-heavy content (the tab bar sits over posters/backdrops the same way this sheet sits over a poster grid/list). Rather than inventing a one-off darker hex (an AI_RULES §2 violation — no new hue/opacity scale without going through the token system), added a new theme token, `glassFillStrong` (dark: `rgba(24,24,24,0.88)`, light: `rgba(251,251,248,0.96)`) to `lib/theme.ts`'s `ThemeColors`, and gave `WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`'s sheet the same `BlurView(intensity=90) + tint` layering the tab bar already uses, clipped to the sheet's rounded top corners via a new `sheetClip` style (iOS needs an explicit clip on each layer; Android auto-clips to its parent's `borderRadius`, already relied on elsewhere). `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` was left on the plain `glassFill` token — the user's complaint was scoped to My Shows/My Movies specifically, and Discover's sheet wasn't reported as having the same visibility issue. Files: `client-mobile/lib/theme.ts` (new `glassFillStrong` token, both themes), `client-mobile/components/WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors — confirmed no other file constructs a literal `ThemeColors` object that the new required field would break (only type-annotation usages elsewhere, via grep).

**(9) New features: YouTube trailers + native Share, on both show and movie detail.** User-suggested trailer feature, implemented server-side-first: `TMDBService.get_show_details()`/`get_movie_details()`'s existing `append_to_response` calls gained `,videos` (zero extra HTTP requests — TMDB returns the videos block in the same payload already being fetched for credits/providers), cached identically (`tmdb_show_videos_{id}`/`tmdb_movie_videos_{id}`, 12h TTL, matching the existing credits/providers convention exactly). New `TMDBService._pick_best_video()` ranks TMDB's often-noisy `videos.results` (trailers, teasers, clips, bloopers, multiple languages) down to one YouTube choice — official Trailer first, then any Trailer, then official Teaser, then any Teaser — and new `get_show_trailer()`/`get_movie_trailer()` reader methods mirror `get_show_credits()`'s cache-then-fallback-fetch pattern exactly, degrading to `None` on any TMDB failure rather than raising (same "don't fail the whole screen over one missing block" precedent as `EpisodeDetailView`'s credits handling). Wired into the existing `ShowDetailView`/`MovieDetailView` responses as a new `trailer_key` field (a plain YouTube video id or `null`) rather than a new endpoint, since both screens already fetch these exact views once per visit. Live-verified against real TMDB via the running container: House of the Dragon → `DotnJ7tTA34` ("Official Trailer"), Inception → `JE9z-gy4De4` ("Official New UK Trailer"). Frontend: new `components/TrailerButton.tsx` — a small pill (a dark circular play glyph + "Trailer" label, no new hue) that renders `null` when `trailer_key` is absent, opening `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v={key}` via `Linking.openURL` (expo-linking, already a direct dependency — no new one added; deliberately not an in-app WebView, since handing off to the real YouTube app is a better video-watching experience than reimplementing one). Placed beside the existing Add-to-Watchlist/Mark-as-Watched pill on both `show/[id].tsx` and `movie/[id].tsx` (new shared `actionRow` style wrapping both). Also added a plain-RN `Share` (native share sheet, zero new dependency) icon button on both screens' header action rows — a `Share2` icon matching its sibling icons' existing white-on-photo treatment exactly — sharing `"Check out {title} on Glix!"`. # Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-24 (Phase 67 — new feature: series-finished celebration, master fix prompt Batch 3. When a toggle/bulk-toggle action's own before/after episode counts cross incomplete→complete for a show whose `status === 'ENDED'` at that moment, a confetti burst + non-blocking banner now shows "Series Complete! You finished {title}." Detection is computed entirely inline inside `toggleWatchState()`'s and `bulkToggleWatchState()`'s existing optimistic `set()` callbacks in `store/watchStore.ts` — using data those functions already compute (before-count from the pre-mutation snapshot, after-count from the newly-built entry) — never from a render, mount, or refetch, which is what guarantees it can't retroactively fire for a show that was already 100% watched before this shipped and can't refire from simply reopening the show's screen. New store state `completedShow: CompletedShowInfo | null` / `clearCompletedShow()`, mirroring `unlockedBadges`'/`popUnlockedBadge`'s exact single-value-in-global-store pattern (not a queue — a show can only cross this threshold once, and both current bulk call sites, Catch-Up cascade and Mark Season Watched, are single-show). New `components/CompletionCelebration.tsx`: Reanimated-only confetti particles (no Lottie, no new dependency — same primitive as `BadgeUnlockModal`/`CascadeModal`/`AnimatedSplash`), `pointerEvents="none"` so they never intercept touches, plus a banner card using Snackbar's exact `pointerEvents="box-none"` + `setTimeout` auto-dismiss convention rather than a blocking full-screen `Modal` like `BadgeUnlockModal` — deliberately non-blocking so finishing a series doesn't trap the user on whatever screen they were on. Wired at the `app/_layout.tsx` root next to `BadgeUnlockModal`, since the triggering mark-watched action can originate from the Shows Hub row, the season screen, or the episode screen, not just one place. **Self-caught defect, fixed before shipping:** first draft's confetti palette used a literal `'#FFFFFF'` — would have been nearly invisible against the light theme's `#EDEEEA` background, since (unlike the app's other fixed-white "photo caption overlay" exceptions) there's no photo underneath a confetti layer, just the screen's own themed background; replaced with `c.textPrimary`. **Scope decision, not silently expanded:** the request was explicitly Shows-only (a movie's single "mark as watched" action already *is* "finishing" — no incomplete→complete transition exists to detect) — noted as a low-cost follow-up rather than built. Files: `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/components/CompletionCelebration.tsx` (new), `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero errors; manual trace of 4 scenarios (single-episode completion fires, bulk/season completion fires, un-marking never fires, a still-Continuing show reaching 100% never fires). # Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-24 (Phase 65 — Profile Hub's own watch-time stats real fix, master fix prompt Batch 3. Phase 60 (Batch 2) fixed `app/analytics.tsx`'s staleness but never touched `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx` — the actual Profile Hub tab, and the screen the user's "profile stats" report was really about. Two independent bugs found by reading the live code, not the changelog: (1) `watched_days`/`watched_hours`/`watched_minutes` are `SerializerMethodField`s on `backend/core/serializers.py` (`total_time_watched // 1440`, `(total_time_watched % 1440) // 60`, `total_time_watched % 60`) — every toggle/bulk-toggle/movie-toggle endpoint's response payload only ever returns `total_time_watched` itself (confirmed via grep across every response dict in `views.py`), never the three derived fields, so the Profile screen's Months/Days/Hours/Mins card silently froze at whatever the last full `fetchProfile()` returned and never moved again, no matter how many episodes got marked watched in between — even though `total_time_watched` itself was (correctly, per Phase 60's own accurate audit of that one field) staying fresh in the store the whole time. (2) `profile.tsx` — itself a bottom tab, which React Navigation keeps mounted/frozen on tab switch rather than unmounting — still had a mount-only `useEffect` fetching profile, the exact staleness class Phase 56 (Watch History) and Phase 60 (Analytics) already fixed on other screens. Fixed: replaced the three separate `useMemo`s reading the stale derived fields with direct arithmetic on `profile.total_time_watched` (mirroring the backend's own formula exactly) — a root-cause fix, since it means these values can never drift from `total_time_watched` again regardless of what any future mutation call site does or doesn't return, rather than a patch that would need repeating at every new call site. Replaced the mount-only `useEffect` with `useFocusEffect` (`@react-navigation/native`, already a dependency, same convention Phase 60 established), so a refocus after watching elsewhere refetches the full profile as defense-in-depth for anything the client-side derivation doesn't cover (badges, streak). Confirmed via grep that `watched_days`/`watched_hours`/`watched_minutes` aren't read anywhere else in the client — safe to stop consuming them on this one screen without touching the API contract. Confirmed `profile.tsx` doesn't render streak at all (Analytics' job, already covered), so the master prompt's streak-recheck item doesn't apply to this specific screen. Files: `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero errors (also removed the now-unused `useEffect` import in the same pass). Not verifiable this session: no device — the stats card updating instantly on a real toggle, `useFocusEffect` firing correctly on a real tab switch.)
# Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-24 (Phase 64 — Discover filter sheet real fix, master fix prompt Batch 3. Re-read `components/DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx`'s current live code from scratch per the batch's own instruction not to reuse Phase 58's diagnosis if it clearly didn't lead to a real fix — found Phase 58's `flex: 1` ScrollView fix and toggle-off `onPress` logic both genuinely present and structurally correct; the mechanical scroll/toggle code was never actually broken. Two gaps found that Phase 58's `tsc`-and-code-tracing-only verification (no device) couldn't have caught: (1) the ScrollView's `contentContainerStyle` used a flat `paddingBottom: 48` with no `useSafeAreaInsets().bottom` accounted for — on a device with a generous gesture-nav/home-indicator inset, the tail content (Reset button) could sit at or under it, reading as unreachable even though technically one more swipe away; compounded by a hidden scrollbar (`showsVerticalScrollIndicator={false}`) giving no visual cue more content existed below the fold. (2) The literal "Trending still can't be tapped off" complaint: `SORT_OPTIONS`' pills rendered `active={sortOrder === opt.key}` for every option including Trending — since `'trending'` is the sheet's own default/neutral state, this pill was highlighted-as-selected by default, reading as a filter permanently stuck on with no way to turn off, even though the toggle-off tap handler underneath it was already working correctly the whole time — the highlighting, not the handler, was the actual bug. Fixed: added `insets.bottom` on top of the existing 48px base padding, made the scrollbar always visible (`showsVerticalScrollIndicator` + Android `persistentScrollbar`), and changed the Sort pills' `active` prop to `opt.key !== 'trending' && sortOrder === opt.key` (Trending's tap behavior — `setSortOrder(sortOrder === opt.key ? 'trending' : opt.key)` — is completely unchanged and still works as the "clear sort" action). Applied the identical safe-area-padding + always-visible-scrollbar fix to `components/WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx` (Phase 63, same batch) so it doesn't inherit the same gap on day one. Confirmed `discoverStore.ts`'s `isFilterActive()`/`setSortOrder`/`fetchFilteredResults`/`loadMoreFilteredResults` completely untouched — pure UI-layer fix. Re-confirmed `LanguageFilterModal.tsx` is unaffected and was already correctly built on a real `Modal` + `SafeAreaView edges={['bottom']}`. Files: `client-mobile/components/DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero errors. # Last Updated (prior): 2026-07-24 (Phase 63 — My Shows/My Movies filter sheet full rework, master fix prompt Batch 3, reworking Phase 57 "for real" per screenshot evidence of it rendering inline/interleaved behind the list. Traced this to the exact opposite of what it looked like: `WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx` was already a structurally correct absolute-positioned overlay, built on the identical Reanimated backdrop/sheet primitive as the already-working `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` (confirmed by reading both files side by side, not assumed from the symptom). The actual bug was render **order** in the parent screens (`app/profile/shows.tsx`/`movies.tsx`): `<WatchlistFilterSheet>` was mounted *before* the screen's `<FlashList>` in JSX. React Native has no implicit z-index across siblings — paint order follows JSX order — so the FlashList, rendered after, painted directly over the sheet regardless of its `position: absolute`. `discover.tsx`'s own `<DiscoverFilterSheet>` is deliberately the very last child in its tree; that's the actual reason it worked and this one didn't, not a difference between the two sheet components themselves. Separately, this phase's original spec had been inverted: the status tabs (All/Continuing/Ended, etc.) had been moved *into* the sheet as a "Status" section, when the master fix prompt's intent was for them to stay a normal top-of-screen pill row outside the sheet. Fixed: moved `<WatchlistFilterSheet>` to render *after* the `<FlashList>`/empty-state block in both screens (last, alongside `<FloatingFilterButton>`), matching `discover.tsx`'s placement — no change to the sheet's own internals needed for this part, since it was never actually broken. Restored the status tabs to a top-of-screen horizontal pill row (new local `TabPill`, mirroring the Shows Hub's own pre-existing `FilterPill` convention exactly), removing the "Status"/"Sort" sections from `WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx` — the sheet now holds only Language + tag toggles (Anime for Shows; Anime + Animation for Movies), per the batch's explicit instruction. Folded "Last Watched" (added in the original Phase 57) into the same single-select tab row as a 4th option (Shows: All/Continuing/Last Watched/Ended; Movies: All/Watch Next/Watched/Last Watched) rather than a separate independent toggle that could desync from the tab selection — `lastWatchedSort` is now a `const` derived from `filter === 'LAST_WATCHED'`. `FloatingFilterButton.tsx` rebuilt bottom-centered (`useSafeAreaInsets`-aware) and always solid `c.accentFill`/`c.onAccent` (was right-pinned and only accent-colored when active before) — a small dot on `c.onAccent` now indicates "filters applied" instead of a full color swap. Confirmed the Language modal, Anime/Animation toggles, and the per-item "Up to Date" status badge all unaffected; confirmed the removed `FilterKey` type stays module-local on both screens, same as Phase 57's own original confirmation. Files: `client-mobile/components/WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`, `client-mobile/components/FloatingFilterButton.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/shows.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/movies.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` zero errors. Not verifiable this session: no device — the paint-order fix follows directly from React Native's documented stacking behavior, but the visual result is unconfirmed on a real screen.)

# Last Updated: 2026-07-24 (Phase 61 — Widget reliability (offline/empty) audit: audited every `syncWidgetData()` call site (`fetchWatchlist`, `toggleWatchState`, `bulkToggleWatchState`, `addShowToWatchlist`, `removeShowFromWatchlist` in `store/watchStore.ts`) and confirmed each only fires after its own API call actually succeeds — never on the optimistic-update or rollback paths — so a failed or offline mutation never pushes stale/incorrect data to the widget; no gap found there (master fix prompt Batch 2, Phase 61). Re-confirmed the existing offline-resilience path is genuinely sound: Android persists the last successful sync to `SharedPreferences`'s `widgetData` key, and `widgets/android/WidgetProvider.tsx`'s `widgetTaskHandler` independently re-reads it on every OS-triggered redraw (resize, `updatePeriodMillis` tick) with no network dependency; iOS relies on WidgetKit's own snapshot persistence via `updateSnapshot()`; `app/_layout.tsx`'s `AppState` background-flush effect and the `registerWidgetTaskHandler` wiring were both re-verified correct (guarded per-platform, wrapped in try/catch, registered at module scope). Real gap found: the widgets' "nothing to show" branch collapsed three genuinely different states into one identical message — never synced yet (fresh install/cleared storage: Android reads back `null` from `SharedPreferences`, iOS props are `undefined` before the first snapshot), explicitly logged out (`clearWidgetData()`, called from `app/settings.tsx` on sign-out, wrote an indistinguishable `{watchlist: [], upcoming: []}` — its own comment says the intent was purely privacy, "Otherwise the home-screen widgets keep showing this account's watchlist to whoever uses the device next," but the resulting copy then told the next person "your watchlist is empty" instead of prompting them to log in), and a genuinely-synced, genuinely-empty account. Fixed by adding a `loggedOut: true` marker to `clearWidgetData()`'s payload (both platforms) and branching all four widget files' empty-state copy on it: "Open Glix to sync your watchlist/shows." (never synced) / "Log in to see your watchlist/upcoming episodes." (logged out) / the original empty copy (genuinely empty), unchanged otherwise. Deliberately did not add an explicit retry mechanism to `syncWidgetData()`'s own try/catch (still a single best-effort attempt, logging and swallowing on failure) — the codebase already has three independent self-healing refresh paths (the next successful mutation's own `syncWidgetData()` call, Android's OS-driven periodic redraw re-reading the last-good cache, and the `AppState` background-flush effect), so a bespoke retry/backoff would have been an unneeded abstraction duplicating coverage that already exists. Files: `store/watchStore.ts`, `widgets/android/WatchlistWidget.tsx`, `widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `widgets/ios/WatchlistWidget.tsx`, `widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new. Not verifiable this session: no device — actual on-screen copy/behavior across a real logout, a real fresh install, and Android's periodic redraw picking up the new `loggedOut` flag.)
# Last Updated: 2026-07-24 (Phase 60 — audited every `total_time_watched` mutation path and the streak recalculation safety net for correctness, and traced the frontend's actual refresh triggers rather than assuming the existing fetch-on-mount calls were sufficient (master fix prompt Batch 2, Phase 60). Backend: confirmed every mutation site — single episode toggle, cascade bulk toggle, single movie toggle, remove-show-from-watchlist, remove-movie-from-watchlist, TV Time import completion — uses the same F()-expression atomic update with a consistent non-negative floor guard; found no gap. Frontend: confirmed `watchStore.ts`'s episode/movie toggle actions already do the correct optimistic-then-authoritative-reconcile dance on `state.profile.total_time_watched`, with rollback to the pre-optimistic snapshot on failure — no drift risk found there either. Found a real, previously-unaddressed gap: no admin tool existed to recover a profile whose `total_time_watched` has drifted from actual watch history (only `WatchStreak` had an idempotent Celery recalculation task as a safety net) — added a new Django admin action, "Recalculate total watch time from history", on `UserProfileAdmin` that re-sums every `WatchState`/`MovieWatchState` row's current runtime, documented as a drift-correction tool (not routine maintenance, since a title's runtime_minutes can shift on TMDB after it was originally credited) — sanity-checked the exact aggregation against the real dev database and it correctly surfaced genuine drift on an existing profile (8413 stored vs 8683 recomputed minutes), confirming both that the tool works and that this gap was real, not hypothetical. Also added a companion admin action on `WatchStreakAdmin` to manually queue the existing `recalculate_watch_streak` Celery task per user — re-verified that task's post-Phase-40 logic is still correct and idempotent, but confirmed it is not on `CELERY_BEAT_SCHEDULE` and only ever fires automatically after a TV Time import completes, so the new admin action is the only manual trigger available for the ordinary day-to-day case. Separately found and fixed a real staleness bug in `app/analytics.tsx`: its dashboard/streak/heatmap/genres/completion/monthly-recap fetches only ran once on mount, and because this screen's stack is frozen (not unmounted) when the user switches tabs, watching something on another tab and switching back to Profile left every one of these server-computed aggregates stale with no refetch trigger — the same staleness class Phase 56 fixed for the Watch History tab, but unlike `total_time_watched`/history there, these aggregates aren't cheap to patch from the mutation site, so the minimal correct fix was `useFocusEffect` (first use of this hook in the codebase; it's a standard `@react-navigation/native` hook, already a direct dependency, not a new one). Flagged, not fixed this phase: `achievements.tsx` and `year-review.tsx` have the identical mount-only fetch pattern and are one hop from Analytics under the same frozen tab stack — same latent bug class, out of this phase's bounded scope, worth a dedicated look later.)

# Last Updated: 2026-07-24 (Phase 58 — Discover's filter sheet, and as of Phase 57 this same batch the new My Shows/My Movies filter sheet, clipped their tail content once it exceeded the sheet's fixed height, with no way to scroll to it (master fix prompt Batch 2, Phase 58). Root cause: each sheet's `ScrollView` had no `style` (only `contentContainerStyle`), so it sized itself to its full content instead of the sheet's remaining space — on Android specifically, the sheet's own `borderRadius` implicitly clips anything overflowing its fixed-height bounds, silently eating the tail (Reset button, last genre/language pills) with no scroll affordance at all. Fixed both `DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx` and `WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx` with `style={{flex:1}}` on the ScrollView (both sheets use a real fixed pixel height from `Dimensions`, so flex:1 correctly claims the remaining space). `LanguageFilterModal.tsx` has the same class of bug but its sheet uses `maxHeight` (not a fixed height) so short lists still hug their content — used `flexShrink: 1` there instead, since `flex: 1` would have fought the parent's content-based sizing and collapsed it to zero height for short lists. Also gave every single-select filter pill (including Discover's "Trending" sort and the new Status pills in `WatchlistFilterSheet`) real toggle-off support — tapping the already-active pill now returns to the default/all state instead of being a no-op, matching genre/anime/language pills which already supported this. Regression audit: confirmed genre selection, language picker, anime toggle, and `fetchFilteredResults`/`loadMoreFilteredResults` pagination are all untouched and unaffected by these changes (traced end to end, not assumed). Files: `components/DiscoverFilterSheet.tsx`, `components/WatchlistFilterSheet.tsx`, `components/LanguageFilterModal.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — same 2-error baseline, zero new. Self-review: confirmed `setSortOrder`'s existing fetch-or-clear-to-default logic in `discoverStore.ts` is untouched by the toggle-off change — it already branches correctly on `isFilterActive()` either way. Not verifiable this session: no device — actual scroll-to-tail behavior with a long genre/language list, whether SHEET_HEIGHT (72%/62%) is generous enough on small screens even after the fix.)
# Last Updated: 2026-07-24 (Phase 56 — Watch History tab desynced from real watch activity, only ever refreshing on mount or manual pull-to-refresh (master fix prompt Batch 2, Phase 56). Read `store/watchStore.ts`'s `toggleWatchState()`/`bulkToggleWatchState()`/`fetchHistory()` and `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s mount `useEffect`/`handleRefresh` live first. **Root cause, confirmed by reading both functions, not assumed:** neither `toggleWatchState()` nor `bulkToggleWatchState()` (covering the season/episode screens, Shows Hub row checkmark, and the Catch-Up cascade — all funnel through these two) ever touched `history` state — they updated `watchlist`/`profile` optimistically and called `syncWidgetData()`, but the Watch History tab's `history` state was only ever populated by the mount-time `fetchHistory()` call and the pull-to-refresh handler. Marking (or unmarking) an episode watched from anywhere else in the app left Watch History stale until the user manually pulled to refresh. **Fix:** both functions now update `history` optimistically in the same `set()` call as the watchlist update — marking watched prepends a synthetic `HistoryEntry` (`id: `optimistic-${episode.tmdb_id}``, built from the already-updated episode + entry's show fields, `watched_at` shared with the same timestamp used for `last_watched_at`'s bump); unmarking filters out any entry matching that episode id. Both paths snapshot `previousHistory` and roll it back in the `catch` block alongside `previousWatchlist`/`previousProfile`, mirroring the existing revert-on-error convention exactly. `bulkToggleWatchState` collects the same data across every episode actually toggled (not just requested) via a `newHistoryEntries`/`unwatchedEpisodeIds` accumulator built during its existing per-entry map, then applies one history update after the loop. **Audit of every other Shows Hub tab, per the batch's own instruction:** traced `buildRows()`'s `allEntries = useMemo(() => getAllEntries(watchlist), [watchlist])` and `rows = useMemo(() => buildRows(allEntries, filter), [allEntries, filter])` (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx`) — WATCH NEXT / HAVEN'T WATCHED FOR A WHILE / HAVEN'T STARTED all derive purely from the `watchlist` state object, which both toggle functions already replace with a new reference on every optimistic update, so those three buckets were already recomputing correctly and immediately — Watch History was the only tab with its own separately-fetched state, hence the only one desynced. Confirmed no tab issues a full `fetchWatchlist()`/`fetchHistory()` refetch on toggle — both toggle functions only ever call `syncWidgetData()` (a local-state read, no network round trip of its own) after the POST resolves. **Files:** `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`. **Verified:** `node --stack-size=8000 tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new. Traced `useCatchupCascade((ids, watched) => bulkToggleWatchState(ids, watched))` (line ~499) confirming the Catch-Up cascade genuinely shares this same code path, not a fork. **Self-review:** reused the exact optimistic-first / snapshot-and-revert convention already established for `watchlist`/`profile` in both functions rather than inventing a new pattern; no new dependency; a full-refetch reconciliation on the next `fetchHistory()` (page 1) naturally replaces any optimistic entries with server-authoritative data, so no permanent drift risk from the synthetic ids. **Not verifiable this session:** no device/emulator — real-device timing of the optimistic Watch History update relative to a live pull-to-refresh race, and general on-screen feel of the new instant Watch History update.)

Read `store/watchStore.ts`'s `syncWidgetData()`, `widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, and `widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx` live first, per the batch's bootstrap instruction. **Root cause, confirmed by reading the live filter, not assumed:** `syncWidgetData()`'s upcoming filter was `item.airDate <= windowEndIso` only — a top-end bound with no bottom, so every Overdue item Phase 54 correctly kept in `buildUpcomingItems()`'s flat array (now recency-gated, but still present for the in-app collapsible section) sailed straight through into the widget's payload, since any past date trivially satisfies "<= a future date." The widget has no collapsible Overdue section to put them in, so the in-app fix alone didn't reach the widget. **Fix:** added an `item.airDate >= todayIso` lower bound so only strictly-future items reach the widget — Overdue items are excluded from the widget entirely rather than shown uncollapsed. The existing 14-day window and the `formatCountdown()`-based per-item countdown string (still shown in the subtitle line) were kept, not reinvented, per the batch's explicit instruction. Files: `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts` (new `formatDayBadge()`), `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts` (`syncWidgetData` filter fix), `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — same 2 pre-existing baseline errors (`watchStore.test.ts`, `HeroCarousel.tsx`), zero new; confirmed `react-native-android-widget`'s `RgbaColor` template type (`` rgba(${number}, ${number}, ${number}, ${number}) ``) matches the token strings used, and `@expo/ui`'s `Color` type (`string | ColorValue | NamedColor`) accepts arbitrary rgba strings, with `border`/`background`+`shapes.roundedRectangle` modifiers confirmed to exist for the card treatment; confirmed `widgetUri()`'s tap-through chain (episode → show → open-app) and both platforms' empty-state branch were untouched by the redesign. Self-review against the batch's own quality checklist: reused the existing 14-day window/recency convention and theme tokens rather than inventing new ones, no new dependency, both platforms updated identically with no forked logic, and the separate Watchlist ("NEXT UP") widget was deliberately left untouched since it was never in scope for this phase's flooding bug. # Last Updated: 2026-07-24 (Phase 54 — Upcoming tab flooded with backlog, not scoped to true upcoming/overdue (master fix prompt Batch 2, Phase 54). Read `lib/upcoming.ts`'s `buildUpcomingItems()`, `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s `buildRows()`/`UpcomingRow`/`renderUpcomingGridEntry`/`groupUpcomingItemsByDate`, and `lib/dateFormat.ts` live first, per the batch's own bootstrap instruction not to trust context.md's prior "Fixed" claim at face value — Phase 47 (2026-07-23, same day) added the aired-but-unwatched "overdue" item per show, and that fix's own gap (never bounded by recency) is what flooded here, not a regression from a later change. **Root cause, confirmed by reading the live function, not assumed:** `buildUpcomingItems()`'s overdue branch pushed a show's next unwatched-and-aired episode unconditionally — any show with any unwatched aired episode qualified, no matter how long ago it aired or was last touched, so a large imported library read as a wall of OVERDUE items, effectively the entire back-catalog rather than genuinely-behind shows. **Fix:** the overdue item now only surfaces when (a) the show has actually been started (`watched_episode_count > 0` — a never-started show belongs to HAVEN'T STARTED, not a second copy of it in Upcoming) and (b) the show counts as "actively behind," defined as either `last_watched_at` within the last 14 days or the missed episode's own `air_date` within the last 14 days — deliberately reusing `buildRows()`'s existing `INACTIVITY_MS = 14 days` recency convention (the same constant that already splits WATCH NEXT from HAVEN'T WATCHED FOR A WHILE) rather than inventing a second, arbitrary threshold; not ambiguous enough to stop and ask the user for a day count when the repo already has an established, documented answer to "what counts as actively behind" for this exact class of show. Genuinely future items (unaired, later `air_date`) are untouched — they were never the flooding source, since by definition they can't be stale backlog. **UI:** the OVERDUE section in the Upcoming tab (List and Grid, `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`) is now a collapsible "OVERDUE (N)" header — `lib/upcoming.ts`'s `groupUpcomingItemsByDate()` now tracks a running `count` on the header entry it emits for the `OVERDUE` label (every overdue item is guaranteed contiguous at the front of the airDate-sorted list, so one running counter is correct without a second pass); `UpcomingSectionHeader` renders that header as a tappable `PressableScale` with a chevron and red/`c.negative` accent specifically when it's the OVERDUE header (every other date header is unchanged, plain, non-interactive), defaulting **closed** (`overdueCollapsed` local state, default `true`) so a bounded catch-up section can never sit ahead of and swallow the real TODAY/TOMORROW/weekday sections the way an always-open flat list did; a new `visibleUpcomingEntries` memo hides the item rows between the OVERDUE header and the next header while collapsed, leaving the header itself always visible as the toggle affordance. The widget (`syncWidgetData()`) inherits this same bound automatically next phase (Phase 55) since it consumes the same unmodified `buildUpcomingItems()` — not touched this phase, kept to its own scope. Files: `client-mobile/lib/upcoming.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`. Verified: `node --stack-size=8000 tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline (`__tests__/watchStore.test.ts`, `HeroCarousel.tsx`), zero new. Manual trace of 4 scenarios: never-started show with a next episode aired 3 days ago → correctly excluded (fails `hasStarted`); started show last watched 20 days ago, missed episode aired 60 days ago → correctly excluded (both recency checks fail); started show last watched 5 days ago, missed episode aired 45 days ago → correctly included (`watchedRecently`); currently-airing show watched through last week, missed yesterday's episode → correctly included (`airedRecently`). Self-review against the batch's own quality checklist: reused `buildRows()`'s existing recency fields/threshold rather than a second definition, no new dependency, collapse state kept as local component state (nothing else needs to react to it, consistent with how `overdueCollapsed`'s sibling `upcomingView`/`activeTab` state is handled on this same screen). Not verifiable this session: no device/emulator — the real on-screen chevron/tap feel for the collapsible header, and whether "OVERDUE (N)" reads clearly against the red/negative accent in both light and dark themes.)

Found the real, fixable gap: `UniversalSearchView` and `DiscoverFilterView` both already returned `page`/`total_pages`/`total_results` (confirmed live — the backend pagination was already fully built), but `discoverStore.ts`'s `runSearch`/`fetchFilteredResults` only ever requested page 1 and replaced `searchResults`/`filteredResults` wholesale on every call — a frontend-only gap, not a TMDB result-count ceiling. Fixed: `discoverStore.ts` gained `searchPage`/`searchTotalPages`/`isLoadingMoreSearch`/`loadMoreSearchResults` and the equivalent `filtered*` set — `loadMore*` actions request the next page and append rather than replace, no-op past the last page or during an in-flight fetch. `discover.tsx` wired `onEndReached`/`onEndReachedThreshold={0.5}` on both `FlashList`s with a small shared `LoadMoreFooter` spinner row (not a full-screen loader — the existing grid stays on screen). (f) Audit: confirmed `DiscoverFeedView`'s `ThreadPoolExecutor` parallelization (Phase 37) is untouched by any of this phase's changes; confirmed every `discover_tv`/`discover_movies`/`get_trending` call this phase touched still passes `use_cache=True` (none of the new params required disabling caching — TMDB's own cache-key hashing in `_request()` already incorporates the full `params` dict, so a genre+language+anime combination naturally gets its own distinct cache entry, no cache-key collision risk introduced); manually traced genre+language, anime+sort, and trending-off+genre combinations against the actual param-building code, then wrote backend tests asserting the same compositions rather than trusting the trace alone. Phase 50 — Logout modal polish, login logo, "Akony" font branding (fix prompt's Phase J): three sub-items. (a) The logout confirmation modal read as generic default UI — traced it to `app/settings.tsx`'s `handleLogout`, which called the bare OS-native `Alert.alert('Log Out', ...)`, not a themed component at all — the actual generic-UI complaint. Built a new `components/LogoutConfirmModal.tsx` matching this app's established modal convention (read `CascadeModal.tsx`/`BadgeUnlockModal.tsx` live first for the exact pattern to match, not guessed): `c.glassFill`/`c.hairline` card with a `BlurView` (intensity 80), `rgba(0,0,0,0.6)` backdrop scrim — explicitly the value CascadeModal already uses, matching the fix prompt's own callout that this is the correct established convention versus the inconsistent `0.85` a past phase already caught and fixed elsewhere (note: `BadgeUnlockModal.tsx` itself actually uses `0.5`, a third value — left alone, out of this phase's scope, not silently "fixed" as if it were part of the ask). Confirm button uses `c.accentFill`/`c.onAccent` — Cinema Neon Yellow, per the fix prompt's explicit instruction — deliberately not the red `c.negative` treatment that would be the more obvious "destructive action" choice, since the fix prompt named the accent color specifically. `performLogout` (including its `clearWidgetData()` call) and the underlying `handleLogout` trigger point are otherwise untouched — this was a visual/UX swap only, exactly as scoped. (b) Login screen: added the real `assets/Glix.png` (1.3MB, confirmed still the genuine asset from the Phase 30 rebrand/app-icon work, not regenerated) above the existing "Glix" wordmark text via `expo-image`'s `Image`, sized 72×72. (c) "Akony" font: checked first, per the fix prompt's own instruction, whether a font by this name already existed anywhere in the repo rather than assuming — `client-mobile/assets/fonts/AKONY.ttf` does exist, confirmed via `file` as a genuine TrueType font (family "AKONY Bold", single weight, digitally signed) — not a placeholder, so no need to stop and ask the user for the asset. Confirmed via `grep expo-font package.json` that this app had never loaded a single custom font before this phase (matching the fix prompt's own claim that a prior phase deliberately deferred a JetBrains Mono asset for exactly this reason) — this was genuine new infrastructure, not a one-line style tweak, exactly as the fix prompt itself warned. Added the dependency via `npx expo install expo-font` rather than guessing a version — Expo's own SDK-54 compatibility resolver picked `~14.0.12`, which also auto-registered the `expo-font` config plugin in `app.json`. Wired via `useFonts({ Akony: require('../assets/fonts/AKONY.ttf') })` in `app/_layout.tsx`, gating the existing `SplashScreen.hideAsync()` call (previously fired unconditionally on mount) and the boot-loader-vs-Stack render branch on `fontsLoaded || fontError`, so no screen in the app can paint before the font is ready — no flash of the system font on the login screen's first frame. Grepped the entire `app/` tree for every literal rendering of "Glix" as the app name (not code comments, not unrelated identifiers like `exportGlixData`) before applying anything, per the fix prompt's own instruction to target every wordmark rendering specifically: found exactly two — `app/login.tsx`'s wordmark `Text` and `components/AnimatedSplash.tsx`'s `WORDMARK = ['G','L','I','X']` per-letter splash reveal (Phase 32's "SVG logo-draw-on sequence" — the ring/glow/disc are genuine SVG, but the wordmark letters themselves are real `Animated.Text`, not vector art, so a `fontFamily` change applies directly). Applied `fontFamily: 'Akony'` to both, and in both places also set `fontWeight: 'normal'` explicitly — Akony ships as a single Bold weight with no sibling regular/other-weight files, and leaving a numeric `fontWeight` on a Text using a custom single-weight font is a known iOS gotcha: the OS tries to synthesize a bolder variant of a family name it doesn't have multiple weights registered under, which silently falls back to the system font instead of rendering the custom one at all — documented inline at both sites so it doesn't get "helpfully" reverted later. `app/register.tsx` was checked too (explicitly named alongside login in the fix prompt's scope) and found to have no existing "Glix" wordmark at all — its heading reads "Create Account", a pre-existing design choice — so there was nothing to restyle there; deliberately did not invent a new wordmark element on that screen, since the ask was to apply the font to existing renderings, not add new UI, and this is called out explicitly here rather than silently under-delivering against the fix prompt's phrasing. No header/nav title anywhere in the app renders the literal app name (confirmed by the same grep), so nothing else qualified.
