Phase 49 — Add-to-Watchlist no longer force-navigates (fix prompt's Phase I): symptom was that adding a show/movie routed the user away to the Shows/Movies Hub (`router.replace` with a `highlightFilter` param) instead of just adding it in place. Read `app/show/[id].tsx`'s `handleAddToWatchlist` and `app/movie/[id].tsx`'s `handlePrimaryAction` live first — both confirmed still doing exactly this, unchanged since whenever they were written. Also checked `app/onboarding.tsx` per the fix prompt's own explicit instruction to verify whether its flow should be exempted rather than silently changing it too: onboarding's own `router.replace('/(tabs)')` fires after a bulk `Promise.all(...addShowToWatchlist(id))` over every selected show, as the deliberate final step of a "finish setup" action — not a per-item add tap at all, a genuinely different code path with a genuinely different UX intent (guiding a brand-new user who has no shows yet into the Hub they're about to see for the first time). Left it untouched — not ambiguous enough to need asking, since it isn't the same bug the fix prompt is describing. Fix: removed the `router.replace` call from both detail screens' add handlers. The "Add to Watchlist" pill/icon on both screens already flips to its tracked state in place with zero new logic needed — it's driven by `watchlistEntry`(`show/[id].tsx`)/`isInWatchlist`(`movie/[id].tsx`) turning truthy the moment the store's own post-add `set()` lands, which was already true before this phase, just previously masked by the immediate navigation away. Added a new `Snackbar` ("Added to Watchlist", no action button — this is confirmation, not an undo-able action like Phase F's remove) on both screens for the momentary confirmation a redirect used to provide, reusing the same component pattern already established by the Catch-Up cascade's undo toast and Phase F's remove-undo toast (now the third `Snackbar` instance on `show/[id].tsx`, each gated on its own independent `visible` state so they don't interfere). `success`'s role in both handlers changed from "should I navigate" to "should I show the confirmation" — the underlying optimistic-add/error-rollback behavior in `addShowToWatchlist`/`addMovieToWatchlist` (both already `Promise<boolean>` for exactly this branching, per the fix prompt's own note) is completely unchanged, so a failed add still surfaces the store's existing error state and simply shows no confirmation, no regression. Files: `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new. Manual trace of both the show and movie add paths (pill/icon flip confirmed driven by existing store-derived booleans, snackbar gating confirmed independent of the other two snackbars already on `show/[id].tsx`). Not verifiable this session: no device — the actual on-screen pill-flip timing/feel and the new snackbar's appearance without a redirect happening alongside it. Phase 48 — My Shows/My Movies language-filter pill scroll fix + Anime filter (fix prompt's Phase H): symptom (screenshot with arrow) was the Language pill, appended at the end of the status-filter pill row on My Shows/My Movies, cut off and unreachable. Read `app/profile/shows.tsx` and `app/profile/movies.tsx` live first — the actual root cause was more fundamental than "doesn't scroll far enough": `styles.pillRow` was a plain `flexDirection:'row'` `View` with no `ScrollView` wrapper and no `flexWrap` at all, so once the status pills (3-4) plus the Language pill exceeded the screen's width, everything past the visible edge was simply clipped with literally no way to reach it — not a scroll-distance tuning issue. Fixed by wrapping both screens' pill rows in a horizontal `ScrollView` (`showsHorizontalScrollIndicator={false}`, the existing `pillRow` style moved to `contentContainerStyle` unchanged — same convention `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s own filter-pill row already uses), confirmed both screens shared the identical pattern before fixing both (per the fix prompt's own "apply to both if both share the pattern" instruction). Added the requested Anime filter pill to both screens as a new self-contained toggle (kept local `useState`, not elevated to the global Zustand store the way `selectedLanguage` is — nothing in the ask requires cross-screen persistence for it, noted as an easy elevation later if wanted), 100% client-side over the already-loaded watchlist, matching Language's own established no-new-API-request convention exactly. Backing heuristic lives in a new shared `client-mobile/lib/anime.ts` rather than inlined in each screen, specifically so Phase K's Discover Hub filter (queued next after this batch) can reuse the identical definition rather than risking a second, subtly different one: TMDB has no dedicated "Anime" genre, so the documented signal is the `Animation` genre name (exact TMDB string, confirmed against `backend/core/services.py`'s `get_show_details`/`get_movie_details` — genres are TMDB's own `g["name"]` values, not internally renamed) combined with `original_language === 'ja'` — deliberately excludes a Western-produced animated show (Animation genre, `en` language) and a live-action Japanese drama (`ja` language, no Animation genre), both real cases that must NOT match. Verified the heuristic (no live TMDB query available this session, reasoned against known real title data instead, stated explicitly): Attack on Titan/Death Note/One Piece (Animation + `ja`) correctly match; Rick and Morty/The Simpsons (Animation + `en`) correctly don't; a live-action Japanese drama like Alice in Borderland (`ja`, no Animation genre) correctly doesn't either. Two variants exported — `isAnimeByGenresAndLanguage` for `Show.genres` (a real TMDB-sourced string array) and `isAnimeByGenreStringAndLanguage` for `MovieCache.genres_string` (comma-separated, split before the same check) — since the two models store genres in different shapes. Files: `client-mobile/lib/anime.ts` (new), `client-mobile/app/profile/shows.tsx`, `client-mobile/app/profile/movies.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new. Not verifiable this session: no device — actual scroll-reachability/gesture feel on a real narrow screen, and whether the Anime pill's icon/label read clearly at the pill's small size in practice. Phase 47 — Upcoming tab surfaces aired-but-untracked episodes (fix prompt's Phase G): symptom was that an episode which aired but was never marked watched simply disappeared from the Upcoming tab once its date passed, rather than staying visible with a way to catch up on it (reference: TV Time's own behavior). Read `lib/upcoming.ts`, `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s `UpcomingRow`/`renderUpcomingGridEntry`/`handleCheckPress`/`handleGridCheckPress`, `lib/dateFormat.ts`, and `store/watchStore.ts`'s `syncWidgetData()` live first — confirmed `buildUpcomingItems()` is genuinely the single shared source for both the in-app Upcoming tab and the widget bridge, so a fix here doesn't need a second widget-side change. Root cause: the function's main loop unconditionally skipped any episode with `air_date < todayIso`, meaning the moment an aired episode's date passed without being marked watched, it fell out of the list forever, silently, with no distinction from "already watched." Fixed by adding one "next unwatched" episode per show — same earliest-aired-unwatched definition `pickNextEpisode()` already uses elsewhere in this file, not a new concept — as an explicit "overdue" item when that episode's air date has passed, sorted by air_date/season/episode so a show with multiple unwatched-and-aired episodes only surfaces the one actually blocking its progress, not its entire backlog (deliberate scope choice, matching the fix prompt's own "for as long as it remains the show's next unwatched episode" wording, not "show every episode ever missed"). `formatUpcomingHeaderLabel()` (`lib/dateFormat.ts`) previously had no branch for a negative day-difference, so an overdue item's header fell through to the "LATER" catch-all — which reads as a future date, backwards for something already past — added an explicit `OVERDUE` bucket ahead of the `TOMORROW`/weekday/exact-date branches. `UpcomingRow` (List view) and `renderUpcomingGridEntry` (Grid view) both distinguish `isAired` (today-or-earlier, gates whether the mark-watched control renders at all — WatchStateToggleView itself 400s on an unaired episode server-side, so a control on a genuinely future item would just error) from `isOverdue` (strictly before today, swaps the countdown text to a red "OVERDUE" label instead of `formatCountdown`'s misleading `00:00:00` clamp for a negative diff). The inline mark-watched control on both views calls a new `handleUpcomingMarkWatched()` wrapper around the existing `handleGridCheckPress` — not a third toggle implementation — which already runs the full catch-up-aware flow (`catchup.checkEpisode()` first, `toggleWatchState()` if nothing to cascade); safe to reuse as-is since every item this control renders on is guaranteed unwatched (`buildUpcomingItems()` only ever emits unwatched episodes) and already present in the local episode cache (it had to be, to have been enumerated from `show.episodes` in the first place). Shows Hub bucketing (`buildRows()`, Phase 41/A) needed no changes — it already derives from `pickNextEpisode()` and each entry's own `air_date`/`last_watched_at`, independent of the Upcoming tab's own filtering, so an aired-but-unwatched episode was already correctly influencing WATCH NEXT vs HAVEN'T WATCHED FOR A WHILE before this phase; confirmed by tracing rather than assumed. Files: `client-mobile/lib/upcoming.ts`, `client-mobile/lib/dateFormat.ts`, `client-mobile/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new. Manual trace of both requested scenarios (episode aired 2 days ago, unwatched → appears in Upcoming as OVERDUE with a working mark-watched control; marking it → `toggleWatchState`'s existing optimistic update flips `is_watched` and bumps `last_watched_at`, which the Upcoming `useMemo` and Shows Hub `buildRows()` both recompute from on the next render, so the item disappears from Upcoming and the show's pill updates in the same tick). Not verifiable this session: no device — the actual on-screen OVERDUE badge/red-text treatment, the checkmark's tap feedback on both List and Grid rows, and whether the widget (same shared pipeline, unmodified) actually renders the new overdue items correctly on a real home screen. Phase 46 — Remove/undo "Add to Watchlist" (fix prompt's Phase F): symptom was that adding a show/movie by accident (or on purpose, wanting to reverse it) had no working remove action. Checked `urls.py`/`views.py` live first rather than assuming the gap — confirmed no `DELETE`-style endpoint existed anywhere for `Watchlist`/`MovieWatchlist`, and no frontend handler was wired to a broken no-op either; the gap was real and total. The fix prompt itself flagged one real data-modeling decision that needed a call, not a guess: does removing also delete the user's episode-level watch history, or only untrack while preserving it? Asked the user directly rather than picking silently, since it's an irreversible-if-wrong choice with real stats/badge consequences — answer: **full delete**. Backend: new `ShowRemoveView` (`DELETE /api/watchlist/remove/`, body `{show_id}`) and `MovieRemoveView` (`DELETE /api/movies/watchlist/remove/`, body `{movie_id}`), both `IsAuthenticated`, both inside `transaction.atomic()` with `select_for_update()` on the profile row (same locking pattern as `WatchStateToggleView`/`BulkWatchStateToggleView`). Each captures the full watch-state snapshot before deleting (every `WatchState`/`MovieWatchState` row for that title, not just what happened to be cached client-side — same "server is the source of truth" reasoning as `CatchupCheckView`'s eager-cache-before-answering design), deletes both the state rows and the `Watchlist`/`MovieWatchlist` join row, and decrements `total_time_watched` by the exact same `F()`-expression pattern (with the same non-negative floor) `WatchStateToggleView` already uses per individual un-watch — `earned_badges` is deliberately left untouched, matching the existing additive-only badge convention everywhere else (un-watching via the toggle endpoints never revokes a badge either, so remove shouldn't behave differently). The response returns exactly what was deleted (`watched_episode_ids`, `was_favorite`, `was_status`, `was_ignore_catchup`) so the frontend can restore it precisely on Undo regardless of what it had cached locally. Frontend: `watchStore.ts` gained `removeShowFromWatchlist`/`undoRemoveShow` and `removeMovieFromWatchlist`/`undoRemoveMovie` — remove optimistically strips the entry from all three watchlist buckets immediately (Shows Hub/Upcoming update in place, since both already derive from the same buckets), then on success calls `fetchProfile()` + `syncWidgetData()` to resync stats and the widget (the fourth and final downstream consumer from the fix prompt's own checklist); on failure rolls the optimistic removal back and surfaces the store's existing error state. Undo replays the server snapshot: re-add via the existing `addShowToWatchlist`, restore favorite/archived/ignore-catchup via the same raw endpoint calls `app/show/[id].tsx` already made inline for those toggles, then `bulkToggleWatchState(watchedEpisodeIds, true)` — which hits the real backend regardless of local cache state, so every previously-watched episode comes back even for seasons the freshly-re-added entry hasn't re-cached yet — followed by a `fetchWatchlist()` to reconcile the visible per-season progress. Deliberately mirrors the Catch-Up cascade's own Undo pattern (act now, offer a real reversal) rather than a deferred-commit/grace-window design, since `Snackbar` was already built exactly for this by that feature and the fix prompt asked to reuse it, not build a second toast component. Surfaced as a `Trash2` icon in the same header-icon-row convention as the existing Archive/Favorite controls on `app/show/[id].tsx` (rendered only when `watchlistEntry` exists — nothing to remove otherwise) and a matching icon added to `app/movie/[id].tsx`'s backdrop action row (grouped with the existing add/watched-toggle icon, rendered only when `isInWatchlist`). Caught one correctness gap this exposed while wiring it up: `app/show/[id].tsx`'s local `isFavorite`/`isArchived` state only ever updated when `watchlistEntry` was truthy — previously the only way that entry went from set to null was navigating away entirely, so it never went stale in place; now that Remove can null it out without leaving the screen, the effect needed an explicit else-branch reset to `false`, or the Heart/Archive icons would keep showing a stale favorited/archived state after a remove. Files: `backend/core/views.py` (`ShowRemoveView`, `MovieRemoveView`), `backend/core/urls.py`, `backend/core/tests/test_views.py` (6 new tests: unauthenticated + not-in-watchlist + full-delete-with-snapshot-assertions, both show and movie sides), `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/show/[id].tsx`, `client-mobile/app/movie/[id].tsx`. Verified: `manage.py check` clean, `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean (no model changes — reused the existing schema), `pytest` inside the `watchtracker_backend` Docker container — 44/44 (38 prior + 6 new), zero failures; `tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, same pre-existing baseline (`HeroCarousel.tsx` ref type, stale `watchStore.test.ts`), zero new. Not verifiable this session: no device — real tap behavior on both detail screens, the Snackbar's on-screen appearance/timing for this new message, and genuinely reproducing a network failure mid-remove to watch the optimistic-rollback path fire live. Phase 45 — Dynamic FlashList card sizing (fix prompt's Phase E): symptom was "cards/posters sometimes too large, forcing extra scrolling," and the fix prompt's own hypothesis was a mismatched `estimatedItemSize` on 7 flagged grid call sites (5 at `260`, 2 at `200`) needing a `Dimensions`-driven computed value like `discover.tsx`'s own `CARD_WIDTH` pattern. Checked the installed `@shopify/flash-list` version directly (per `AGENTS.md`'s "don't trust cached assumptions, read the real docs" rule) before writing anything — it's `2.0.2`, and FlashList v2's own `FlashListProps.d.ts` has no `estimatedItemSize` field at all, its runtime `.js` source has zero references to the string anywhere, and its own README states outright: "Unlike v1, FlashList v2 automatically handles item sizing... doesn't require any estimates." All 9 `estimatedItemSize` call sites in the repo (the 7 flagged plus 2 more Phase 39 had separately hand-tuned to `110`/`138`) were dead code carrying zero runtime effect — every phase back through 39 had filed the resulting `tsc` excess-property errors under "FlashList typing gap" without ever checking why the prop wasn't in the type, so the premise behind both this phase's ask and Phase 39's own "audit" was already stale at the time either ran. Removed all 9 (and the now-inapplicable size-math comments on the two Phase-39-tuned ones) — this alone eliminates the entire error category from the `tsc` baseline, not just avoids adding to it. That still left the real bug: `estimatedItemSize`'s value never controlled visual card size in v2 to begin with, so the actual cause of "too large" had to be the card's real rendered dimensions. `ShowPosterCard`/`MoviePosterCard` (Shows Hub, Movies Hub, Profile Shows/Movies, Upcoming grid — 5 FlashLists) all ran `numColumns={preferredLayout === 'grid' ? 2 : 1}`, rendering each poster nearly twice the width (and, via `aspectRatio: 2/3`, height) of `discover.tsx`'s own already-shipped `SearchResultCard` grid, which uses `numColumns={3}` with the same poster+title+subtitle content density — a real, visible size inconsistency, not a perception issue. Bumped all 5 to `numColumns={3}` to match; no per-card code changes needed since both card components already size via `flex:1`/`width:'100%'`/`aspectRatio`, not a fixed pixel width — FlashList's own column math handles the rest, arguably more robust across device widths than a manual `Dimensions.get('window')` constant would have been (no orientation-change staleness risk). Investigated the master prompt's own alternate hypotheses for the Upcoming tab specifically ("card rendering more content than needed," "fixed padding/margins that don't collapse") — ruled out: `UpcomingRow`'s list-mode layout is a flat per-episode row (110px, poster 80px + padding), already lean, with no per-show variable-content container that could fail to collapse. Files: `app/(tabs)/index.tsx` (2 FlashLists), `app/(tabs)/movies.tsx`, `app/profile/shows.tsx`, `app/profile/movies.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/discover.tsx` (2 FlashLists), `components/HorizontalMediaList.tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — 2 errors, both pre-existing baseline (`HeroCarousel.tsx` ref type, stale `watchStore.test.ts`), zero new — down from 11, the entire FlashList category gone. Not verifiable this session: no device — real on-screen card size/density at 3 columns across actual phone widths, and whether it reads as "right-sized" rather than "too small" in the other direction, can't be confirmed without one; this is a visible density change across 5 screens, flagged here plainly rather than buried, in case 2-column was intentionally chosen for a more prominent poster look and the user wants it reverted on any specific screen. Phase 44 — Mark-season-watched control on the outside row (fix prompt's Phase D): `app/show/[id].tsx`'s `SeasonCard` rows never fetched or displayed any episode/watched counts at all (`episodeCount`/`watchedCount` props existed on the component but were never passed from the screen) — every row silently showed "Tap to view episodes" regardless of real progress, and there was no way to mark/unmark a season watched without navigating into it. Read `components/SeasonCard.tsx`, `app/show/[id]/season/[season].tsx` (the season screen's own working Mark/Unmark Season Watched button — reused, not reimplemented), `lib/useCatchupCascade.ts`, and `store/watchStore.ts`'s `bulkToggleWatchState` live first. Fixed: `app/show/[id].tsx` now derives a per-season `{episodeCount, watchedCount}` map from `watchlistEntry.show.episodes` (the same field `lib/upcoming.ts` reads), using the same local-midnight `todayLocalIso()` aired-episode filter used everywhere else in the app — a season the user has never opened simply isn't in `episodes` yet, so it stays in the pre-existing "Tap to view episodes" state until visited, same fallback as before this phase. `SeasonCard` gained an optional `onToggleWatched`/`isTogglingWatched` prop pair rendering a 28px checkmark circle matching `EpisodeRow`'s existing checkmark iconography exactly (filled `accentFill` + `Check` icon when the season's aired episodes are all watched, plain outline otherwise) — omitted entirely when the show isn't in the watchlist, since there's nothing to toggle. The tap handler always fetches the season fresh via the same `GET /shows/<id>/season/<n>/` (`SeasonEpisodesView`) the season screen itself calls, rather than trusting the possibly-stale/absent local cache, then routes through the identical logic the season screen uses: un-watch direction goes straight to `bulkToggleWatchState`, watch direction goes through `useCatchupCascade().checkSeason()` first (same server-side chronological check, same `CascadeModal`/`Snackbar` Undo affordance, now also rendered on this screen) and only calls `bulkToggleWatchState` directly if the check finds nothing to cascade — no second "mark season watched" implementation anywhere. A real ordering bug was self-caught before it shipped: the new hooks were initially placed before `displayShow`'s `const` declaration further down the same component, which the new callbacks' dependency arrays reference — a genuine TDZ `ReferenceError` at render time, not just a lint nit — fixed by moving `displayShow`'s definition earlier, above the new hooks and still before the component's conditional early-returns (Rules-of-Hooks compliant). Files: `components/SeasonCard.tsx`, `app/show/[id].tsx`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — 11 errors, same pre-existing baseline as Phase 43, zero new. Not verifiable this session: no device — real tap behavior, the checkmark's visual state against an actual poster photo, and the Catch-Up modal's on-screen appearance from this new entry point. Phase 43 — Home-screen widget reliability, episode-specific tap-through, countdown, 2-week window (fix prompt's Phase C): read `store/watchStore.ts`'s `syncWidgetData()`, `lib/upcoming.ts`, both `widgets/android/*.tsx`, both `widgets/ios/*.tsx`, `widgets/android/WidgetProvider.tsx`, `app/_layout.tsx`, and `lib/navigation.ts` live first. Traced every call site of `syncWidgetData()` (3: after `fetchWatchlist`, `toggleWatchState`, `bulkToggleWatchState`) and found a real gap: `addShowToWatchlist` never called it, so a freshly-added show silently didn't appear in the widget until some unrelated action happened to trigger a full `fetchWatchlist()` elsewhere — fixed by adding the call. Separately, `fetchWatchlist()` calls inside the season/episode screens are deliberately fire-and-forget (Phase 37's latency fix) — meaning if the user backgrounds the app immediately after marking something watched, that write can lose the race against the process suspending, leaving the widget stale until the next unrelated sync; no `AppState` listener existed anywhere in the app to catch this. Fixed: a new `AppState.addEventListener('change', ...)` in `app/_layout.tsx`, gated on `isAuthenticated`, calls `syncWidgetData()` (no network call, just flushes current in-memory store state) the moment the app transitions to `'background'`. Widget rows previously deep-linked only to the show's general page (`watchtracker://show/<id>`) — `lib/upcoming.ts`'s `UpcomingItem` gained an `episodeId: number | null` field (the real `CachedEpisode` tmdb_id for a cached upcoming episode, null for the synthetic `next_episode_to_air` item which has no cached episode row to link to yet), `pickNextEpisode()`'s return already carried a real episode id for the "Next Up" widget; both Android widget row components now build `watchtracker://episode/<id>` first, falling back to the show link, falling back to opening the app — the same 3-tier fallback pattern, extended by one tier. The "Airing Soon" widget's flat top-5 slice became a real 14-day window (`buildUpcomingItems().filter(airDate <= +14d).slice(0, 30)`, Android's `ListWidget` already scrolls to reach all of it) with a `countdown` string precomputed once per sync via the exact `formatCountdown()` the in-app Upcoming tab uses (`"Xd XXh XXm (Weekday)"`), since the widget itself can't run a live per-second tick. `widgets/android/WidgetProvider.tsx`'s native-triggered `requestWidgetUpdate()` calls were unguarded (no `.catch()`, no outer try/catch) — wrapped, matching the defensive pattern `syncWidgetData()` already used. **Found en route, more serious than the stated ask:** while adding `episode_id`/`countdown` fields to the iOS widget props, `tsc --noEmit` surfaced that the *entire* iOS widget implementation no longer type-checks against the installed `@expo/ui@~0.2.0-beta.9` — checked the package's own live type defs (not assumed): `Image` now only renders SF Symbols via `systemName` (`ImageProps` has no `source`/URL field at all — genuinely no remote-image-loading capability exists anywhere in this package version), and `Text`/`VStack`/`HStack` dropped their `color`/`font`/`style` props entirely in favor of a `modifiers` array (`foregroundColor()`/`font()`/`background()`/`padding()`/`lineLimit()` from `@expo/ui/swift-ui/modifiers`). Both `widgets/ios/WatchlistWidget.tsx`/`UpcomingWidget.tsx` were rewritten against this real current API — TMDB posters can no longer render on iOS at all (a real capability loss in the dependency, not a regression from this pass; a generic SF Symbol glyph stands in, documented inline) — and, since `createWidget`'s layout function already receives a `WidgetEnvironment` with `widgetFamily` that neither file had ever consumed, both now render a second compact row on `systemMedium` instead of hard-coding `props.watchlist?.[0]`/`props.upcoming?.[0]` — WidgetKit itself has no scrolling in home-screen widgets (a real platform constraint, not fixable from any dependency version) and `systemSmall`/`systemMedium` share the same fixed height per `app.json`'s `supportedFamilies` (no `systemLarge`), so this is the real ceiling, not an arbitrary cap. Files: `client-mobile/lib/upcoming.ts`, `client-mobile/store/watchStore.ts`, `client-mobile/app/_layout.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/WidgetProvider.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/WatchlistWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/android/UpcomingWidget.tsx`, `client-mobile/widgets/ios/WatchlistWidget.tsx` (full rewrite), `client-mobile/widgets/ios/UpcomingWidget.tsx` (full rewrite). Verified: `node --stack-size=8000 tsc --noEmit` — 11 errors total, down from the prior baseline (the iOS widget rewrite eliminated its entire ~16-error category outright, not just avoided adding to it); the 11 remaining are the already-documented FlashList `estimatedItemSize` typing gap (9 sites), `HeroCarousel.tsx`'s ref type, and the stale `__tests__/watchStore.test.ts` `isLoading` reference — zero new errors anywhere, confirmed by file name against every touched file. Manual trace of the `syncWidgetData()` call graph and the new `AppState`/episode-deep-link/14-day-window logic. Re-confirmed, still true against the currently installed `expo-widgets@57.0.3`: no `widgetURL`/`Link`/tap-to-open mechanism exists for `createWidget` at all (only `LiveActivityFactory.start(props, url?)` supports a URL, Live-Activity-only) — iOS tap-through stays a real, unfixable-from-this-repo library limitation, restated rather than silently re-attempted. Not verifiable this session: no Android/iOS device or emulator — actual widget render reliability, real tap-through (both platforms), the corrected countdown/window on a real home screen, and the iOS rewrite's actual visual output (glyph-instead-of-poster, the two-row `systemMedium` layout) are all reasoned from live type defs and code tracing only, never seen rendering. Phase 42 — TV Time import: chunked/resumable + diff-aware re-import (fix prompt's Phase B): `run_tvtime_import` processed a whole export (up to ~1,100 sequential TMDB calls) as one Celery invocation under `soft_time_limit=1500`/`time_limit=1560`; on a 300+ show library the soft limit fired mid-run, and regardless of whether that was caught per-item or by the outer handler, the code unconditionally cleared `job.payload = {}` at the end — destroying the only record of what still needed processing, so a resubmission always restarted at item 0. Separately, `_import_one_show` already skipped duplicate DB writes but built its season-fetch list from the full `wanted` set before checking that, so an already-fully-imported show still re-fetched every watched season from TMDB on every resubmission. Fixed: `run_tvtime_import` now processes `IMPORT_CHUNK_SIZE=10` shows+movies per invocation and re-enqueues itself (`apply_async(countdown=1)`) when work remains, using the already-persisted-per-item `job.processed` as a resume cursor; `soft_time_limit`/`time_limit` dropped to 300s/330s (sized for one chunk, not the whole export); only a `SUCCESS` job clears `payload` now, a `FAILED` job (chunk timeout or the pre-existing orphaned-job close-out) keeps both `payload` and `processed`. `_import_one_show` now cross-references `wanted` against already-cached-and-marked (season, episode) pairs before calling `tmdb.get_season_episodes()`, skipping the season fetch entirely once nothing's left to mark for that show. `TVTimeImportView.post` now also checks the user's latest `FAILED` job and resumes it in place (status back to `RUNNING`, cursor untouched) when its `payload` is byte-identical to the resubmission and `processed > 0`; a differing payload still starts a fresh job as before. Documented, deliberate scope boundary: true zero-network-cost diffing (skipping the id-resolution call itself) would need `CachedShow` indexed by `tvdb_id`/`imdb_id`, which doesn't exist and is out of this phase's ask — one resolution call per show remains unavoidable on every re-run. Found and fixed en route, unrelated: the dev Postgres container was missing an already-committed migration (`0009_importjob_updated_at`) never applied to this container's volume — `manage.py migrate` applied it plus this phase's own `0010_alter_importjob_payload` (help_text-only). Files: `backend/core/tasks.py`, `backend/core/views.py`, `backend/core/models.py`, new `backend/core/tests/test_tvtime_import.py`. Verified: `pytest` inside the `watchtracker_backend` Docker container — 39/39 (33 baseline + 6 new: fresh import, chunked interruption + resume across two invocations, diff-aware re-import with zero season re-fetches/zero duplicate `WatchState` rows, the status endpoint, and both view-level resume paths); `manage.py check` clean; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean. Not verifiable this session: a real 300+ show end-to-end re-import against live TMDB — no device/running backend session; verified instead via code tracing and the test suite's simulated scenarios against a faked TMDB network boundary, with all higher-level caching/DB-write logic exercised for real. Phase 41 — Shows Hub status-pill correctness + speed (fix prompt's Phase A): `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s `buildRows()` bucketed a *started* show into WATCH NEXT vs HAVEN'T WATCHED FOR A WHILE by recency (`entry.last_watched_at` within 14 days, or a next episode aired within 14 days), but the store's optimistic mark-watched reducers (`toggleWatchState`/`bulkToggleWatchState`) only bumped `watched_episode_count`, never `last_watched_at`, and the toggle endpoints don't return it — so marking the first episode of a back-catalog show (next episode aired >14d ago) left `last_watched_at` null and dropped the show into HAVEN'T WATCHED FOR A WHILE instead of WATCH NEXT, stuck there on the Hub until an unrelated `fetchWatchlist()`. Fixed: both reducers set `last_watched_at = now` on mark-watched (touched entry only, watch direction only). Fresh-add → HAVEN'T STARTED, local-midnight anchoring, and uncached-season bucketing (categorization uses backend-annotated counts, not a client recount) all re-confirmed correct. Also gated the Hub's always-on `useNow(1000)` tick — which re-rendered the whole heavy WATCH LIST FlashList once a second for a countdown only UPCOMING shows — to `activeTab === 'upcoming' && upcomingView === 'list'`. Files: `store/watchStore.ts`, `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`. Verified `tsc --noEmit` — 30 errors, same baseline, zero new; on-device speed unverifiable (no device). Phase 40 — Google Sign-In final-touches audit (fix prompt's Phase 6, P4) + the requested overall pre-push verification pass: re-checked `lib/socialAuth.ts`, `SocialSignInButtons.tsx`, `backend/core/social_auth.py`/`auth_views.py`/`urls.py`, both `.env` files, and `app.json`'s plugin/bundle-id config against live code (not the stale fix-prompt snapshot) — everything was already correctly wired since Phase 27/28/34/38, `com.watchtracker.app` deliberately kept as the bundle ID through the Phase 30 rebrand since Google/Apple provisioning is keyed to it, nothing left to fix in-repo. Still-open items stay external: Apple Developer Program enrollment (own paid account needed) and the actual on-device OAuth handshake (no device/simulator here). The "overall test before push" pass then caught a real, unrelated, previously-undetected bug: `watchtracker_celery`/`watchtracker_celery_beat` were both live crash-looping (`docker ps` showing `Restarting (1)`) on `ValueError: ... URL scheme is redis://. A Redis SSL connection URL should use the scheme rediss://` — `backend/config/celery.py` forced `broker_use_ssl`/`redis_backend_use_ssl` unconditionally (meant for Upstash's `rediss://` in production) against local Docker Compose's plain `redis://redis:6379/0`, meaning **no Celery task had been running locally at all**: no imports, no badge/streak recalculation, no push notifications, Beat not firing `sync_active_shows`/`send_weekly_digest` — the entire mechanism Phase 31 built. Fixed by gating each SSL block on its own resolved URL actually starting with `rediss://`; rebuilt both containers and confirmed stable (worker connected, all 8 tasks registered, beat started clean, neither container restarted again). Verified, whole session: `pytest -v` inside the live `watchtracker_backend` container (its Postgres has the `CREATEDB` grant the native instance lacks) — **33/33 passing**, the first full current-suite run this session; `manage.py check` clean; `makemigrations --check --dry-run` clean; `tsc --noEmit` — 30 errors, same baseline as Phases 34-39, zero new. Not verifiable this session: no device for the mobile app; the Celery fix is confirmed via clean logs/no restart, not a real scheduled task actually firing (Beat's `maxinterval` is 5 minutes). Phase 39 — Card-size polish (fix prompt's Phase 5, P3): audited every `FlashList estimatedItemSize` call site (9 total) against its real card component's measured height. Found and fixed two genuine underestimates, both in the profile screens' dense list rows: `app/profile/shows.tsx`'s `ShowListRow` was `96` (just the posterWrap's own height) when the true row — rowContent's padding + its 4 stacked children (title/status pill/progress bar/episode count) + the row's own `marginBottom: 10` — measures `110`, matching the ShowRow/MovieRow/UpcomingRow convention elsewhere of poster-height-plus-margin rather than a bare poster guess. `app/profile/movies.tsx`'s `MovieListRow` was `104` when the common case (runtime + genre + rating all present, true for virtually every real TMDB movie) measures `138`. Both fixed with an inline comment documenting the math, mirroring `ShowRow.tsx`/`MovieRow.tsx`'s own documented-constant convention. Everything else audited and left alone, explicitly not silently dropped: the 5 grid call sites sharing `estimatedItemSize={260}` (`ShowPosterCard`/`MoviePosterCard`, `index.tsx`/`movies.tsx`/`profile/shows.tsx`/`profile/movies.tsx`) and `discover.tsx`'s 2 search/filter-grid sites (`estimatedItemSize={200}`, `SearchResultCard`) are all width-dependent (`aspectRatio`-based poster cards) — a real "exact match" fix needs a `Dimensions.get('window')`-driven computed value (the repo's own established pattern in `discover.tsx`/`GenreGrid.tsx`/etc.), which is a bigger change than a polish pass; deferred, flagged here rather than silently left. `HorizontalMediaList.tsx`'s `142` and the Shows/Movies Hub's own list-mode `108`/`110` values were re-verified correct against `ShowRow`/`MovieRow`/`UpcomingRow`'s own documented math — no fix needed. Also investigated the Upcoming tab's mixed header/row list (single `110` estimate covering both ~41px headers and ~110px rows): the installed `@shopify/flash-list` version's `overrideItemLayout` only supports overriding `span`, not `size` (verified directly in its `.d.ts`), so per-type initial-size correction isn't possible with the current dependency version — ruled out as unfixable here, not a new bug, low severity since it only affects first paint before FlashList v2's own auto-measurement takes over. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (stack-size workaround) — 30 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new (the two edited files each show up in the already-tracked 8-site `estimatedItemSize` typing-gap category, not as new errors). Not verifiable this session: no device to visually confirm the corrected estimates reduce initial-paint jank. Phase 38 — Backup/export two-file rework (fix prompt's Phase 4, P2): `lib/migration.ts`'s `exportGlixData()` produced one combined JSON (shows + movies + profile) via the share sheet. Reworked to write and share two separate files — `glix-shows-YYYY-MM-DD.json` (profile + shows) and `glix-movies-YYYY-MM-DD.json` (movies) — mirroring the TV Time GDPR import's own two-file convention (`tvtime-series-*.json`/`tvtime-movies-*.json`), via a new shared `writeAndShare()` helper. Sequential, not parallel, since `Sharing.shareAsync` only resolves once its sheet is dismissed — showing both share sheets at once isn't possible. Audited the existing export/backup code first for correctness bugs (per the bootstrap-verify rule) — none found: `fetchWatchlist` already loads the full unpaginated watchlist (Phase 35), the movies endpoint is deliberately unpaginated, and `is_watched` is correctly user-scoped in `MovieCacheSerializer`. Scope confirmed with user (original Phase 4 wording lost in an earlier compaction, no master-prompt file kept in-repo to recheck verbatim) before starting. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (stack-size workaround) — 30 errors, same pre-existing baseline, zero new. Not verifiable this session: no device to physically confirm two sequential share sheets render correctly on iOS/Android. Phase 37 — Performance pass (fix prompt's Phase 3): two real latency sources found and fixed, one ruled-out-as-already-fixed. (1) `app/show/[id]/season/[season].tsx` and `app/episode/[id].tsx` both `await`ed a full `fetchWatchlist()` (`/watchlist/?page_size=all`, heavy prefetch joins) before dismissing their loading spinner, even though neither screen renders from the Zustand watchlist — pure added latency on every season/episode open. Fixed: both now fire `fetchWatchlist()` without awaiting it (it already swallows its own errors into store state). (2) `DiscoverFeedView` (`backend/core/views.py`) bundled 3 (tv) or 4 (movie) independent TMDB calls into one response but ran them sequentially, paying the sum of each call's worst-case request+retry latency on a cold cache. Fixed: parallelized via `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (3-4 workers), capping latency at the slowest single call. (3) "Can't reach Glix" flakiness and TMDB rate-limit backoff tuning — checked live code, already fixed in Phase 34 (`lib/api.ts`'s GET-only retry interceptor + 15s timeout, `services.py`'s tightened `Retry` backoff) — confirmed still correct, nothing to redo. Verified: `python manage.py check` clean (venv interpreter), `ast.parse` clean, `tsc --noEmit` at the same 30-error pre-existing baseline, zero new errors. Not verifiable this session: no running backend/device to measure actual wall-clock improvement. Phase 36 — Widget/deep-link follow-up: the fix prompt's Phase 2 asked to rebuild the widget UI, fix its empty state, and fix a deep-link back-navigation dead end. Re-verified against live code (not the stale snapshot the prompt was authored from): Android widget resize/scroll/tap-through and both widgets' empty states were already fixed in Phase 34 below — confirmed still correct, nothing to redo. The real remaining gap: `app/show/[id].tsx` (the widget's only deep-link target, `watchtracker://show/<id>`) called bare `router.back()` on its two back buttons, which no-ops when that screen is the only entry in the nav stack (opening the app cold via a widget tap) — a dead end with no way back but a force-quit. Fixed via new `lib/navigation.ts`'s `goBack(router)` helper (`canGoBack()` check, falls back to `router.replace('/(tabs)')`), wired into both back buttons. Also researched and confirmed (Expo's own versioned widget docs, fetched live) that iOS widgets have no tap-target mechanism in `expo-widgets`/`@expo/ui` at all — no `widgetURL`/`Link` documented for `createWidget` — so the Phase 34 iOS gap stays correctly out of scope, now for a stronger reason (library limitation, not just missing device time) than previously stated. Phase 35 — Watchlist under-fetch fix: `fetchWatchlist` fetched only page 1 (20/bucket), so the whole app (Profile "My Shows" count, Shows Hub, Home/Upcoming tab, home-screen widget — all derive from the same in-memory buckets) under-reported a ~200-show import as "My Shows: 40" with shows missing from Upcoming/widget. Fixed: `WatchlistView` now honours `?page_size=all` (unpaginated, no extra DB cost — the full prefetch already ran); `fetchWatchlist` requests it. Backend idempotent-job guard + live-derived bucketing ruled out as causes. Code-verified (py_compile + tsc clean); real 200-show E2E re-import + live Celery Beat trigger deferred (no running backend/device this session). Phase 34 — Import reliability + duplicate-job guard, Android widget resize/scroll/tap-through, Google Sign-In error diagnostics, real navigation-lag fix from 6 unscoped Zustand store subscriptions, backend/.env.prod untracked from git, and EAS Update/OTA configured (expo-updates + eas update:configure) so future JS-only fixes can ship without a full rebuild. See AUDIT.md/PROJECT_STATUS.md for full detail. Phase 32 — Premium animated splash: native black splash hands off seamlessly to a Reanimated/SVG logo-draw-on sequence in loading.tsx, replacing the static wordmark + spinner; expo-splash-screen wired for the native→JS handoff. Phase 31 — Push notifications actually wired end-to-end: an Expo push send module, new-episode detection in the existing refresh_show_cache task, a weekly digest task, and — the actual root cause of nothing ever sending — a Celery Beat service that didn't exist before. Phase 30 — Global rebrand "WatchTracker" → "Glix" across docs/app.json/assets/codebase, categorized Language Filter modal (Major Indian Languages / Global Languages sections), and the Shows Hub's default filter changed to WATCH_NEXT on open. Phase 29 — Rapid pre-deployment audit (1 critical light-theme bug fixed) + language filter + Analytics back button. Phase 28 — Forgot Password via email OTP + styled HTML email, and the EAS Android dev-client build fix: root-caused the `attr/actionBarSize` `mergeDebugResources` duplicate to a vestigial `com.android.support` lib from `react-native-shared-preferences`, fixed via a config plugin excluding the legacy support group. Phase 27 — Sign in with Google/Apple: direct ID-token verification via PyJWT/JWKS, not django-allauth. Phase 26: Django admin modernized with django-unfold. Phase 24: TV Time GDPR import rebuilt on Celery. Phase 25: pre-deployment DevSecOps/API/UI/UX audit)

> **For Claude Code / any new AI agent reading this:**
> This file is the single source of truth for the entire Glix V2 project.
> Read this before making ANY changes. Every section is kept current.

---

## Project Overview

Glix is a **premium TV & movie tracking app** — a dark, glassmorphic alternative to TV Time.

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Design System | Adaptive light/dark (Phase 12, `lib/theme.ts`) — dark default: pitch black `#000000` bg, glass `rgba(30,30,30,0.65)`, hairline `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)`; light: paper `#EDEEEA` bg, raised near-white cards. Cinema Neon Yellow `#E4FA1A` accent in both (splits into `accentFill`/`accentInk` — see §2a) |
| Mobile Framework | React Native + Expo SDK 54 + Expo Router v6 |
| State Management | Zustand (`watchStore`, `discoverStore`) |
| Backend | Django 6 + Django REST Framework + PostgreSQL |
| External Data | TMDB API v3 (proxied through backend, fully cached) |
| Auth | JWT via `djangorestframework-simplejwt` |
| Infrastructure | Docker Compose (backend + postgres + redis + celery containers) |
| Admin UI | django-unfold (Phase 26) — Tailwind-based theme, `UNFOLD["COLORS"]["primary"]` derived from the app's own accent `#E4FA1A` converted to OKLCH, not the package default purple |

---

## Monorepo Folder Structure

```text
watchtracker/                           ← project root
├── docker-compose.yml                  ← runs backend + postgres
├── context.md                          ← THIS FILE (master reference)
├── ROADMAP.md                          ← feature checklist + completion %
├── AUDIT.md                            ← known bugs + technical debt
├── PROJECT_STATUS.md                   ← current phase / last completed
│
├── backend/
│   ├── .env                            ← dev secrets (TMDB_API_KEY, DB creds, SECRET_KEY)
│   ├── .env.prod                       ← production secrets template
│   ├── Dockerfile
│   ├── requirements.txt
│   ├── manage.py
│   ├── pytest.ini
│   ├── config/
│   │   ├── settings.py                 ← DRF, SimpleJWT, Postgres, Celery, CORS
│   │   ├── urls.py                     ← mounts /api/ → core.urls
│   │   ├── celery.py                   ← Celery app instance
│   │   └── wsgi.py / asgi.py
│   └── core/
│       ├── models.py                   ← ALL database models (24 model classes)
│       ├── serializers.py              ← core DRF ModelSerializers (Watchlist, WatchState, CustomList, etc.)
│       ├── services.py                 ← TMDBService (TMDB proxy + cache layer)
│       ├── views.py                    ← watchlist, watch-state toggle, catch-up, movies, discover feed, health check
│       ├── search_views.py             ← search + show/movie detail endpoints
│       ├── auth_views.py               ← register / login / logout / Google+Apple / password-reset OTP
│       ├── profile_views.py            ← profile read + update, avatar options, stats resync
│       ├── dates.py                    ← has_released() (backend counterpart of lib/dateFormat.ts::hasAired()) ← NEW (Phase 74)
│       ├── airtime.py                  ← TVmaze show-slot + per-episode airstamp fetch (TMDB has no time-of-day) ← NEW (Phase 73, extended Phase 75)
│       ├── comment_views.py            ← community comments + replies + feed (Phase 75)
│       ├── comment_serializers.py
│       ├── comment_permissions.py
│       ├── comment_urls.py
│       ├── review_views.py / review_serializers.py / review_urls.py  ← ShowReview/MovieReview, watched-gate (Phase 75) ← NEW (Phase L / 52)
│       ├── social_views.py / social_serializers.py / social_urls.py  ← follow graph, search, public profiles, activity feed ← NEW (Phase 74)
│       ├── lists_views.py / lists_urls.py  ← user-created lists ("Movies2026") ← NEW (Phase 70)
│       ├── rewatch_views.py / rewatch_urls.py  ← show rounds + per-episode ticks, movie rewatch counter ← NEW (Phase 75.7)
│       ├── recommendations_views.py / recommendations_urls.py  ← cross-library "For You" ← NEW (Phase 71)
│       ├── analytics_views.py          ← 12 analytics endpoints (incl. AnalyticsMoviesView, Phase 74)
│       ├── analytics_serializers.py
│       ├── analytics_urls.py
│       ├── throttling.py               ← LocMemCache-backed Anon/User throttles, off the Redis default cache ← NEW (Phase 75.8)
│       ├── tasks.py                    ← Celery badge safety-net + show resync + push triggers + TV Time import + stalled-import self-heal
│       ├── push_notifications.py       ← Expo push send + dead-token cleanup (Phase 31)
│       ├── password_reset.py           ← OTP forgot-password flow (Phase 28)
│       ├── signals.py                  ← auto UserProfile, badge/streak evaluation, cache-bust receivers
│       ├── badge_constants.py          ← badge slug ↔ threshold mapping
│       ├── social_auth.py              ← Google/Apple ID-token verification + get-or-create (Phase 27)
│       ├── cache_keys.py               ← centralized cache-key builders (watchlist, analytics, friends-feed, etc.)
│       ├── pagination.py               ← StandardResultsPagination
│       ├── permissions.py              ← placeholder — per-object checks are still ad hoc `get_object_or_404(..., user=)`
│       ├── exceptions.py               ← global DRF exception handler
│       ├── admin.py                    ← django-unfold ModelAdmin for all models + custom User/Group (Phase 26)
│       ├── urls.py                     ← root URL conf, includes all *_urls.py modules (see API table below)
│       └── tests/                      ← 192 tests passing (Phase 75) — one file per view/feature module
│
└── client-mobile/
    ├── app.json                        ← Expo config (plugins, App Group entitlement)
    ├── package.json
    ├── tsconfig.json
    ├── eas.json                        ← EAS build config

    │   │   ├── index.tsx               ← Shows Hub — dual-tab (WATCH LIST / UPCOMING) ← REFACTORED

    │   │   └── profile.tsx             ← Profile Hub
```

## All API Endpoints

| Method | Path | View | Notes |
|--------|------|------|-------|
| POST | `/api/auth/register/` | RegisterView | |
| POST | `/api/auth/login/` | LoginView | Returns access + refresh tokens |
| POST | `/api/auth/logout/` | LogoutView | Blacklists refresh token |
| POST | `/api/auth/refresh/` | TokenRefreshView | SimpleJWT built-in |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/auth/google/` | GoogleLoginView | Sign in with Google — body `{id_token}`, verifies via `core/social_auth.py`, returns `{access, refresh, profile, created}` |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/auth/apple/` | AppleLoginView | Sign in with Apple — body `{id_token, first_name?, last_name?}` (name only sent on the user's first-ever Apple authorization), same response shape |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/auth/password-reset/request/` | PasswordResetRequestView | Body `{email}` — always 200 with a generic message (enumeration-safe); emails a 6-digit OTP via Gmail SMTP if the account exists |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/auth/password-reset/verify/` | PasswordResetVerifyView | Body `{email, code}` — returns `{reset_token}` on success; 5-attempt cap, single-use code |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/auth/password-reset/confirm/` | PasswordResetConfirmView | Body `{reset_token, new_password}` — sets the password, returns `{access, refresh, profile}` (logs the user straight back in) |
| GET | `/api/watchlist/` | WatchlistView | Paginated, buckets: to_watch / up_to_date / archived; each entry now annotates `last_watched_at` for recency-sorted pills |
| POST | `/api/watchlist/add/` | ShowAddView | Adds show w/o touching episode state; eager-caches season 1 |
| GET | `/api/continue-watching/` | ContinueWatchingView | |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/watch-history/` | WatchHistoryView | Paginated reverse-chronological ledger of individual episodes watched |
| POST | `/api/watch-state/toggle/` | WatchStateToggleView | Auto-adds to watchlist; **rejects marking an unaired episode watched** (400) |
| POST | `/api/watch-state/bulk-toggle/` | BulkWatchStateToggleView | Cascade catch-up; unaired episode ids are silently dropped from the watched batch rather than erroring the whole cascade |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/watch-state/catchup-check/` | CatchupCheckView | Server-authoritative "does this show have earlier unwatched episodes?" — episode mode (`episode_id`) or season mode (`show_id`+`season_number`); eager-caches any missing earlier season via TMDBService before answering |
| POST | `/api/watchlist/favorite/` | FavoriteToggleView | |
| **NEW** POST | `/api/watchlist/catchup-preference/` | CatchupPreferenceView | Sets per-show `ignore_catchup` ("Never for this show") |
| POST | `/api/watchlist/archive/` | ArchiveToggleView | |
| POST | `/api/episode/interaction/` | EpisodeInteractionView | Emotion + MVP vote |
| GET/PATCH | `/api/profile/` | ProfileView | PATCH body `{"profile_picture": "https://..."}` |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/profile/avatar-options/` | AvatarOptionsView | Real TMDB `/person/popular` headshots for the avatar picker's "Cast" tab (24h cache) |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** POST | `/api/profile/resync-stats/` | ProfileStatsResyncView | Recomputes `total_time_watched` from a true `Sum()` over WatchState/MovieWatchState (not the incrementally-maintained counter); returns verified shows/movies counts too. Backs the Profile Hub's tap-to-sync stat cards |
| GET/PATCH | `/api/notifications/preferences/` | NotificationPreferenceView | push_token + the 2 toggles now actually drive sends — see Phase 31 |
| GET | `/api/movies/watchlist/` | MovieWatchlistView | |
| POST | `/api/movies/watch-state/toggle/` | MovieWatchStateToggleView | |
| POST | `/api/movies/add/` | MovieAddView | |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/movies/<id>/detail/` | MovieDetailView | Full TMDB movie data |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/movies/<id>/credits/` | MovieCreditsView | Cast + crew (cached) |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/movies/<id>/watch-providers/` | MovieWatchProvidersView | Streaming providers |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/movies/<id>/recommendations/` | MovieRecommendationsView | Similar movies |
| GET | `/api/discover/feed/?type=tv\|movie` | DiscoverFeedView | Sectioned hero + lists (fixed sections, no genre/sort) |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/discover/filter/?type=tv\|movie&genre=<id>&sort=trending\|popular\|top_rated&page=<n>` | DiscoverFilterView | Real TMDB `/discover/{tv,movie}` genre+sort browsing; `trending` genre-filters actual trending results server-side (TMDB's `/trending` has no `with_genres`) |
| **NEW** GET | `/api/discover/genres/?type=tv\|movie` | DiscoverGenresView | Real TMDB cover image per genre for `GenreGrid.tsx` tiles (24h Django cache) |
| GET | `/api/search/shows/` | ShowSearchView | TV-only search |
| GET | `/api/search/universal/` | UniversalSearchView | **Upgraded:** relevancy engine + fallback |
| GET | `/api/shows/<id>/` | ShowDetailView | Cached TMDB show data |
| GET | `/api/shows/<id>/season/<n>/` | SeasonEpisodesView | |
| GET | `/api/episodes/<id>/` | EpisodeDetailView | |
| GET | `/api/episodes/<id>/credits/` | EpisodeCreditsView | |
| GET | `/api/shows/<id>/credits/` | ShowCreditsView | Aggregate across all seasons |
| GET | `/api/shows/<id>/watch-providers/` | WatchProvidersView | |
| GET | `/api/shows/<id>/recommendations/` | ShowRecommendationsView | |
| GET/POST | `/api/comments/` | CommentListCreateView | |
| **NEW (Phase 75.6)** GET | `/api/comments/feed/` | CommentFeedView | One paginated feed of recent comments across the viewer's tracked shows — replaced Discussions' old 8-parallel-request client-side fan-out |
| GET/POST | `/api/comments/<id>/replies/` | CommentReplyListCreateView | |
| GET/PATCH/DELETE | `/api/comments/<id>/` | CommentDetailView | |
| POST | `/api/comments/<id>/like/` | CommentLikeToggleView | |
| POST | `/api/comments/<id>/report/` | CommentReportView | |
| GET | `/api/moderation/reports/` | ModerationReportListView | |
| POST | `/api/moderation/reports/<id>/resolve/` | ModerationReportActionView | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/dashboard/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/statistics/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/genres/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/actors/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/providers/` | Stub (no per-user provider data yet) | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/completion/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/heatmap/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/streak/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/year-review/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/monthly-summary/` | | |
| GET | `/api/analytics/achievements/` | | |
| POST | `/api/import/tvtime/` | TVTimeImportView | Enqueues only — returns `202 {job_id, total, status}` |
| GET | `/api/import/status/<uuid:job_id>/` | ImportJobStatusView | Poll progress + final counts; scoped to the requesting user |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** GET/POST | `/api/lists/` | CustomListsView | User-created lists ("Movies2026", etc — distinct from Watchlist/MovieWatchlist). GET returns item_count + up to 4 cover posters per list in O(1) queries; POST quick-creates by name |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** GET/PATCH/DELETE | `/api/lists/<id>/` | CustomListDetailView | List metadata + full items (GET), rename/description/privacy (PATCH), delete (DELETE) — ownership via inline `get_object_or_404(..., user=request.user)`, this codebase's established convention |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** POST | `/api/lists/items/toggle/` | CustomListItemToggleView | Presence-based add/remove of a `{media_type, tmdb_id}` item to/from a list, same pattern as WatchStateToggleView |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** GET | `/api/lists/membership/?media_type=&tmdb_id=` | CustomListMembershipView | Which of the user's lists already contain this item — powers AddToListSheet's checkmarks |
| **NEW (Phase 71)** GET | `/api/recommendations/for-you/` | ForYouRecommendationsView | Cross-library personalized recs — seeds from the user's own top-watched shows/movies, merges TMDB's per-title `/recommendations` across seeds, ranks by cross-seed frequency. Excludes already-tracked titles. Cached 6h per user (no signal invalidation — staleness has no correctness cost here, unlike the watchlist cache) |
| **NEW (Phase L/52)** GET | `/api/reviews/shows/` / `/api/reviews/movies/` | ShowReviewListView / MovieReviewListView | Paginated list of the caller's own reviews |
| **NEW (Phase L/52)** GET/POST/DELETE | `/api/reviews/shows/<tmdb_id>/` / `/api/reviews/movies/<tmdb_id>/` | ShowReviewView / MovieReviewView | POST upserts (`update_or_create`); half-star `Decimal` rating (Phase 74). **Phase 75:** POST 403s unless ≥1 episode (or the movie) is marked watched — DELETE stays ungated |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** GET/POST | `/api/lists/` | CustomListsView | User-created lists ("Movies2026", etc — distinct from Watchlist/MovieWatchlist). GET returns item_count + up to 4 cover posters per list in O(1) queries; POST quick-creates by name |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** GET/PATCH/DELETE | `/api/lists/<id>/` | CustomListDetailView | List metadata + full items (GET), rename/description/privacy (PATCH), delete (DELETE) |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** POST | `/api/lists/items/toggle/` | CustomListItemToggleView | Presence-based add/remove of a `{media_type, tmdb_id}` item to/from a list |
| **NEW (Phase 70)** GET | `/api/lists/membership/?media_type=&tmdb_id=` | CustomListMembershipView | Which of the user's lists already contain this item |
| **NEW (Phase 74)** GET | `/api/users/search/` | UserSearchView | Ranked user search — `is_following` annotation, private accounts excluded |
| **NEW (Phase 74)** POST | `/api/users/<username>/follow/` | FollowToggleView | Single presence-toggle endpoint (no separate follow/unfollow); self-follow → 400; nonexistent-or-private target → 404 (deliberately indistinguishable). **Phase 75:** `is_self` added to the response edge shape, fixing a confirmed 400 when your own row rendered a tappable "Follow" button |
| **NEW (Phase 74)** GET | `/api/users/<username>/followers/` / `/api/users/<username>/following/` | FollowerListView / FollowingListView | |
| **NEW (Phase 74)** GET | `/api/users/<username>/` | UserProfileDetailView | Public profile blob, cached 300s; viewer-relative `is_self`/`is_following`/`follows_you` always computed fresh |
| **NEW (Phase 74)** GET | `/api/feed/activity/` | FriendsActivityView | 14-day read-time fan-in over followees' activity. **Phase 75:** rewritten from a `WatchState`/`MovieWatchState` union to real `ShowReview`/`MovieReview` rows (Activity now shows what someone thought, not just that they watched something); fixed a confirmed cache-key bug where every page returned page 1's payload for the full TTL |
| **NEW (Phase 75.7)** GET/DELETE | `/api/rewatch/shows/<tmdb_id>/` | ShowRewatchDetailView | GET the active round + its watched episode ids; DELETE cancels the round |
| **NEW (Phase 75.7)** POST | `/api/rewatch/shows/<tmdb_id>/start/` | ShowRewatchStartView | 400 unless every aired episode is already watched and no round is already active; `round_number = max(existing) + 1`, starting at 2 |
| **NEW (Phase 75.7)** POST | `/api/rewatch/episodes/<episode_tmdb_id>/toggle/` | RewatchEpisodeToggleView | Presence toggle within the active round; `±runtime_minutes` on both time-watched counters; stamps `completed_at` when the round covers every aired episode |
| **NEW (Phase 75.7)** POST/DELETE | `/api/rewatch/movies/<tmdb_id>/` | MovieRewatchCreateView | +1 row / removes the most recent row |

## Database Models (core/models.py)

| Model | Key Fields | Notes |
|-------|-----------|-------|
| `UserProfile` | user (1:1), profile_picture, total_time_watched, earned_badges[], **`is_private`** (NEW Phase 74), **`total_rewatch_time_watched`** (NEW Phase 75) | Auto-created by signal. `is_private`: "ghost mode" — excludes from search/activity/public profile. `total_rewatch_time_watched` is a subset already counted in `total_time_watched` |
| `SocialAccount` (NEW) | user (FK), provider (google/apple), provider_user_id, email, created_at | Links a User to a verified Google/Apple identity (Phase 27). Unique on (provider, provider_user_id) — the stable `sub` claim, never the email |
| `CachedShow` | tmdb_id (PK), title, poster_path, backdrop_path, status, vote_average, total_seasons, genres[], `next_episode_air_date`/`next_episode_season_number`/`next_episode_number`/`next_episode_name` (Phase 15), `original_language` (Phase 29), `airs_time`/`airs_timezone`/`airtime_checked_at` (Phase 73), **`tvmaze_id`/`next_episode_air_datetime`** (NEW Phase 75) | 12h staleness TTL. `airs_time`/`airs_timezone`: TVmaze-sourced broadcast slot (TMDB has no time-of-day). `tvmaze_id` caches the show-level lookup so the per-episode airstamp fetch doesn't repeat it |
| `CachedEpisode` | tmdb_id (PK), show (FK), season_number, episode_number, air_date, runtime_minutes, `notified_at` (Phase 73), **`air_datetime`** (NEW Phase 75) | `air_datetime`: exact UTC instant from TVmaze's per-episode `airstamp` — fixes missing release times on slot-less streaming shows |
| `Watchlist` | user, show, progress_percentage, watched_episode_count, **ignore_catchup** (NEW) | `ignore_catchup`: "Never for this show" Catch-Up modal preference |
| `WatchState` | user, episode, watched_at | Presence-based; toggle creates/deletes. `watched_at` is `default=timezone.now` (NOT `auto_now_add`) so a TV Time import can backfill the real historical date; serializer pins it read-only |
| `EpisodeInteraction` | user, episode, emotion_emoji, mvp_character_id, mvp_character_name | |
| `Comment` | user, show, episode, body, spoiler_tag, soft-deleted | |
| `CommentLike` | user, comment | |
| `CommentReport` | reporter, comment, reason, status | |
| `ShowReview` / `MovieReview` (NEW Phase L/52) | user, show/movie, `rating` (Decimal, half-star 0.5–5.0, `CheckConstraint`, Phase 74), note | Private-by-default. `UniqueConstraint(user, show)`/`(user, movie)`, POST upserts. **Phase 75:** POST gated on having watched (≥1 episode / the movie) |
| `Follow` (NEW Phase 74) | follower (FK), following (FK) | Presence-based follow edge (mirrors `CommentLike`/`WatchState`) — deliberately no `status`/request-approve flow. `UniqueConstraint(follower, following)` + a self-follow `CheckConstraint` |
| `WatchStreak` | user (1:1), current_streak, longest_streak, total_streak_days | |
| `NotificationPreference` | user (1:1), push_token, notify_new_episode, notify_weekly_digest | |
| `MovieCache` | tmdb_id (PK), title, poster_path, backdrop_path, release_date, runtime_minutes, genres_string, vote_average, **`original_language`** (NEW Phase 29) | `original_language`: ISO 639-1 from TMDB, blank on rows cached before Phase 29 until next refresh |
| `MovieWatchState` | user, movie, watched_at | Presence-based; `watched_at` is `default=timezone.now`, same reason as `WatchState` |
| `MovieWatchlist` | user, movie, added_at | |
| `ImportJob` (NEW) | user, status, payload, `payload_fingerprint` (sha256, Phase 72), total, processed, shows_imported/skipped, movies_imported/skipped, episodes_marked, errors, detail, finished_at | One TV Time import run. `payload` stages the normalised export (cleared on finish) — a full export is ~3MB, too big for a Celery arg. Polled by `ImportJobStatusView` |
| `CustomList` (NEW Phase 70) | user, name, description, is_private, created_at, updated_at | User-created list, e.g. "Movies2026" — distinct from Watchlist/MovieWatchlist's built-in "want to watch" trackers |
| `CustomListItem` (NEW Phase 70) | list (FK), media_type (tv/movie), tmdb_id, added_at | Stores tmdb_id directly, not a DB FK to CachedShow/MovieCache — no contenttypes/GenericForeignKey precedent in this codebase; safe because those cache rows are only ever `update_or_create`'d, never deleted, and the only entry point (a detail screen) already forces the row to exist. `unique_together(list, media_type, tmdb_id)` |
| `ShowRewatch` (NEW Phase 75.7) | user, show, round_number, started_at, completed_at (nullable) | A rewatch "round," parallel to `WatchState` by design (not a change to its `UNIQUE(user, episode)`, which is load-bearing across badges/analytics/the "already watched" check). `round_number` starts at 2 (round 1 = the original watch-through). `UniqueConstraint(user, show, round_number)` |
| `RewatchEpisodeState` (NEW Phase 75.7) | rewatch (FK), episode, watched_at | Presence-based per-episode tick scoped to one round. `UniqueConstraint(rewatch, episode)` |
| `MovieRewatch` (NEW Phase 75.7) | user, movie, watched_at | Append-only (no unique constraint) — a rewatch is a real repeatable event, undone by deleting the most recent row |

---

## TMDBService Methods (backend/core/services.py)

All methods are **cache-first**. The service uses `_request()` which supports Django cache (`use_cache=True`) or model-level caching.

| Method | TMDB Endpoint | Cache Strategy |
|--------|--------------|----------------|
| `get_show_details(tmdb_id)` | `/tv/{id}?append_to_response=aggregate_credits,watch/providers` | DB model (12h) + credits/providers in Django cache. **CHANGED (Phase 15):** now also extracts `next_episode_to_air` into `CachedShow.next_episode_*` |
| `get_movie_details(tmdb_id)` | `/movie/{id}?append_to_response=credits,watch/providers` | DB model (12h) + credits/providers in Django cache |
| `get_movie_credits(tmdb_id)` | `/movie/{id}/credits` | Django cache key `tmdb_movie_credits_{id}` (12h) |
| `get_movie_watch_providers(tmdb_id)` | `/movie/{id}/watch/providers` | Django cache key `tmdb_movie_providers_{id}` (12h) |
| `get_movie_recommendations(tmdb_id)` | `/movie/{id}/recommendations` | `use_cache=True` (1h). **CHANGED (Phase 22):** now also extracts `backdrop_path`/`overview` (TMDB always includes them, previously dropped) so movie/[id].tsx's "More Like This" cards can pass a complete optimistic-routing param set |
| `get_show_credits(tmdb_id)` | Uses cached `tmdb_show_credits_{id}` or `/tv/{id}/aggregate_credits` | Django cache (12h) |
| `get_watch_providers(tmdb_id)` | Uses cached `tmdb_show_providers_{id}` | Django cache (12h) |
| `search_shows(query)` | `/search/tv` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `search_multi(query)` | `/search/multi` | `use_cache=True` (1h) — returns `popularity` field |
| `get_season_episodes(tmdb_id, season)` | `/tv/{id}/season/{n}` | DB model (12h) |
| `get_trending(media_type, time_window)` | `/trending/{type}/{window}` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `get_popular_shows/movies()` | `/tv/popular` / `/movie/popular` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `get_anticipated_movies()` | `/movie/upcoming` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `get_trending_shows()` | `/trending/tv/week` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `get_airing_today_shows()` | `/tv/airing_today` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `get_top_rated_movies()` | `/movie/top_rated` | `use_cache=True` (1h) |
| `get_recommendations(tmdb_show_id)` | `/tv/{id}/recommendations` | `use_cache=True` (1h). **CHANGED (Phase 23):** now also extracts `backdrop_path`/`overview` (TMDB always includes them, previously dropped) so show/[id].tsx's recommendation cards can pass a complete optimistic-routing param set |
| **NEW** `get_popular_characters(limit)` | `/tv/{id}/aggregate_credits`, `/movie/{id}/credits` (via `get_show_credits()`/`get_movie_credits()`) across trending TV + popular movies | No `/character/popular` endpoint exists on TMDB — builds a "character" pool from top-billed cast of currently trending titles, keyed by `character` name instead of actor name. **CHANGED (Phase 16):** replaces the earlier `get_popular_people()` (`/person/popular`, real-celebrity pool, not character-specific) |
| **NEW** `discover_tv(genre_id, sort_by, page)` | `/discover/tv?with_genres=&sort_by=` | `use_cache=True` (1h); `vote_count.gte=100` guard when sorting by rating |
| **NEW** `discover_movies(genre_id, sort_by, page)` | `/discover/movie?with_genres=&sort_by=` | Same as above, movie counterpart |
| **CHANGED** `get_trending(..., include_genre_ids=False)` | `/trending/{type}/{window}` | New optional param retains each item's `genre_ids` (TMDB always returns it, previously discarded) + now surfaces `page`/`total_pages`/`total_results`; both additive, existing callers unaffected |
| `get_episode_credits(...)` | `/tv/{id}/season/{n}/episode/{e}/credits` | No cache (fresh per call) |
| `get_episode_full_credits(...)` | Same as above | No cache |
| `find_by_external_id(...)` | `/find/{external_id}` | No cache |

## Search Relevancy Engine (NEW — Phase 8)

Located in `backend/core/search_views.py`. Applied to every `GET /api/search/universal/` response.

**Scoring algorithm:**

```
Score = title_match (0–50) + log_popularity (0–15) + recency_bonus (0/10) + vote_quality (0–5)

title_match:
  +50  exact full-query match
  +30  all query tokens present in title
  +0–20 proportional partial token coverage

popularity:
  +min(15, log1p(tmdb_popularity) × 1.5)  ← caps outsized influence

recency:
  +10 if release_date within ±6 months of today (catches upcoming releases)

vote quality:
  +min(5, vote_average × 0.5)
```

**Punctuation-stripped fallback:** If first-pass query returns < 3 results, the engine strips punctuation (e.g. "Spider-Man: Brand New Day" → "Spider Man Brand New Day"), retries, and merges deduplicated results.

## Zustand Stores

### watchStore.ts (persisted)
- `watchlist[]` — all tracked shows
- `profile` — user profile + badges
- `unlockedBadges[]` — badge unlock queue for BadgeUnlockModal
- `history` — **NEW**, paginated watch history fed from `/api/watch-history/`
- `fetchHistory(page?)` — **NEW**, paginates watch history feed
- `heatmapData` — calendar heatmap
- `popUnlockedBadge()` — dequeues one badge for modal display
- `addShowToWatchlist(showId)` — **NEW**, calls `/watchlist/add/`, prepends the returned entry into `to_watch` on success. Returns `Promise<boolean>` so callers (show detail) know whether to route.
- `addMovieToWatchlist(movieId)` — **CHANGED**, now returns `Promise<boolean>` (was `void`) for the same route-on-success reason.
- `hasPreviousUnwatched`/`hasPreviousUnwatchedForSeason` — **REMOVED (Phase 17)**. Replaced by a server call (`POST /watch-state/catchup-check/`) made directly from `lib/useCatchupCascade.ts` — see the Phase 17 section below.
- `setCatchupPreference(showId, ignoreCatchup)` — **NEW**, calls `/watchlist/catchup-preference/`, optimistic across all 3 buckets.
- `preferredLayout`/`toggleLayout()` — **REMOVED/REPLACED (Phase 75.5)** — see `defaultLayout`/`layoutOverrides`/`useLayoutFor()` below.
- `defaultLayout: 'list' | 'grid'` — **NEW (Phase 75.5)**, persisted, Settings-owned. `layoutOverrides: Partial<Record<'shows'|'movies'|'myShows'|'myMovies'|'myAnime', 'list'|'grid'>>` — persisted, per-screen. `setDefaultLayout(mode)` sets the default **and clears every override**; `setLayoutForScope(scope, mode)` sets one screen's override. `useLayoutFor(scope)` selector returns `layoutOverrides[scope] ?? defaultLayout`. Persist `version` bumped with a `migrate` step seeding `defaultLayout` from any pre-existing `preferredLayout`.
- `updateProfilePicture(url)` — **NEW (Phase 14)** — optimistic PATCH `/profile/` with `{profile_picture: url}`; reverts `profile` on failure. Backs the new Profile avatar picker.
- `activeRewatch` on `WatchlistEntry` / `rewatch_count` on `MovieWatchlistItem` — **NEW (Phase 75.7)** — mirror the serializer additions of the same name.
- `showRewatchDetail` + `fetchShowRewatchDetail()`/`startShowRewatch()`/`cancelShowRewatch()`/`toggleRewatchEpisode()`/`addMovieRewatch()`/`removeMovieRewatch()` — **NEW (Phase 75.7)** — wrap the `/api/rewatch/*` endpoints; episode toggle mirrors `toggleWatchState`'s optimistic-then-reconcile pattern but writes `RewatchEpisodeState` instead of `WatchState` while a round is active.
- `analyticsDirtyAt` — **NEW (Phase 75.8)** — bumped in `fetchProfile()`; `analytics.tsx`/`profile.tsx` gate their focus-effect refetch batches on this instead of refetching unconditionally on every focus.
- `Episode.air_datetime` / `Show.next_episode_air_datetime` — **NEW (Phase 75.1)** — mirror the new `CachedEpisode`/`CachedShow` serializer fields, feeding `resolveAirInstant()`.

### discoverStore.ts (in-memory, NOT persisted)
- `activeSegment: 'tv' | 'movie'`
- `feedData: Record<'tv'|'movie', DiscoverFeedResponse | null>` — cached per segment
- `searchResults[]` / `isSearching`
- `fetchFeed(segment)` — calls `/api/discover/feed/?type={segment}`, skips if already cached
- `runSearch(query)` — calls `/api/search/universal/`
- `clearSearch()` — resets search state
- **NEW** `filteredResults[]` / `isLoadingFiltered` / `filteredError` — Filter & Sort sheet results, separate from `feedData` and `searchResults`
- **NEW** `fetchFilteredResults()` — calls `/api/discover/filter/` with current `activeSegment`/`selectedGenreId`/`sortOrder`
- **NEW** `isFilterActive()` — `selectedGenreId !== null || sortOrder !== 'trending'`
- **NEW** `resetFilters()` — clears genre/sort back to defaults + clears `filteredResults`
- `setSelectedGenreId`/`setSortOrder` now auto-fetch (or clear, if back to fully-default) — **fixed**, previously set state with no effect on any list
- **NEW** `genreCovers: Record<'tv'|'movie', Record<genreId, GenreCover>>` + `fetchGenreCovers(segment)` — real TMDB images for `GenreGrid.tsx`, cached per segment
- **NEW (Phase 71)** `forYou: ForYouItem[]` / `isLoadingForYou` / `forYouError` / `hasFetchedForYou` — cross-library recommendations, independent of `activeSegment` (mixed tv/movie results); `fetchForYou()` calls `/api/recommendations/for-you/` once per session (backend already caches 6h server-side). Rendered as a "For You" row on the Discover Hub, above the curated sections

### listsStore.ts (in-memory, NOT persisted) — NEW (Phase 70)
Mirrors `discoverStore.ts`'s "kept separate to avoid polluting the persisted slice" precedent — nothing here is worth surviving an app restart offline.
- `lists[]` / `isLoadingLists` — the user's custom lists (name, item_count, cover_posters, is_private)
- `activeListDetail` / `isLoadingListDetail` — one list's full item detail, backing `app/lists/[id].tsx`
- `membership: Record<"media_type:tmdb_id", listId[]>` — which lists contain the item currently open in `AddToListSheet`
- `fetchLists()`, `createList(name, description?, isPrivate?)`, `renameList()`, `deleteList()`, `fetchListDetail(listId)`, `fetchMembership(mediaType, tmdbId)`
- `toggleListItem(listId, mediaType, tmdbId)` — optimistic add/remove, same snapshot-then-rollback-on-error convention as `watchStore.ts`

Also new on `watchStore.ts` (Phase 70): `resyncStats()` / `isResyncingStats` — calls `POST /profile/resync-stats/`, updates `profile.total_time_watched` from the verified recomputed value. Backs the Profile Hub's tap-to-sync stat cards.

### socialStore.ts (in-memory, NOT persisted) — NEW (Phase 74)
Mirrors `discoverStore.ts`'s/`listsStore.ts`'s "nothing here is worth surviving an app restart offline" precedent.
- `searchResults[]` / `isSearching` — `GET /users/search/`
- `activityFeed[]` / `isLoadingActivity` / `hasMoreActivity` — paginated friends activity feed (`GET /feed/activity/`); **Phase 75:** cards are now review shapes (`{type:'review', media_type, tmdb_id, title, poster_path, rating, note, user, updated_at}`), not watch-history shapes
- `viewedProfile` / `isLoadingProfile` — a public profile blob + its followers/following lists, backing `user/[username].tsx`/`connections.tsx`
- `toggleFollow(username)` — presence toggle against `POST /users/<username>/follow/`, optimistic
- `PublicUser`/`FollowEdge` types gained `is_self?: boolean` (Phase 75) — fixes a confirmed bug where your own row in a followers/following list rendered a tappable "Follow" button that 400s
